# TAAI website — project context

GATE DA 2027 exam-prep marketing site for TAAI (live at taai.live; LMS is a separate app at learn.taai.live, not in this repo).

## Tech stack

- Plain HTML + inline `<style>` + JSX, no bundler. Each of the 6 main pages is fully self-contained: nav, footer, and all components are copy-pasted JSX inside that page's own `<script type="text/babel">` block.
- `build.mjs` precompiles the JSX (via `@babel/core` + `@babel/preset-react`) into plain JS and writes the result to `dist/`, which is what Netlify deploys (`netlify.toml`: `publish = "dist"`).
- Local dev serves the **source root directly**, not `dist/` — source pages still have Babel Standalone (`<script type="text/babel">`) and load React/ReactDOM from a CDN, so they transpile live in-browser. This means editing a page and refreshing works with no build step, but it also means anything that only exists in `dist/` (generated output) is invisible to the dev server unless it's also written into source.

## Commands

- `npm run build` — compiles pages + regenerates the blog + sitemap into source, then copies everything to `dist/`.
- `npm run watch` — same, plus rebuilds a page's compiled output when its source file changes.
- Local preview: a static server pointed at the **project root** (not `dist/`), typically on port 8080.

## Pages

`index.html`, `gate-da-courses.html`, `gate-da-test-series.html`, `gate-da-toppers.html`, `contact.html`, `gate-da-free-notes.html`, `gate-da-progress-tracker.html` — each listed in `PAGES` in `build.mjs`. Clean URLs (`/gate-da-courses`, etc.) are handled by redirects in `netlify.toml`, with 301s from the old short names (`/courses`, `/test-series`, `/testimonials`, `/resources`) and from `.html` extensions so old links don't break — `gate-da-progress-tracker.html` is the exception: it was renamed from `progress.html`/`/progress` on 2026-07-25 with no redirect from the old slug at all, by request. `gate-da-free-notes.html` fetches notes/lecture metadata at runtime from published Google Sheet CSVs (`sheets/` holds local snapshot templates, gitignored). `gate-da-progress-tracker.html`'s main content (not its nav, which matches the other pages) is the one exception to the "JSX per page" rule below — see the Progress tracker section.

## Blog (added 2026-06-27)

Static, no backend/database. Source of truth is `content/blog/*.md` (frontmatter + Markdown).

- `build.mjs`'s `generateBlog()` reads those files and writes plain static HTML (no JSX/Babel involved) into the **source** `blogs/` folder: `blogs/index.html` (listing) and `blogs/<slug>/index.html` (each post) — written to source rather than `dist/` specifically so the source-serving dev server can preview it like every other page. The generic asset-copy step in `build()` then carries `blogs/` into `dist/` for the real deploy.
- These generated files (`blogs/index.html`, `blogs/*/`) and the root `sitemap.xml` are gitignored — they're build artifacts regenerated from `content/blog/`, same logic as `dist/` itself not being committed.
- Visual design is shared via `blogs/blog.css` (extracted design tokens/nav/footer from the main pages, plus blog-specific styles matching the site's offer-card visual language — gradient hero blobs, gradient `<em>` emphasis, top-strip+icon-badge cards) and `blogs/blog.js` (vanilla JS: mobile hamburger menu, scroll progress, back-to-top, fade-in reveal — a deliberately simplified stand-in for the React pages' nav, since duplicating the full stateful Nav component's hover-pill/signin-cycle animations wasn't worth it for content pages).
- `sitemap.xml` is generated dynamically (was a hand-maintained static file before) and includes every post automatically.
- `/admin` is Decap CMS (`admin/index.html` + `admin/config.yml`), backend `git-gateway` — a non-technical writer logs in (Netlify Identity, optionally via Google as an external provider) and publishing commits a Markdown file straight to `content/blog/`, which triggers a normal Netlify rebuild.
- **"Blocks" — a fill-in-the-blanks system so a non-technical writer can build a data-rich post (charts, tables, FAQ, diagrams) without touching HTML/CSS** (added 2026-09-04, after an initial hand-coded version of `gate-da-syllabus-2027` — its own custom fonts/colors/SVG charts, all raw HTML in the Body field — made clear that level of design isn't something the plain Markdown editor can produce, nor something to expect from a non-technical writer). **`gate-da-syllabus-2027` itself was then rebuilt on Blocks once the system existed** (same day) — explicitly, since keeping the one showcase post as a hand-coded exception would defeat the point; it now uses the standard site design (`--purple`/`--pink`/etc.) like every other Blocks post, not its original bespoke look, and is safe to open and edit through `/admin` normally, unlike a raw-HTML post would be. In `/admin`, a post's optional **Blocks** field (`admin/config.yml`, a Decap "variable types" list) lets the writer add, in any order, any number of: Section heading, Paragraph text, Bar chart, Grouped bar chart, Line chart, Data table, Step diagram (things accumulating over steps), Two-path diagram (one thing splits into two outcomes), Comparison rows, Two-column comparison, Syllabus/topic grid, FAQ, or Resource links — each just plain labels/numbers/text fields, rendered into the actual chart/table/diagram by `renderBlocks()`/`BLOCK_RENDERERS` in `build.mjs`, styled by the `.block-*` rules appended to `blogs/blog.css` (using the site's own existing `--purple`/`--pink`/`--blue`/etc. tokens, plus two new ones — `--rise`/`--fall` — added specifically for chart trend coloring). Rendered after the regular Body field, in the order the writer added them. Bar/line/grouped-bar charts size themselves as a % of the block's own data (its min/max), not fixed pixel coordinates — required since block data is arbitrary and unknown ahead of time, unlike a hand-tuned one-off chart. **Scope was a deliberate, explicit call**: templatize everything from the source article, including the illustrative diagrams (step/two-path/comparison), not just the obviously-reusable data charts — the "Two-column comparison" block is a knowingly simplified stand-in for the one figure that didn't reduce to structured data at all (a hand-drawn "scattered dots vs. one connected line" illustration) — that one visual metaphor specifically isn't reproduced, everything else is.
  - **`blocks_json` — a paste-from-LLM path added 2026-09-04, once it was clear the manual Blocks list itself (click "Add" per block, then hand-type into every field) was still too much friction for a non-technical writer, who realistically drafts with an LLM anyway.** A second, optional frontmatter field (`admin/config.yml`, a Decap `code` widget with `output_code_only: true` so it stores as a plain string) holds one pasted JSON array describing all of a post's blocks in one shot; `loadPosts()` in `build.mjs` uses it in place of the manual `blocks` list whenever it's non-empty (checked first, falls back to `blocks` only if empty) — same field names/shapes as the manual list, just JSON instead of clicking through the UI. Malformed JSON here throws and fails the whole build, naming the offending post file — deliberate, since this runs at build time (not in front of a live user), so failing loudly here beats silently shipping a post with blocks missing. `admin/index.html`'s `LLM_PROMPT_TEXT` is the actual prompt a writer copies into ChatGPT/Claude alongside their source article — transcribed by hand from the same 13 renderer functions' field contracts, shown in a copy-ready textarea above the existing Blocks reference cards in the preview pane. Verified end-to-end with a throwaway test post (`blocks_json` covering 5 block types, screenshotted rendering correctly, then a deliberately-broken JSON string confirmed the build fails with a clear file-named error) before being deleted, never committed. **Keep `LLM_PROMPT_TEXT` in sync by hand** whenever a block type's fields change in `build.mjs`/`admin/config.yml` — same maintenance burden `BLOCKS_REFERENCE_HTML` already has, now doubled.
  - **Nested Blocks data forced a real YAML parser** — `parseFrontmatter()` used to be a hand-rolled regex parser that only understood a flat list of scalar strings under one key (fine for the original `tags` field); it would have silently mis-parsed or dropped Blocks' nested lists-of-objects-containing-their-own-nested-lists. Replaced with `js-yaml` (pinned to **`4.x`**, not the newer `5.x` — that major version's API is a ground-up rewrite with no default export and a very different, lower-level surface not needed here; confirmed by hitting `SyntaxError: ... does not provide an export named 'default'` on first install). A field from a Decap list using a single `field:` (not `fields:`) serializes as a plain array of values — e.g. `series`, `x_labels`, `columns`, any `values` list; mixing this up with an object-array field (`fields:`, plural — e.g. `bars`, `steps[].items`) silently reads `undefined` off a plain string, so `build.mjs`'s block renderers deliberately handle the two differently per field, not uniformly.
- **`/gate-da-subjects.js` (project root) is the single source of truth for the GATE DA subject list/order** (added 2026-09-04, after 3 separate hand-maintained copies — the tracker, `/team`, and the blog's tags — had already drifted out of sync once) — defines `window.GATE_DA_SUBJECTS`, a plain array of 10 names with no emoji/styling (must match `schedule_tasks.subject` exactly, since `progress.js` matches real schedule data by this string). Loaded via a plain `<script src="/gate-da-subjects.js">` tag placed *before* the consuming page's own script (see `gate-da-progress-tracker.html` and `team/index.html`) — this fits the site's existing no-bundler, plain-`<script>`-tag architecture, and the generic asset-copy step in `build.mjs` already carries any new root-level file into `dist/` with no build-script changes needed. `progress.js`'s `CANONICAL_SUBJECTS` and `team/team.js`'s `SUBJECT_OPTIONS` both read from `window.GATE_DA_SUBJECTS` now (each still falls back to its own hardcoded copy of the same list if that global is somehow missing, purely as a defensive backstop, not a second source of truth to maintain) — `team.js` additionally pairs each name with its own emoji (`SUBJECT_EMOJI`, matched by array position), since that's presentation specific to the Discord post, not part of a subject's canonical identity. **The one place this can't reach**: `admin/config.yml` is YAML consumed by Decap CMS, not JS — it can't load this file directly, so its blog tag list (see below) stays a manually-synced copy with a comment pointing back here. If the subject list ever changes, update `/gate-da-subjects.js` first, then `admin/config.yml`'s tags by hand to match.
- **Blog tag list matches the tracker's subject list exactly** — `admin/config.yml`'s `tags` options used to have one combined "Python & DSA" tag; split 2026-09-04 into separate Python/Data Structures/Algorithms tags specifically to match the centralized subject list above, rather than three different subject breakdowns existing across the site. No existing posts referenced the old `python-dsa` tag at the time (checked `content/blog/` directly), so this was a clean rename, not a migration.
- **`gate-da-syllabus-2027` gets a deliberate internal link from `index.html`'s and `gate-da-courses.html`'s hero sections** (`.hero-guide-link`, added 2026-09-06) — a small "📘 New: Free GATE DA Syllabus 2027 guide" line under each page's primary CTA buttons, added specifically for SEO: before this, the post was only reachable via `/blogs`, meaning zero internal link equity flowed to it from the site's two highest-authority pages. Deliberately unobtrusive (small text link, not styled as a competing CTA) and placed after the real conversion buttons, not before. Don't remove this without adding an equivalent link elsewhere — the point is to keep at least one high-authority internal link pointing at this post. `lastmod` in the generated sitemap is intentionally NOT bumped just because of this — it should only change when the post's own content changes, not when other pages link to it.
- **Known gotcha**: Decap CMS resolves `config.yml` as a relative URL. Visiting `/admin` without a trailing slash makes the browser resolve it against `/` instead of `/admin/`, 404ing regardless of browser — fixed via `<base href="/admin/">` in `admin/index.html`. Don't remove that tag.
- Netlify Identity + Git Gateway + the Google login provider are dashboard-only settings, not something committable — and Identity login only works on a real deployed domain, never on `localhost`.

## Progress tracker (`/gate-da-progress-tracker`, added 2026-07-21; slug renamed from `/progress` 2026-07-25 with no redirect from the old path)

A day-by-day study schedule tracker at `taai.live/gate-da-progress-tracker`, open to any student (no LMS/enrollment check — this is intentionally separate from `learn.taai.live`). First real backend/database this repo has ever had; everything else in this site is static.

- **Frontend**: `gate-da-progress-tracker.html` + `progress.js`. The nav is React/JSX ported verbatim from the other pages (added 2026-07-24, scoped light-theme CSS vars on `#nav-root` so it doesn't leak into this page's dark theme) — but the tracker itself (calendar, streak, pomodoro, etc.) is vanilla JS, no React/Babel. `progress.js` renders that part by building HTML strings and setting `innerHTML`, not a framework.
- **Backend**: Supabase (Postgres) accessed only through Netlify Functions in `netlify/functions/` — the frontend never talks to Supabase directly. `lib/supabase.js` and `lib/csv.js` hold shared helpers. Functions: `register`, `complete-task`, `streak`, `subject-progress`, `pomo-settings`, `pomodoro-complete`, `pomodoro-leaderboard`, `rename`, and `tracker-data` (a single consolidated endpoint for the page-load batch — see the gotcha below; it replaced 4 separate one-off functions that used to exist here: `schedule`, `progress`, `last-week-focus-leaders`, `pomo-sessions`).
- **Student identity**: cookie only (`taai_user`, base64-encoded JSON, 365-day expiry), no passwords/auth. `register.js` looks up-or-creates by email.
- **Schedule data flow — deliberately manual, no automatic external sync of any kind.** The user hands over each month's schedule directly (as a PDF or similar); convert it to `sheets/schedule.csv` (`Date | <Subject columns...>`, dates as `DD/MM/YYYY`, one cell = one task) and run `npm run load-schedule` (`scripts/load-schedule.mjs`) to load it into Supabase — validate through the real `parseCsv`/`toIsoDate` in `netlify/functions/lib/csv.js` first, don't hand-write SQL. There used to be a `sync-schedule.js` cron function pulling from a published Google Sheet; removed at the user's explicit request ("I will provide you the schedules, don't read from anywhere else") — don't reintroduce an automatic fetch from Sheets or any other external source without being asked.
- **Streak** (`streak.js`) and **subject-progress** (`subject-progress.js`) are both computed from the *entire* schedule history server-side, not from whatever month the client currently has loaded — a month-scoped version would make progress look like it resets every time the student navigates months, since e.g. a subject can span many months and a streak must span backward past the currently-viewed month.
- **"Progress by subject" always shows the full GATE DA syllabus**, not just subjects that happen to be scheduled yet — `CANONICAL_SUBJECTS` in `progress.js` is a hardcoded display-name list merged with whatever `subject-progress.js` actually returns; unscheduled ones show at 0%. Names are matched by exact string, so if a future month's sheet spells a subject differently than `CANONICAL_SUBJECTS` (or differently than an already-synced month did), it'll show up as an extra row instead of merging — keep this list's spelling in sync with whatever's actually typed into schedule PDFs/CSVs.
- **The "live now" pulsing dot exists on all three leaderboards, not just the weekly top-20** (extended to the daily top-10 "Today" card 2026-09-04, and to the last-week champions card outside Focus Mode 2026-09-07) — `fetchLiveStatusByEmail()` in `lib/supabase.js` is the one shared definition of "live" (running `pomo_active_session` row whose `phase_end_at` hasn't passed yet — see its own comment for why `running` alone isn't sufficient), used by `pomodoro-leaderboard.js`'s weekly board, `fetchTodayLeaders()`'s daily leaders, and now `fetchLastWeekLeaders()` too, so none of the three can disagree on what counts as live. `progress.js`'s `liveDotHtml()` (previously only called from the weekly `renderLeaderboardRows`) is now also called from `renderTodayLeaderboardRows`/the daily viewer-rank row, and `renderLastWeekChampions()`/its own viewer-rank row. `fetchLastWeekLeaders` fetches live status unconditionally regardless of caller (same tradeoff already accepted for `fetchTodayLeaders`, which is also called from the Discord daily post) — its OTHER caller, `discord-dispatch.js`'s `weekly_leaderboard` source, just doesn't render the extra fields, harmless unused data rather than a real cost. **A real, easy-to-miss regression this surfaced and fixed in the same change**: the "dim non-live rows so an active student stands out" CSS rule was deliberately scoped to exclude `#champions-card`, with an explicit comment explaining why — `fetchLastWeekLeaders` never returned `is_live` at the time, so including it in that rule would have permanently dimmed every row on that card (`:not(.leaderboard-row--live)` matching unconditionally when no row could ever carry that class). Once `is_live` started flowing through, that premise flipped, and the rule needed to be *actively extended* to `#champions-card`, not just left alone — caught by re-reading the CSS's own comment rather than assuming the styling would "just work" once the data was there.
  - **A "sonar ping" ripple version of the dot was tried, verified working (scale/opacity sweep confirmed numerically, no clipping despite `overflow: hidden`), shipped, then rejected on sight and reverted the same day** ("can the live pulsing dots be better?" → shipped the ripple → "no i don't like it revert to previous"). All of it — `.live-dot-ripple`, its `@keyframes live-ripple`, `.live-dot-slot`'s `text-align: center` — reverted back to the original plain `live-pulse` scale+opacity breathing animation, byte-for-byte. `liveDotHtml()` in `progress.js` no longer renders the ripple span. A reminder that passing every technical check (no clipping, real animation, correct sizing) doesn't guarantee a design change actually lands well subjectively — worth a quick "does this look right to you" check before investing further polish next time a request is this open-ended, rather than shipping straight from "it works."
- **The last-week champions card gained its own 60s poll (2026-09-07), following up on the live-dot addition above** — that dot's data (`fetchLastWeekLeaders`) was only ever fetched once, at page load, since the checklist page (where this card lives, outside Focus Mode) had no polling loop at all; the dot was really "who was live when the page loaded," never actually live while someone sat on the page. `netlify/functions/last-week-leaders.js` is a new, deliberately standalone endpoint wrapping the already-shared `fetchLastWeekLeaders` — same "small independently-pollable endpoint" reasoning already established for `streak.js`/`subject-progress.js`/`pomo-settings.js`, rather than folding this into `tracker-data.js`'s batch (which only serves the one-time page-load fetch). `renderLastWeekChampions()` was split into a rows-only `renderLastWeekChampionRows()` wrapped in a new `#champions-rows` div, exactly mirroring how `renderLeaderboardCard`/`renderLeaderboardRows` are already split — so `refreshLastWeekChampions()` (new, mirrors `refreshLeaderboard()`) can patch just the rows on each poll tick without regenerating `.streak-champions-card`'s `innerHTML` and replaying its `.fade-in` entrance. `LAST_WEEK_POLL_MS`/`startLastWeekPoll()`/`stopLastWeekPoll()` (new) are the mirror-image of the existing `LEADERBOARD_POLL_MS` machinery, but active in the opposite context — gated on `!state.focus` (as well as the usual `document.hidden`) since the champions card only ever renders outside Focus Mode, so this poll and the Focus Mode leaderboard poll are never both running at once: `enterFocus()` calls `stopLastWeekPoll()`, the `popstate` handler's exit-Focus-Mode branch calls `startLastWeekPoll()`, `init()` calls it unconditionally at the end (self-gating handles whether it should actually start), and the `visibilitychange` listener grew an `else` branch mirroring the existing Focus Mode one for this context. The initial-render `armConfetti('champions-card', ...)` call (in `renderCalendar`) was already deliberately narrower than the one on the Focus Mode leaderboard — only an actual top-5 finish, not just any placement at all, per its own existing comment — so the new poll's arming call was written to match that same narrower condition, not copied from `refreshLeaderboard`'s broader one. Verified end-to-end: the new endpoint hit directly against real production Supabase (returned correct `leaders`/`viewerRank` shape, live-status fields included); a fresh headless-Chrome load of the real page confirmed `#champions-rows` renders inside `#champions-card` on the checklist; a 65-second real-time headless run confirmed exactly one request to `/api/last-week-leaders` fires at the ~60s mark with zero console errors, i.e. the poll genuinely runs client-side, not just "the code looks right."
- **The page background was pared back, then re-upped, both on 2026-09-08** — two rounds of direct feedback in quick succession. Original: a repeating dot-grid (`body`'s `background-image`) plus 3 saturated, fast-bouncing colored orbs (`.orb-1`/`.orb-2`/`.orb-3`, 22-32s cycles, opacity up to 0.14) — "too vibe coded," the exact "AI-template" look. First pass overcorrected: dot-grid removed, down to just `.orb-1` at 0.06 opacity on a slow 52s/~50px drift — then called "too boring." **Landed two rounds later on a middle point**: dot-grid stays gone (that was the actual "AI-template" tell, never asked to come back), but a second orb was restored — `.orb-1` (purple, top-right) and `.orb-2` (blue, bottom-left), dropping only the smallest/pink `.orb-3` — with opacity brought back close to the original (0.13/0.10 vs. the original 0.14/0.11) and motion at a calmer-than-original-but-clearly-alive pace (34s/40s cycles, ~80px drift, vs. the original's 26s/32s and ~140px, and well past the interim version's near-static 52s/~50px). Diagonal top-right + bottom-left placement gives the page real depth at a glance rather than being flat until an orb happens to drift past. This only touches the shared page-level backdrop (`body`/`.bg-orbs`, used across the whole tracker) — at the time this shipped it was what showed through every card's frosted-glass background (`.pomodoro-card`/`.focus-card`/`.streak-champions-card`/`.leaderboard-card` all used `rgba(255,255,255,0.05)` + backdrop-blur, so they showed whatever was behind them), which is why this affected the Pomodoro card's background too even though `.pomodoro-card` itself had no decorative pseudo-elements of its own — **that glass-card treatment and the per-card corner blobs mentioned below were both removed in the very next round, see the next bullet.** First pass verified visually via headless Chrome screenshots at desktop, Focus Mode (as a real disposable test student — registered, screenshotted, then every row for that email deleted across `task_progress`/`pomo_daily_sessions`/`pomo_active_session`/`pomodoro_stats`/`students` immediately after, same discipline as every other live-data test this session), and mobile (390px); the re-up was verified visually at desktop width before shipping. **Same day, one more round**: `.orb-1`'s color was swapped from purple to pink ("let's not do purple anymore") — purple is the dominant accent everywhere else on the page (nav, buttons, most gradient text, and — at the time — the streak/today card corner blobs), so the ambient glow now picks up the other end of the site's own pink→purple→blue brand gradient instead of doubling down on purple; `.orb-2` stayed blue. Verified with a clipped headless-Chrome screenshot of just the top-right corner (`Page.captureScreenshot`'s `clip` option) rather than a full-page shot, since the orb is subtle enough that a full-page render didn't clearly show the color at a glance.
- **The card system was converted from glassmorphic to flat/minimalist the same day** ("change the design to minimalist than glassmorphic" — direct feedback, following the background rounds above). Every tracker-page card (`.reg-card`, `.streak-champions-card`, `.streak-hero`, `.focus-card`, `.subject-breakdown`, `.heatmap-card`, `.day`, `.pomodoro-card`, `.pomo-settings-toggle`, `.leaderboard-card`, `.today-card`) had its translucent `rgba(255,255,255,0.0X)` background + `backdrop-filter: blur(...) saturate(180%)` + multi-layer glow `box-shadow` (drop shadow + inset highlight) replaced with a flat `background: var(--card)` (`#1e1e24`) + a plain `border: 1px solid var(--line)`, no shadow and no blur at all — the same flat idiom `.month-nav button` already used elsewhere on this page, so this brings the rest of the page in line with a pattern that already existed rather than inventing a new one. `.today-card` kept its distinguishing `2px solid var(--purple)` border (that's a functional "this is today" marker, not part of the glass treatment). The decorative blurred corner blobs on `.streak-hero` (`::before`/`::after`, amber + purple) and `.today-card` (`::before`, purple) were removed outright too — they're pure glow decoration in the same visual language as the blur-glass cards, so leaving them would have undercut the minimalist direction even with the cards themselves flattened; these were the same blobs an earlier round of feedback in this same conversation had deliberately left alone when the ask was narrower (just "the background"), now correctly in scope once the ask became "the design" as a whole. Explicitly NOT touched: the shared nav component's own glass panels (`.nav-pill`, `.nav-dropdown`, `.mobile-menu`, `.nav-logo::after`) — that's the same React/JSX component ported verbatim across all 6 main site pages (see "Tech stack" above), so changing its look here would desync it from every other page rather than being this page's own design choice; and the brand gradient elements (streak/day-count numbers, Pomodoro ring + time text, primary buttons) — those are brand identity, not glassmorphism, and weren't part of the ask. `.pomodoro-card`'s `transition: box-shadow .4s ease` was dropped as dead code in the same pass (confirmed via grep of both this file and `progress.js` that nothing — no CSS state, no JS — ever actually changed that card's box-shadow). Verified visually via headless Chrome at desktop (checklist + the `#champions-card`/`#leaderboard-card`/heatmap/subject-breakdown all flat now), Focus Mode (as a real disposable test student, same register-screenshot-delete-every-row discipline as above), and mobile (390px) — flat cards read cleanly at all three, no layout regression from dropping the shadows/blur.
- **The exam-countdown chip (top of the Pomodoro card in Focus Mode — "151 days till GATE") was redesigned off a pasted screenshot** ("make this better looking and not in the form of a pill" — direct feedback with an attached image, 2026-09-08). Was a `border-radius: 999px` pill with a bare inline SVG icon and a plain purple-tinted translucent background. Now a small `border-radius: 12px` rect chip using the same `var(--card)`/`var(--line)` flat idiom the card-system pass above just established, with the calendar icon moved into its own `.exam-countdown-icon` badge (28px rounded square, `rgba(139,92,246,0.14)` fill) instead of sitting bare inline, and the day count itself bolded up via a new `.exam-countdown-num` span (`font-weight: 800`, `15px`, full `--ink` instead of the surrounding `--ink-dim`) so it reads as a small stat card rather than a status pill. `progress.js`'s `buildStatsPanelHtml()` markup was updated to match (wraps the icon in `.exam-countdown-icon`, wraps the number in `.exam-countdown-num`) — `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped since this changes what a live tab actually renders. Verified with a real disposable test student in Focus Mode (registered, screenshotted at both a tight zoomed crop via `Page.captureScreenshot`'s `clip` option and full-viewport, then every row deleted immediately after) and at mobile width (390px) — reads as a distinct small stat chip at both sizes, not a pill.
- **Same day, immediate follow-up: the exam-countdown number got bigger + a count-up animation on open, with spacing reserved for its real 3-digit max** ("make the number bigger and have a count up animation... the max can be 365 days that is 3 digits, manage the spacing according to it"). `.exam-countdown-num` grew 15px → 28px bold; `progress.js` gained `animateExamCountdown()`, a plain `requestAnimationFrame` count-up (900ms, ease-out cubic) from 0 to the real day count, reading the target off a `data-days` attribute the server-rendered markup now carries (initial text content is literally `0`, so there's nothing to flash-then-reset — the animation is the only thing that ever writes the real number in). Skips straight to the final value under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` rather than animating anyway. **The 3-digit spacing ask is handled by CSS alone, not JS**: `.exam-countdown-num` has `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums` plus `min-width: 3ch; text-align: right` — sized for the real worst case (GATE is at most ~365 days out) — so "days till GATE" never shifts sideways as the animation runs through 1-digit, 2-digit, then 3-digit values. Hooked into the one place that already needed it: `animateExamCountdown()` is called at the tail of `renderCalendar()` (alongside `bindCalendarEvents()`/`observeFadeIns()`), which — since `buildStatsPanelHtml()` only runs inside `renderCalendar()`'s `state.focus` branch — means this fires exactly on "Focus Mode was just (re-)entered," matching "whenever someone opens the page." `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again. Verified live against a real disposable test student: sampled the DOM mid-animation (~300ms into the 900ms run) and confirmed a real in-between value (113, correctly between 0 and the true 151), i.e. the count-up is a real running animation, not a static number that merely looks animated in a screenshot. **One verification gap from this pass, caught and closed in the very next one below**: the "no horizontal shift" screenshot check here compared a mid-run frame against the final frame, but both happened to already be 3-digit numbers (113 and 151) — meaning it could never have caught a 1-or-2-digit-vs-3-digit shift even if one existed, the actual case the spacing ask was about.
- **Two more rounds, same day, right after**: "the proportions of text and numbers are not correct," then "make the animation more smooth and appealing." **Proportions**: the first pass's 28px number against the inherited 12.5px caption (~2.2x) was too dramatic for text meant to read inline as one phrase ("151 days till GATE") — unlike `.streak-number`'s much bigger ratio against `.streak-label`, which gets away with a huge jump specifically because that pairing is stacked (hero number, caption below), not read as a continuous sentence. Rebalanced to 23px number / 14px caption (~1.6x) — number still clearly bigger/bolder/brighter than the original 15px, but close enough to the caption that the pair still reads as one phrase. **This also caught a real bug while investigating**: `.exam-countdown-num`'s `min-width: 3ch` was silently not applying at all — a plain inline `<span>` ignores `min-width`/`text-align` entirely per the CSS spec (only block/inline-block/etc. respect them), and the element had no explicit `display` override, so it was rendering as a bare inline span the whole time. Added `display: inline-block`. Verified two ways after the fix: (1) an isolated detached-element test (a throwaway `<span class="exam-countdown-num">` appended to `document.body`, cycled through `'9'`/`'99'`/`'151'`) confirmed all three now measure the exact same `44.984375px` width — the reservation genuinely holds across digit counts, not just coincidentally between two same-digit-count samples like the previous pass's check; (2) instrumented the real page with a `requestAnimationFrame` sampler recording every frame's `text`/`left`/`width`/`top` during an actual count-up, which surfaced a second thing worth recording: the live page *does* show `left`/`width`/`top` drifting during roughly the first 700ms of an animation, but a frame-by-frame trace proved this is the page's own pre-existing `.fade-in` entrance transition (`transform: translateY(20px) scale(0.97)` easing to rest, present on `.focus-card` and `.exam-countdown` themselves, same as every other card on this page) compounding with the digit count-up in that same window — a `scale()` transform changes `getBoundingClientRect()`'s reported width/position even though the underlying CSS box is unchanged, which is what the raw numbers were actually showing, not a spacing-reservation failure. Once sampled past that ~700ms settle window, position was pixel-stable. Nothing about the fade-in itself was touched — it's expected, shared, pre-existing site behavior, not something this feature introduced or something the "manage the spacing" ask was about. **Animation smoothness**: swapped the ease-out-cubic timing curve for an exponential ease-out (`1 - 2^(-10·progress)`, decelerates much harder into the landing than cubic does) and extended the duration 900ms → 1150ms for a more deliberate, premium-feeling count rather than a linear-ish digit flip. Added a one-shot `.pop` class + `@keyframes exam-countdown-pop` (scale 1 → 1.18 → 1) that fires the instant the count-up lands on its final value, reusing the exact `cubic-bezier(.34,1.56,.64,1)` overshoot-and-settle curve already used elsewhere on this page for bump/pop effects, rather than inventing a new easing curve — confirmed via the same rAF-sampler instrumentation that the `.pop` class is genuinely absent through the whole counting run and only appears on the very last sampled frame. Not tested on mobile for the same reason as the pass above — Focus Mode is desktop-only.
- **A lighting-based separator between the countdown chip and the ring/leaderboard below it was tried, rejected twice, then abandoned — 2026-09-08.** First attempt: a blurred radial-gradient "light pool" (`::after` on `.exam-countdown`, `filter: blur(3px)`) sitting in the existing 22px flex gap. Direct feedback ("it looks terrible," confirmed via a follow-up multi-select question to isolate which part) identified this specifically — the blur read as a smudge, not a light effect. Second attempt: replaced it with a small glowing *line* instead (a `linear-gradient` bar + `box-shadow` glow, no `filter: blur()` at all) — a more contained, deliberate-looking effect, and one that measured cleanly in isolation. **Rejected again anyway** ("still looks terrible"). Rather than try a third lighting variant blind, pulled the effect entirely — the separator is now just the existing 22px flex gap alone, same plain spacing every other section boundary on this page already uses, no glow of any kind. In hindsight this is probably the right call structurally, not just a retreat: the page had just gone through the glassmorphic→minimalist pass above (removing every blur/glow-based card treatment site-wide), so re-introducing a glow here — no matter how well executed — was reintroducing exactly the visual language that pass had just deliberately eliminated everywhere else. If a "lighting" separator is revisited, it should probably be reconsidered against that constraint explicitly (does *any* glow effect belong on a page that just went flat) rather than iterating on execution quality alone. **Resolved properly the same day**: "make the countdown chip separate" — moved `buildStatsPanelHtml()`'s output out of `.focus-card` entirely rather than trying yet another internal-divider treatment. It used to render as the card's first flex child, sharing one continuous border/background with the ring/leaderboard section; now it's a genuinely separate sibling element (wrapped in a new `.exam-countdown-wrap` for centering + a bottom margin, since it no longer sits inside `.focus-card`'s flex column and needs its own layout instead of inheriting `align-self: center` from a parent it's no longer part of) rendered directly above `.focus-card` in the DOM. Real page background now shows between the two boxes, which is what actually reads as "separate" — no visual effect needed once the two are structurally two different elements instead of one. `animateExamCountdown()`/`#exam-countdown-num` needed no changes — `document.getElementById` doesn't care where in the DOM the element lives. Verified against a real disposable test student that both boxes render correctly, the chip sits centered above the card with a clear gap, and the count-up animation still fires normally in its new position.
- **Start/Reset/Skip got icons plus a bouncier press, same day** ("make the ... button animations and look better"). Was plain text pills with a flat `scale(0.96)` press and no icons. Each button now pairs a small inline SVG icon (`.pomo-btn-icon`, new — play/pause/reset/skip glyphs, defined once as shared `POMO_ICON_*` constants in `progress.js` since the Start/Pause one needs to be swapped live, not just set once at render) with its label (`.pomo-btn-label`); the buttons became `inline-flex` to hold both. The press itself now releases on the site's own signature overshoot curve (`cubic-bezier(.34,1.56,.64,1)`, the same one `.focus-toggle`/`.nav-dd-item` already use) instead of the plain ease every transform on this element used before, and the press scale deepened slightly (0.96 → 0.93) for more noticeable feedback; hover gained a touch of `scale(1.02)` alongside the existing `translateY(-2px)` lift. **The Start/Pause icon itself swaps with a small bounce** (`.pomo-btn-icon-pop`, a `scale(0.4→1.25→1)` keyframe on just the icon, not the whole button) every time `pomo.running` flips — `updatePomoDisplay()` now sets the icon's `innerHTML` directly (previously it only ever set `toggleBtn.textContent`, since there was no icon to preserve) and re-triggers the animation via remove-class → force reflow (`void el.offsetWidth`) → re-add-class, the standard trick for restarting a CSS animation that's already present, since simply re-adding an already-present class is a no-op. Verified against a real disposable test student (with notification permission pre-granted via `Browser.grantPermissions` so Start didn't hit the permission-prompt branch instead of actually starting): screenshotted the idle state (Start, play icon), clicked Start, confirmed the button's label read "Pause" and the icon visibly mid-bounce in a screenshot taken 150ms into the animation, then paused again to leave the test student idle before cleanup.
- **A nav logo hover-jitter theory was fixed site-wide, then turned out to be the wrong bug — "the logos move jitterily on hover" (2026-09-08) was actually about the Pomodoro Start/Reset/Skip button icons, not the nav logo at all; see the next bullet for the real fix.** The nav-logo change described below is still real, still shipped, and structurally correct on its own merits — just not what this specific report was about. Two more user messages narrowed it down ("the jitter i was talking about was about start reset and skip button", then "i meant the logos beside the texts" — "logos" being this user's word for the small icon graphics beside each button's label, added in the immediately preceding change). Worth remembering for next time: when a vague visual complaint ("jitter", "logos") arrives right after a UI change, check what was JUST touched before reaching for a same-page-but-unrelated theory, especially when a literal keyword match ("logos" → the actual nav logo) is available — it's an easy, plausible-sounding wrong guess.
- **The shared nav logo's hover jitter was fixed site-wide** ("the logos move jitterily on hover," 2026-09-08) — every one of the 7 main pages carries the exact same `.nav-logo:hover { transform: translateY(-2px); ... }` rule (the nav is a React/JSX component ported verbatim across all of them, see "Tech stack"), and all 7 had the same underlying bug: putting `transform` directly on the SAME element `:hover` is tested against means the hover-detection box itself moves the instant hover starts. A cursor resting near the edge the transform moves away from (here, the bottom edge, since `translateY(-2px)` moves the box up) can end up outside the shifted box, firing `mouseleave`, which reverts the transform and moves the box back under the cursor, re-firing `mouseenter` — a rapid on/off flicker. Fixed by moving the `transform` off `.nav-logo` itself and onto the inner `svg`/`img` instead (`.nav-logo:hover svg, .nav-logo:hover img { transform: translateY(-2px); }`) — hover detection now stays on `.nav-logo`, whose own box never moves, while only the inner logo visually lifts; same restructuring for the `:active` press state and the mobile-breakpoint variant (which no longer needs to re-state `translateX(-50%)` alongside the lift, since the parent's transform is now constant and only the child moves). Applied identically to all 7 pages (`index.html`, `gate-da-courses.html`, `gate-da-test-series.html`, `gate-da-toppers.html`, `contact.html`, `gate-da-progress-tracker.html`, `gate-da-free-notes.html` — the last one is missing the `:active` state and the "Home" hover tooltip the other 6 have, so its fix is correspondingly smaller, just the hover transform move) rather than only the page this was reported on, since it's the same shared component everywhere and the bug was structural, not page-specific. **Verified the fix eliminates the actual mechanism, not just "looks fine now"**: dispatched a real `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` mousemove to the exact bottom-edge pixel of the logo (the specific boundary the bug depended on) and re-checked computed styles 6 times in a row at that same fixed coordinate — `.nav-logo:hover` matched every time, `getComputedStyle(.nav-logo).transform` stayed `none` throughout (proving the hover box itself never moves, so there's no geometry for a cursor to fall outside of), while the inner image's own transform consistently showed the `translateY(-2px)` lift. This is a structural guarantee, not a timing-dependent one — the feedback loop the original bug depended on is no longer possible by construction, regardless of exact cursor position or animation timing.
- **The real jitter, found and fixed the same day: the Pomodoro Start/Pause icon was bouncing every second while the timer ran, not just when actually clicked.** `updatePomoDisplay()` — which drives the countdown ring/time text/labels — runs once per second via `pomoTickCore()`'s own `setTimeout(pomoTick, 1000)` self-scheduling, not only when Start/Pause is actually pressed. The icon-swap logic added in the buttons-enhancement pass above (`toggleIcon.innerHTML = ...; classList.remove/add('pomo-btn-icon-pop')`) had no guard against this — it unconditionally replayed the swap-and-pop bounce on *every single call*, meaning the icon visibly bounced once a second for the entire length of a running session, not once per genuine Start/Pause toggle. Fixed with a `data-state="play"|"pause"` attribute on `#pomo-toggle-icon` (set in the initial render markup to match whatever's actually shown, and read/written by `updatePomoDisplay()`) — the swap-and-pop block now only runs when `pomo.running`'s derived state actually differs from what's already recorded there, a no-op on every other one of the 60-ish per-minute calls that don't represent a real toggle. **Verified precisely, not just "looks better now"**: instrumented a `MutationObserver` on the icon element from a real disposable test student, started the timer, and confirmed zero further class/attribute mutations across ~4.5 seconds of ticking (previously this would have shown one bounce per second) — the fix genuinely stops the per-tick replay, not just makes it look less obvious in a screenshot. One loose end noted but not chased further: the observer logged two near-identical mutation records at the exact same millisecond on the initial Start click itself (both reflecting the same before→after class change) — most likely a MutationObserver/headless-Chrome batching artifact rather than a real double-execution (the actual reported bug, the per-second replay, is unambiguously gone regardless), but worth another look if a real "double bounce on the very first click only" report ever surfaces.
- **Still not done — one more report the same day: "when i still hover the logos beside start reset and skip still have an uncanny motion."** The per-tick fix above was real and necessary but didn't address this; this was the ORIGINAL hover-transform-quality theory from the very first buttons pass, which turned out to be correct after all, just not what the first two reports in this chain ("jitter" alone, then narrowed to "logos beside the texts") turned out to be about. Root cause: hover on `.pomo-btn` combined `translateY(-2px)` with `scale(1.02)` on the overshoot bezier `cubic-bezier(.34,1.56,.64,1)` — a ~13px icon resizing by just 2% while riding an oscillating easing curve is a visible wobble at that size (sub-pixel anti-aliasing shifts read as "uncanny" far more readily on a small icon than the same relative motion on the button's own text label). Fixed by dropping `scale()` from hover entirely and switching hover's easing to a plain non-oscillating `ease-out` — now just `translateY(-2px)`, matching every other proven-non-jittery hover-lift button already on this page (`.focus-toggle`, `.month-nav button`). The overshoot bounce wasn't deleted outright — moved to `:active` only (via a `transition` override scoped to that state), since a discrete momentary press doesn't carry the same continuously-reevaluated-hover-state risk a wobbling idle-hover animation does. **Verified with real hover, not a synthetic DOM event**: a JS-dispatched `MouseEvent('mouseover')` does NOT drive real `:hover` (confirmed directly — `getComputedStyle` showed no transform at all across 22 sampled frames using that approach, since `:hover` matching is driven by the renderer's actual tracked pointer position, not JS event dispatch); switched to CDP's `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` (an actual synthetic pointer move) and re-sampled — the button's translateY progressed smoothly and monotonically from 0 to exactly -2px across ~14 frames with no overshoot past -2 and no oscillation back toward 0, while the icon itself showed no independent transform of its own at any sampled frame (it just travels with the button as a normal flex child, never resizes). This is the frame-by-frame proof the wobble is actually gone, not just a subjective "looks calmer" judgment.
- **Still not fixed after that — "still it exists," then narrowed with a preview-style question to "a visible jump/snap" specifically (not a shake/wobble, not a delayed-settle).** The previous pass's fix was real (the overshoot wobble it targeted is genuinely gone, confirmed by the frame data above) but wasn't the actual complaint. A jump/snap description points at a different, more fundamental issue than easing quality: a `translateY(-2px)` transition, however mathematically smooth its curve, only advances a fraction of a device pixel per frame at 1x DPI over ~200ms — browsers snap composited transform layers to whole device pixels when painting, so a movement this small on something this small (a ~13px icon, where one pixel is ~8% of its own size) inherently renders as a discrete step between 2-3 pixel positions, not a glide. No easing curve can fix that — it's a rendering-precision ceiling, not an animation-quality problem, which is why two rounds of curve/scale adjustments both failed to resolve it. **Fixed by removing the hover transform entirely** — `.pomo-btn-primary:hover`/`.pomo-btn-secondary:hover` now only change `box-shadow`/`background`, no `transform` at all, so there is no position for anything (button or icon) to snap between. The `:active` press bounce (`scale(0.93)`) is untouched — a discrete, one-off press has no "compare this frame against the last one" continuity for a viewer to perceive stepping in, unlike a continuously-rendered idle-hover animation. **Verified at the pixel level this time, not just via `getComputedStyle`'s transform matrix** (which had already looked clean in the previous pass despite the bug still being real) — captured the icon's real `getBoundingClientRect()` before hover and again ~300ms after a real CDP `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` settled onto the button: `x`/`y`/`width`/`height` were byte-for-byte identical in both reads, and both showed `transform: none`. There is now categorically no movement for a snap to occur in, not just a smoother version of the same movement.
- **The daily top-10 board (inside Focus Mode) got a staggered entrance on genuine page load/visit, richened up after an initial pass felt flat** ("can i have subtle animation as the daily top 10 loads on page reload or page visit?", then "nah make a better animation" once shipped, 2026-09-08). `renderTodayLeaderboardRows()` gained an `animate` param — when true, each row gets a `.leaderboard-row--enter` class plus an inline `animation-delay` of `index * 45ms`, giving a top-to-bottom cascade rather than all 10 rows (or up to 11, with the viewer's own gap row) popping in at once. First cut was a plain 0.4s fade + 8px rise on a gentle ease — rejected outright. Shipped version: a 0.55s fade + 16px rise + a touch of scale (`0.96 → 1`), riding the site's own signature overshoot-and-settle bezier (`cubic-bezier(.34,1.56,.64,1)`, already used for bump/pop moments elsewhere on this page) instead of a plain ease, so each row arrives with a small deliberate bounce. The `scale()` here is safe in a way it wasn't for the Pomodoro button icon a few entries above — this animates a large ~300px-wide row once on page load, not a ~13px icon under continuous hover, so the pixel-snapping-reads-as-a-jump failure mode documented there doesn't apply to a one-shot animation at this size. `both` fill-mode holds the from-keyframe (invisible) through each row's delay so staggered rows don't flash visible-then-hidden first. **Deliberately only passed `true` from `renderTodayLeaders()` (the genuine initial render) — `refreshLeaderboard()`'s poll-driven patch of `#today-leaderboard-rows` calls the same function with no argument**, so the stagger plays once per real page visit/Focus-Mode-entry, not every 30-60s poll tick — same "don't replay an entrance effect on a routine background update" discipline the rest of this page already applies to whole-card `.fade-in`s, just scoped per-row here instead. Respects `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`. Scoped to the daily top-10 board specifically, per what was asked — the weekly top-20 board and last-week champions card share the same underlying row-render pattern but were left untouched. Verified against a real disposable test student: captured a burst of screenshots across the ~1s stagger+bounce window (50ms through 1050ms) confirming the cascade visibly progresses row-by-row and settles cleanly with nothing stuck mid-animation; earlier opacity/delay sampling on the first-draft version (before the bounce/scale rework) had already confirmed the underlying stagger timing mechanism itself was correct, so the rework only needed re-verifying the new keyframe's visual result, not the delay plumbing again.
- **"same for the top 20" — extended the stagger-bounce entrance to the weekly top-20 board too, same day, and hit a real timing gap doing it that the daily board never had.** `renderLeaderboardRows()` gained the same `animate` param, `renderLeaderboardCard()` passes it, and the shared helper was renamed `todayLeaderboardRowEnterAttrs` → `leaderboardRowEnterAttrs` since it's now used by both boards. **The gap**: the daily top-10 board's data (`state.todayLeaders`) is already in hand from the initial `tracker-data` batch fetch by the time Focus Mode is entered, so its card's synchronous first render IS the real, data-having render — passing `animate=true` there just works. `state.leaderboard` (top-20) is different — it's *only* ever populated by `refreshLeaderboard()`'s own async fetch, which `enterFocus()` kicks off right *after* rendering the card, so `renderLeaderboardCard()`'s synchronous call almost always still sees `state.leaderboard.length === 0` (rendering the "No focus sessions logged yet" empty-state message, not real rows) — meaning the *actual* first appearance of real rows happens later, inside `refreshLeaderboard()`'s resolve, which deliberately never animates (that's the poll-tick path). Naively passing `animate=true` to `renderLeaderboardCard()` alone was therefore a no-op in practice — confirmed empirically, not just reasoned about: an rAF-sampling probe watching `#leaderboard-rows` from before the click showed rows appearing already at `opacity:1` with no `.leaderboard-row--enter` class at all on the first population. **Fixed with a `leaderboardRowsAnimatedThisVisit` flag**: `renderLeaderboardCard()` sets it (and animates immediately) only if `state.leaderboard` already has data at that exact synchronous moment (true on a *re-entry* into Focus Mode later in the same page visit, where a previous `refreshLeaderboard()` already populated it — re-entry then correctly replays the stagger immediately, no fetch wait needed); otherwise `refreshLeaderboard()`'s *own* resolve checks the flag, animates on whichever call is the first to actually deliver `state.leaderboard.length > 0`, and sets the flag so every resolve after that (the routine 30-60s poll) doesn't replay it. Re-verified with the same rAF-sampling technique after the fix: the frame where rows first appear now genuinely carries `leaderboard-row--enter` starting at `opacity:0`, progressing smoothly through the fade exactly like the daily board's already-working case — the empirical check that caught the bug in the first place is also what confirmed the fix.
- **The Pomodoro gauge's dome got speedometer-style dial ticks** ("can we have mock lines that of a car's speedometer?", 2026-09-08). `pomoTickMarksHtml()` (new, `progress.js`, defined near `POMO_RING_R` since it shares that same center/radius) draws 11 short radial `<line>`s evenly spaced across the arc's own 180-degree sweep, computed with plain trig (`cx=100, cy=95` — the same center the arc's own `M 25 95 A 75 75 0 0 1 175 95` path uses) rather than hardcoded coordinates, alternating longer "major" ticks (even indices) and shorter "minor" ticks (odd indices) the way a real gauge dial does. Radii (`rOuter=66`, `rMajorInner=52`, `rMinorInner=60`) are deliberately kept inside `r=70` — the arc's own stroke (width 10, centered on `r=75`) visually spans `r=70` to `r=80`, so ticks at `rOuter=66` sit fully clear of it, never overlapping or getting hidden under the colored progress stroke. Styled neutral/flat (`.pomo-tick`/`.pomo-tick--major`, translucent white) regardless of work/break mode — deliberately not switching color with the arc's own work/break gradient, so they read as fixed dial markings rather than another mode-colored element competing with the gradient arc and gradient time text for attention. Rendered once into the SVG right after `<defs>`, since `renderPomodoro()` itself only ever runs once per page load (same reasoning already documented for the gradient `<linearGradient>` defs it sits beside). Verified visually via a real disposable test student in Focus Mode — ticks render cleanly inside the dome, evenly spaced, clear of both the arc and the FOCUS/BREAK label.
- **`.focus-card` got the same dotted texture already used behind the bundled-courses section on `gate-da-courses.html`** ("the dotted background we have behind the bundled courses card, can we have the same behind the pomodoro cards?", 2026-09-08). That section (`#combos`) layers two static `background-image`s: an inline SVG `feTurbulence` fractal-noise grain tile (`200px 200px`) and a fine dot grid (`radial-gradient(rgba(...) 1px, transparent 1px)`, `28px 28px`) — copied onto `.focus-card` verbatim (same noise SVG data URI, same `28px` dot spacing), with the dot opacity bumped from `#combos`'s `0.055` to `0.09` since `#combos` sits on a much darker `#07060f` base than this page's `--card` (`#1e1e24`) — the same low opacity would have been close to invisible here. Deliberately NOT the canvas-based `DotField` component `#combos` also layers on top of this same CSS pattern elsewhere on the site — that's a separate, much heavier animated/mouse-reactive effect, not what a "dotted background" request was asking for; confirmed directly when asked separately ("will it use too much resources?") that this is a plain static `background-image` with no JS/canvas involved, categorically different from the WebGL/Canvas effects this codebase's own known-gotchas already flag as expensive (a continuous-render-loop cost, which a static repeating CSS tile simply doesn't have). Applied via `background-image`/`background-size` layered on top of `.focus-card`'s existing `background: var(--card)` (not replacing it) — the same two-declaration pattern `#combos` itself uses. `.pomodoro-card`/`.today-card`/`.leaderboard-card` inside `.focus-card` already had `background: none` from the earlier glassmorphic→minimalist pass, so the texture shows through all three automatically, no changes needed there. Verified with a 6x-zoomed screenshot of genuinely-empty card space (not a card edge or child boundary, which an earlier, less careful crop had mistaken for "texture missing here" before re-checking computed `background-image` on both `#focus-card` and `.today-card` directly) — both the grain and the dot grid are clearly visible and correctly positioned.
- **Corrected the same day: the static texture above was the wrong layer — "i see nothing" / "those were not static dots" made clear the actual reference was `#combos`'s *other*, animated layer, the canvas-based `DotField` React component (mouse-reactive dots that bulge away from the cursor), not the flat CSS pattern.** In hindsight the first pass's own reasoning ("deliberately NOT the canvas-based DotField... that's not what a 'dotted background' request was asking for") was an assumption that turned out backwards — the real component was exactly what was meant, and the static texture alone was subtle enough at this page's lighter `--card` tone to read as "nothing" at a glance. Since this page's tracker portion is plain JS (only the shared nav is React/JSX), `DotField` itself (a `React.memo` using hooks/refs) couldn't be reused directly — ported the underlying Canvas2D logic to two plain functions, `startPomoDotField()`/`stopPomoDotField()` (`progress.js`, near `renderPomodoro()`), matching the physics/constants of the site's *actual* `combos-dotfield` usage exactly (`dotRadius=3.5`, `dotSpacing=14`, `bulgeStrength=67`, the same two gradient colors) rather than the component's fuller default feature set (sparkle, wave motion, a visible cursor-follow glow) — all three are explicitly off in that real usage too (`sparkle={false}`, `waveAmplitude={0}`, `glowRadius={0}`), so porting them would have been dead code from the start. Scoped to `#pomo-dotfield-canvas` behind just `.pomo-today-row` (the timer + today's-leaderboard row), not the much taller `.focus-card` that also holds the day's checklist — dots bulging under real checkbox/task text would hurt readability for no benefit, and "the pomodoro cards" (this request's own wording) reads as the timer/leaderboard specifically. `.pomo-dotfield` sits `position:absolute; inset:0; z-index:0; pointer-events:none;` behind the two cards (each given `position:relative; z-index:1;` so they paint above it); both already had transparent (`background: none`) backgrounds from the earlier glassmorphic→minimalist pass, so the dots show through them with no further changes needed there. **Lifecycle discipline, given `renderCalendar()` fully replaces `app.innerHTML` (and therefore the canvas) on every call**: `stopPomoDotField()` runs unconditionally at the very top of `renderCalendar()`, before that replacement, cancelling the running `requestAnimationFrame` loop/interval/`resize`+`mousemove` listeners so nothing is ever left pointing at a detached canvas; `startPomoDotField()` runs at the tail, only `if (state.focus)`, against the fresh canvas element. Verified thoroughly, not just visually: (1) read the canvas's own pixel data directly (`getImageData`) and confirmed ~24,000 non-transparent pixels out of ~485,000 total — genuine dot content being drawn, not an empty/transparent canvas; (2) drove a real synthetic cursor across the canvas via `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` and confirmed a visible bulge-displacement in a before/after screenshot pair; (3) stress-tested the cleanup discipline specifically — entered and exited Focus Mode four times in a row (via real `history.back()`, the actual exit mechanism, not just a state flag) and confirmed zero thrown exceptions and that `#pomo-dotfield-canvas` is genuinely removed from the DOM after each exit, i.e. no leaked rAF loop surviving past the canvas that spawned it; (4) confirmed Start/Reset/Skip still work correctly with the new absolutely-positioned canvas layer sitting behind them (`pointer-events:none` on the dotfield container is what guarantees this, but tested the real click path directly rather than trusting that alone).
- **The whole dotted-background effort above (both bullets) was cancelled the same day and fully reverted** — a legitimate follow-up question ("will it use too much resources... students use this page 12-14 hours daily") led to actually measuring it (CDP `Performance.getMetrics`, not assumption): with the effect widened to cover the whole tall `.focus-card` per a request in progress at the time ("i wanted it to be outside behind the cards," ~6,600 dots at this spacing once it spanned the checklist section too), the unconditionally-running `requestAnimationFrame` loop cost ≈0.79s of `ScriptDuration` over a 15s sample — a real, sustained ~5% of one CPU core for as long as Focus Mode stayed open, not the "no measurable cost" this file's own known-gotchas note attributes to the much smaller `DotField` usage on `gate-da-courses.html` (a shorter section, far fewer dots). An idle-pause fix was built and measured working (`ScriptDuration` dropped from `0.070s`/5s while actively settling to `0.006s`/20s once idle — the loop stops scheduling itself ~1.5s after the mouse stops moving nearby, restarting only on real movement) — but the request was cancelled outright before that optimized version ever shipped ("cancel it," following "can the whole grid idea"). Reverted via `git checkout ee009ba -- gate-da-progress-tracker.html progress.js` (a real, tracked commit, not a silent discard) — `ee009ba` being the last commit before any of this dotted-background work started — bringing both files back to a plain flat `.focus-card` with no texture and no canvas at all. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped once more afterward specifically to resync `progress.js` and `lib/supabase.js`'s copies, which the revert had left pointing at two different stale values from mid-sequence. The two bullets above are kept rather than deleted, same as this file's established practice for other tried-and-rejected work (e.g. the lighting-separator saga) — they're an accurate record of what was actually built and why it didn't ship, not documentation of current behavior.
- **Two small, unrelated follow-ups the same day**: (1) the speedometer dial ticks (see the earlier entry above) were sized down — `pomoTickMarksHtml()`'s major/minor tick lengths shrank from 14px/6px to 8px/4px (`rMajorInner`/`rMinorInner` moved from `52`/`60` to `58`/`62`, keeping the same `rOuter=66`), and `.pomo-tick`/`.pomo-tick--major`'s stroke widths thinned from `1.5`/`2` to `1`/`1.5` — smaller, more delicate dial marks, still clearly readable as gauge ticks in a screenshot check. (2) `.exam-countdown-num` (the "days till GATE" chip's number) picked up the same animated pink→purple→blue gradient text treatment already shared by `.streak-number`/`.program-day-num`/`.pomodoro-time` (`background: linear-gradient(120deg,#FF7FB7,var(--purple),#4D8BFF); background-size:200% 100%; -webkit-background-clip:text; background-clip:text; color:transparent; animation: streak-grad 6s ease infinite;`, reusing the shared `@keyframes streak-grad` rather than defining a new one) instead of flat `--ink` — ties it into the same "real stat number" visual language the page's other headline figures already use. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped for the tick-size change (a real `progress.js` geometry change); the gradient number is CSS-only.
- **The top-20 board's Timer column became a depleting progress bar instead of MM:SS digits** ("instead of the timer digits... can't we show custom decreasing progress bar for every student. will use too much resources?", 2026-09-08). Traced the resource question against the actual existing mechanism before building anything, learning from the DotField saga just above — `tickLeaderboardTimers()` already re-queries and updates every `.leaderboard-timer` in the DOM once a second (bounded to ~20 rows), so swapping a `textContent` write for a `style.width` write is the same order of magnitude, categorically unlike a 60fps canvas loop; didn't need a fresh CPU measurement to know that, just reading the existing code. **The one real data gap**: `phase_end_at` alone gives a countdown, not a *fraction* — a bar needs the phase's total length too, which is customizable per student (1-120min for work). `fetchLiveStatusByEmail` (`lib/supabase.js`) now also selects `pomo_active_session.total_seconds` and returns it as `pomo_phase_total_seconds`, flowing through `pomodoro-leaderboard.js`'s existing `pomoFieldsFor()` spread with no changes needed there beyond updating its own not-live fallback object to match the new field shape. `pomoTimerHtml(phaseEndAt, totalSeconds, mode)` renders a small `<span class="leaderboard-timer">` track containing a `.leaderboard-timer-fill` sized to `remaining/total`, falling back to `total = remaining` if `total_seconds` is ever missing (bar just starts full rather than breaking); `mode` colors the fill with the same pink/purple/blue-for-work, green/cyan-for-break gradient the ring itself already uses (`.leaderboard-timer--break`). The exact remaining time isn't gone — it's a `title` tooltip now, kept in sync by the same per-second tick. **A real UX bug avoided by scoping the track background to `[data-phase-end]` specifically**: the fill lives inside a track `<span>` styled with a background — if that background applied unconditionally, every non-live student's row would show an empty track pill with nothing in it (worse than the old behavior, where a non-live row was simply blank); scoped via `.leaderboard-timer[data-phase-end]` so only rows that actually have a phase to show get a visible track at all. `transition: width 1s linear` on the fill makes each second's jump glide smoothly rather than stepping, matching the "decreasing" framing of the ask. Verified against real production data, not synthetic: curled the live endpoint directly and confirmed genuinely different `pomo_phase_total_seconds` values per real student (their actual customized session lengths — 3600s, 1500s, 7200s, etc.); loaded the real page as a real (then-deleted) disposable test student and confirmed several real live students' bars showing distinct, correct fill percentages matching their own remaining/total fractions, a real Break-phase student correctly showing the green/cyan gradient, and non-live students correctly showing no bar at all.
  - **The bars weren't centered under the "TIMER" column header, fixed 2026-09-14** — `.leaderboard-timer` is a direct grid item inside `.leaderboard-row` (this column has no wrapping cell of its own, just this span positioned by the row's `grid-template-columns`), and a fixed-width (`46px`) `inline-flex` item defaults to sitting at its column's start edge rather than centering, unlike the header's plain text (which centers via `text-align`). Fixed by adding `justify-self: center` to `.leaderboard-timer` — centers a grid item within its own cell along the inline axis, the correct tool for this specific mismatch (a fixed-width item inside a wider column) rather than `text-align`, which doesn't apply to a flex box. Verified against 6 real rows on the live weekly board: every bar's horizontal center now matches the header's center exactly (byte-identical `getBoundingClientRect()` center-x, not just "looks close" in a screenshot).
  - **"How can the timer bar be better?" — the bars now flag urgency, not just work/break mode, 2026-09-14.** A bar at 8% remaining and one at 92% used to look identical hue-wise (fill color only ever reflected work vs. break), so who's about to finish never stood out without hovering for the tooltip. `LEADERBOARD_TIMER_URGENT_PCT` (10) in `pomoTimerHtml()` and `tickLeaderboardTimers()` both now check the current fraction — under that threshold, `.leaderboard-timer--urgent` switches the fill to a warm amber→red gradient regardless of mode (urgency reads as more useful than work/break at that point) plus a slow pulse. `tickLeaderboardTimers()` toggles the class every second, not just `pomoTimerHtml()` at render time — a bar that was rendered well above the threshold needs to actually gain the class once it ticks down past it while already on screen, which could be minutes after that row last re-rendered. **The pulse is opacity-only, no `transform`/`scale`, deliberately** — this bar is exactly the size class (46×6px) that a much earlier round of this same session already proved snaps/jitters visibly under any `transform`-based animation (see the Pomodoro Start/Reset/Skip button-icon jitter saga above); opacity has no geometry to snap between, sidestepping that failure mode entirely rather than re-learning it. Respects `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`. Verified against a real seeded session (not a synthetic CSS-only check) — a disposable test student's `pomo_active_session` set to 3.5% remaining (53s left of a 1500s phase) correctly showed `leaderboard-timer--urgent` applied, the exact amber→red `background-image` computed, and `animationName: leaderboard-timer-urgent-pulse` all present on the real live page. **That first verification only covered a work-phase row — a direct follow-up ("hope it's not the same case for break") caught that the break case hadn't actually been checked, only reasoned about via CSS cascade order.** Verified separately with a seeded break-phase session (12s left of a 300s break, `mode: 'break'`): the row correctly carries both `leaderboard-timer--break` and `leaderboard-timer--urgent` classes at once, and the computed fill `background-image` is the amber→red urgency gradient, not the green→cyan break one — the later-source-order rule genuinely wins for real, not just by inspection of the stylesheet. **Immediately reversed at direct follow-up request ("we don't need urgency for break")** — on reflection, a "hurry up" cue only makes sense for a focus session about to end, not a break, which doesn't need urgency pressure at all. `pomoTimerHtml()`'s `urgent` check gained `mode !== 'break' &&`, and `tickLeaderboardTimers()`'s per-second toggle now reads whether the element already carries `leaderboard-timer--break` (set at render time, so reliable enough for this — that class is only ever touched by a full row re-render, same staleness level the fill color itself already has) before allowing the urgent class at all. Re-verified with the same seeded-session technique both ways: a break row at 4% remaining now correctly keeps its plain green→cyan fill with no urgent class and no animation, both at initial render and after several more ticks; a work row at the same 4% still correctly turns amber→red — confirming the fix actually changed break's behavior without quietly breaking work's.
  - **"There is just a little feedback on the start pause button... can there be a bigger feedback on the clock... so there is a lesser frequency of misclicks," 2026-09-14.** The existing icon-pop bounce on Start/Pause is real feedback but small (~13px icon) and easy to miss, which is exactly what invited a second, misclick-y press when someone wasn't confident the first one registered. Added a bigger confirmation on genuinely starting (or resuming from pause) a phase: `.pomo-ring-start-flash`, a one-shot class on `.pomodoro-ring-wrap` (280px+ — the whole ring+mode+time group scales together as one, since everything visually inside the wrapper rides along with its `transform`) triggered from the exact same guarded block in `updatePomoDisplay()` that already fires the icon-pop (same `data-state`-based "did `pomo.running` genuinely just change" guard, so this doesn't replay every second like the icon-pop bug it deliberately avoids repeating), but scoped further to only the transition *into* running — never on Pause, since the ask was specifically about the start moment. A brief scale pulse (`cubic-bezier(.34,1.56,.64,1)`, the site's own established overshoot curve) plus a synced glow-intensity flash on the ring stroke itself (`filter: drop-shadow(...)` animating up then back to its steady-state value). **Deliberately safe to scale here unlike the small Pomodoro button icons** documented earlier in this file (their jitter saga is the reason `transform` was removed from their own hover state entirely) — 280px+ has no pixel-snapping risk at this scale delta, unlike a ~13px icon where even a few-percent scale reads as a visible snap. Respects `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`. Verified with 7 checks against a real Focus session: no flash before Start, flash class applied immediately on click, a mid-animation `getComputedStyle().transform` sample showing genuine scaling (`matrix(1.039...)`, not `none`), settling back to `transform: none` once the animation ends, zero `animationstart` events fired across 3.5s of ticking while running (confirms the per-tick-replay bug class is correctly avoided), no flash on Pause, and a flash again on Resume.
  - **The daily top-10 "Today's Leaders" board briefly got Status + Timer columns matching the weekly board, shipped then reverted the same day (2026-09-21).** Built in response to "can you include the timer status column here?" — `renderTodayLeaderboardRows()` gained `pomoStatusHtml(...)`/`pomoTimerHtml(...)` calls and `renderTodayLeaders()` gained a `.leaderboard-columns` header row, needing zero server changes since `fetchTodayLeaders()` already spread the live-status fields unused. Reverted immediately at the very next message ("revert") with no further elaboration given — taken as referring to this change specifically since it was the last thing shipped and discussed. `progress.js`/`gate-da-progress-tracker.html` are back to the daily board's pre-existing shape (no header row, no Status/Timer columns); the weekly board's own Status/Timer columns are untouched. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again for the revert itself, same as the original ship — an already-open tab needs to pick up either direction of a real rendering change.
  - **A screenshot of Today's Leaders showing "GATE DA ASPI…" (truncated) was first misread as "the live dot is too close to the name" and fixed the wrong way — widening `.live-dot-slot`/`.leaderboard-col-name`'s padding 13px→18px, shipped, then immediately corrected by direct follow-up ("i didn't ask you to do that i said the names are not visible full... increase the length of the names visible") and reverted back to 13px.** The real cause, found only once actually looking at where this card renders: at wide viewports, Today's Leaders sits beside the Pomodoro ring in `.pomo-today-row` (see the "sits in the timer's own unused side space" comment near line 440), where `#today-leaderboard-card` is deliberately capped at a narrow `flex: 0 1 300px` companion width — narrow enough that the row's rank/arrow columns (`40px`/`16px`, sized for the full-width boards) left too little of that 300px for the name before `text-overflow: ellipsis` kicked in; "GATE DA ASPIRANT" (16 chars) didn't fit. Fixed two ways together, scoped to this one narrow-card context only (`.pomo-today-row #today-leaderboard-card`, not the shared base `.leaderboard-row` rule other boards use): the card's own `grid-template-columns` override shrank its rank/arrow columns to `26px`/`10px` (a 1-2 digit rank doesn't need the full-width boards' allowance, and every pixel clawed back goes straight to the name's `minmax(0,1fr)` column), and the card's flex-basis grew `300px→360px` — confirmed there's real slack for this even at the narrowest viewport Focus Mode allows (1081px, per the `.focus-toggle-wrap` gate): the ring (340px, fixed) + gap (110px) + card need ~750px total against roughly 1000px available inside `.wrap`'s max-width at that breakpoint. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again for the real fix (a third bump in one day, after the wrong first guess and its revert). Verified against real production data, not synthetic: seeded a disposable test student and screenshotted the real card at 1440px — a real existing student's name ("GATE DA ASPIRANT") that was truncating before now renders in full; an artificially long stress-test name (26 chars) still truncates, as expected — the fix buys back real room, it doesn't make the column infinite. **Worth remembering**: a vague "spacing"/"visibility" complaint about names right after a truncation-prone layout change is worth checking for actual `text-overflow: ellipsis` truncation first, not just gap/padding between adjacent elements — the same "check what was just touched, and check the concrete rendered output" discipline this file's `feedback_visual_verification` memory already calls out, here missed because the fix was guessed from the screenshot's general cramped feel rather than reading what the "..." in "GATE DA ASPI…" actually meant.

Most rows carry 0-5 small streak balls, nowhere near enough to need the width that column was often sitting on entirely unused (confirmed visually — several real rows show no streak content at all next to a wide empty gap before Status). Grew Name's cap `130px→180px`, taking the extra 50px straight out of Streak's flexible space rather than widening the card itself (unlike Today's Leaders' narrow-card fix, this board already had plenty of width to spare in the one column built to absorb it). `.leaderboard-columns`' header grid updated to match so "NAME"/"STREAK" stay aligned with the data columns below them. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped.
