- **A real student report ("had already added 2 hours session in the morning but it isn't visible"), plus a second independent one from a different student the day before ("i think my time is not added .. i studied from 1 am to 2 am and now from 9 am to 11 30 am" showing only 1.8h), triggered an investigation that disproved the leading theory but couldn't pin the actual cause — leading to new logging instead of a fix, 2026-09-21.** Checked directly against production for the first student: `pomo_daily_sessions` had zero rows for that day at all (not even a partial one), while `pomo_active_session.credited_through` was still stamped from the *previous* day's last real credit — meaning the completion call was genuinely attempted (confirmed separately: a real "Break's over... 5/6 sessions today" notification had fired, which only happens after a work phase completes and `pomoAdvance()` runs) but never actually landed as a credit. **The leading theory — that a `CLIENT_VERSION`-mismatch-triggered mid-session reload was corrupting the server's verification state — was directly disproven, not just reasoned away**: reproduced the exact scenario against a real disposable test student (start a work phase, force a genuine page reload partway through via CDP, let it run to natural completion) and the session credited correctly regardless. Tracing why: `sendPomoStoppedBeacon()` does fire on a reload (it fires on every teardown, by design — see its own comment) and does force `pomo_active_session.running` to `false` server-side, but `pomodoro-complete.js`'s verification never actually checks `running` at all, only `mode`/`phase_end_at`/`phase_started_at`/`total_seconds` — none of which the beacon touches, since `isNewPhase` in `pomo-active.js` only resets `phase_started_at` when `running && secondsLeft === totalSeconds` (a genuine fresh start), which a beacon sending `running:false` can never trigger. **A second, real, separate bug was confirmed along the way, but ruled out as the cause of the missing credit**: `pomo.completedSessions` (the session-dot counter) never resets for a tab that's never reloaded, confirmed with a second student's raw `pomo_active_session.completed_sessions` reading **1026** — direct, hard evidence that tab has been continuously open for a very long time with zero reloads. This explains a confusing "5/6 sessions today" display (see the known-gotchas section for the already-documented, only-partially-fixed root cause) but is unrelated to server-side crediting, which never reads this counter for anything. **The actual root cause of the missing credit is still unknown** — every mechanism traceable from the code was checked and ruled out; what was missing was any server-side record of a rejected/errored completion attempt to actually investigate, since the endpoint's 400/500 responses left zero trace anywhere once sent. Fixed *that* gap instead: `pomodoro_credit_failures` (new table, `supabase/schema.sql`) logs every rejection from `pomodoro-complete.js` — `reason` (`no_active_session`/`wrong_mode`/`phase_end_mismatch`/`no_phase_started_at`/`invalid_total_seconds`/`insufficient_elapsed`/`invalid_minutes`, plus the three RPC-error paths), the claimed `phaseEndAt`, a snapshot of whatever `pomo_active_session` held at rejection time (`null` if no row existed at all), and the elapsed/claimed millisecond figures for the timing-based rejection. The single combined `if (!session || ... )` OR-condition was split into separate checks specifically so each branch can log its own precise reason — the old code could only ever say "could not verify this session" with no way to tell which of 5 different conditions actually failed. Logging is fire-and-forget-safe but not silently dropped: wrapped in its own try/catch (a logging failure must never turn an already-decided rejection into a 500, or mask the real error) but still `await`ed before responding, same "Netlify Functions don't reliably run background work after a response is sent" reasoning already applied elsewhere in this codebase. **A pre-existing, unrelated partial-failure gap was noticed while restructuring this, not fixed** — if `credit_pomodoro_phase` succeeds but the subsequent `increment_pomodoro_stats`/`increment_pomo_daily_sessions` calls fail, `credited_through` is already stamped, so a client retry of that same completion would hit the idempotent `alreadyCredited` no-op path and never actually get its minutes counted; now at least visible via the new `week_increment_error`/`day_increment_error` log reasons, not silently fixed. **Requires a manual step before it does anything**: like every other schema change in this project, the new `CREATE TABLE` can't be run through `supabase-js` — needs pasting into the Supabase SQL editor against production before `pomodoro_credit_failures` actually exists; until then the `try/catch` around the insert makes this a safe no-op, not a break. No `CLIENT_VERSION` bump — server-only, response shape unchanged for every existing caller.
  - **8-day retention added the same day** (originally 7, widened to 8 "to be safe" on direct follow-up), after a direct question about whether this table (and separately, the new `pomo_hourly_activity` table below) risked growing large enough to threaten the Supabase free tier. `pomo_hourly_activity` turned out to be a non-issue by construction (a fixed 24 rows, see its own entry below) — but `pomodoro_credit_failures` is genuinely different: a real per-event log with no natural cap, unlike every other table this project writes to on a schedule (`pomo_daily_sessions`/`pomodoro_stats` are bounded by active-student-count x elapsed time; this one just grows forever on every rejected completion, with the new retry logic in `recordPomodoroCompletion` meaning a single genuinely-failing session can now log up to 3 rows instead of 1). Growth is low in practice — this only fires on a *failure*, not every completion — but "low but unbounded" was worth capping rather than assumed to stay low. `credit-failure-cleanup.js` (new, scheduled function, `00:30` IST daily) deletes anything older than `RETENTION_DAYS` (8) — long enough to still be useful for correlating with a real student report shortly after it happens (the table's actual purpose), short enough that this table can never meaningfully grow past about a week and a day's worth of failures. Verified directly against the real production table (already migrated earlier this session): invoked locally via `curl localhost:8080/.netlify/functions/credit-failure-cleanup` and got a clean `{"ok":true,"deletedBefore":"..."}` response with the correct cutoff.
  - **This investigation also surfaced that production had gone stale — `taai.live/api/version` was still `2026-09-17-4`, 8 commits (and several real fixes, including this session's) behind what was in git.** Confirmed directly via `curl`, not assumed. Means none of this session's work — including the logging fix above — was actually live on the real site while this was being investigated; a genuinely-failed completion happening on the live site right now still leaves zero trace until an actual deploy ships this.
  - **Asked to "check the whole tracker" for related bugs given the pattern, a systematic pass through every remaining tracker Netlify Function turned up one real, live, currently-affecting-students bug, unrelated to Pomodoro crediting — `tracker-data.js`'s own `fetchStreak()` was a leftover, un-migrated duplicate of the streak computation, missing the exact protection `streakScheduledDatesFor()` was built to add.** When the streak-caching migration landed (see the "Cache streak" entry in Future Considerations), `streak.js`, `pomodoro-leaderboard.js`, and `team-students.js` were all switched to read the cached `students.current_streak` column — `tracker-data.js` (the page-load batch endpoint, called on every fresh visit) was simply missed, and kept its own separate, hand-rolled streak computation that walked `schedule_tasks`/`task_progress` directly, exactly the pre-fix logic `streakScheduledDatesFor()` exists to guard against. **Confirmed live and currently wrong, not just theoretically stale**: queried both computations directly against the top 20 real students by streak — 8 of them (every one of the six highest, all 51-52-day streaks) showed a number **2 lower** from `tracker-data.js`'s own computation than the correct, cached value — the exact shape of the already-documented Aug 29/30 schedule-reload bug, just still live in this one un-migrated path. In practice this meant: every fresh page load showed a wrong (lower) streak for these students, which then silently "corrected" itself the moment they ticked any task (since that refresh hits the now-fixed `streak.js`) — a confusing flicker with no error, easy to miss unless someone was watching closely. Fixed by replacing `fetchStreak()`'s whole hand-rolled computation with the same single cached-column read `streak.js` already does. Verified directly: re-queried `tracker-data.js` for the same real student (Baction/`ayushmittal629@gmail.com`) after the fix — now returns `52`, matching the cached value exactly, where it returned `50` before. No `CLIENT_VERSION` bump — same reasoning as the original streak-caching migration: server-only computation fix, response shape (`{streak: N}`) unchanged.
  - **Redirected back to dipta's specific missing session ("iwas talking about dipta's missing session") found the actual, simple, likely explanation: `recordPomodoroCompletion()` (`progress.js`) had zero retries on the one request that actually determines whether a finished session counts at all.** A real asymmetry, sitting right next to the exact comment that should have flagged it: `savePomoActiveRemote()` (the routine Start/Pause/Reset/Skip state sync, comparatively low-stakes) already retries up to twice with 800ms backoff, and its own comment explains why — "a dropped request could cost a real student real credit." `recordPomodoroCompletion()` — the actual `/pomodoro-complete` credit call, fired exactly once per finished work phase — had no such protection, a single `.catch()` that silently gives up. A dropped request, a slow Netlify cold start, or any momentary network blip on that one POST loses the credit permanently, with nothing on screen ever indicating it happened: `pomoAdvance()` still runs regardless (session dot fills, break starts, the "Break's over" notification still fires later) since it's sequenced after the credit *attempt* resolves, success or failure alike — exactly matching what was reported and confirmed earlier the same day (a real "Break's over... 5/6 sessions today" notification with zero corresponding `pomo_daily_sessions` row). Fixed by wrapping the same call in the identical `attempt(retriesLeft)` retry pattern `savePomoActiveRemote()` already uses — retries blindly on any failure rather than distinguishing a genuine server rejection from a transient one, since `api()` doesn't preserve HTTP status codes to tell them apart and `savePomoActiveRemote()` already accepts that same tradeoff (a wasted retry against a genuine rejection is harmless, just a few hundred ms slower to give up). **Verified with a real, targeted test, not just reasoned about**: used CDP's `Fetch` domain to intercept and forcibly fail the *first* real `/api/pomodoro-complete` request from a live disposable test student (simulating exactly the dropped-request scenario this fixes), confirmed a second request followed ~800ms later and landed successfully — `pomo_daily_sessions` correctly showed the session credited, where before this fix that exact same dropped first request would have been the whole story, permanently. **This doesn't retroactively explain or recover dipta's specific already-lost morning session** — that failure (whatever caused it) already happened on the currently-deployed, pre-fix production code, and left no trace to diagnose after the fact (see the credit-failure-logging entry above). It does mean the same class of failure is now far less likely to recur, for her or anyone else, once deployed. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped — a real `progress.js` behavior change.
  - **A second, different real student (Puneet) reported the same broad symptom — "normal sessions aren't registering" — 2026-09-22, leading to a genuinely different root cause and a real structural fix, not a repeat of the retry fix above.** Investigated by pulling the exact `session_snapshot` PostgREST stored inside his `pomodoro_credit_failures` rows at the moment of rejection, not just the failure reason: at the moment his real 10-minute session's completion was rejected (`phase_end_mismatch`), the server's `pomo_active_session` row showed a completely different, unrelated **5-minute paused phase that had started 37 minutes earlier** — not his real, just-finished session at all. His actual phase had genuinely run to completion, but by the time its completion call landed, something else had already overwritten the only server-side record that call needed to verify against. **Root cause**: `pomo_active_session` is one shared row per email by design (for cross-device sync), but nothing ever protected an actively-running, not-yet-expired phase from being silently clobbered by a *different*, stale tab/device re-syncing its own old idle state — exactly matching the earlier "two tabs" pattern already found for dipta, just via a different mechanism (an unrelated stale sync overwriting the row, not a same-account request-ordering race). **Fixed with real device/tab ownership, not a race-avoidance trick**: `getPomoDeviceToken()` (`progress.js`) generates a random per-tab identity into `sessionStorage` — deliberately `sessionStorage`, not `localStorage`, so it's distinct across tabs/devices but stable across reloads of the *same* tab (a legitimate reload mid-session must never look like a different device taking over) — sent as `deviceToken` on every `/pomo-active` call. `pomo-active.js` stamps this as `owner_token` (new `pomo_active_session` column) whenever a genuinely new phase starts, and refuses to let a write from a *different* token overwrite an existing phase that's still `running` and hasn't hit its own `phase_end_at` yet — the owning device keeps priority until its session naturally completes, expires, or is changed by that same device. A same-device write (matching token, or no token recorded at all — e.g. pre-migration) is never blocked. **Deliberately narrow in scope**: this only protects a session that's still genuinely active and unexpired — a student closing a tab (which already forces `running:false` via the existing `sendPomoStoppedBeacon` mechanism) and opening a fresh one later is correctly *not* treated as a conflict, since there's nothing left to protect by then. **Fault-tolerance discipline applied to both new queries this needed** (the same lesson already learned twice this session — all-time-minutes, malpractice-ack): the ownership check is its own separate, try/caught query, never folded into the existing `mode/total_seconds/phase_started_at` select that Start/Pause/Skip/Reset already depends on for every single call; and the upsert itself attempts to write `owner_token` first, catching a pre-migration "column does not exist" error and silently retrying without it, rather than ever failing the routine sync outright. Verified end-to-end against a real disposable test student, simulating the exact failure precisely: (1) "Device A" starts a genuine 10-minute session; (2) "Device B" (a different token) immediately tries to sync an unrelated 5-minute paused state, matching Puneet's real scenario exactly — server correctly responds `{ignored: 'another device owns the active session'}`, and a fresh read confirms Device A's real session (`mode: work, totalSeconds: 600`) is completely untouched; (3) Device A's own completion attempt still runs the normal elapsed-time check afterward (correctly rejected in this test only because real time wasn't actually waited out, proving the anti-cheat verification itself is untouched by this fix, not bypassed); (4) a separate same-device write (Device A pausing its own session) confirmed to still succeed normally, proving the protection doesn't block legitimate single-device usage. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped.
- **The whole page now auto-refreshes once a day at local midnight** ("can this card refresh everyday at 12am?" about the exam-countdown chip specifically, broadened on request to "maybe have a harmless refresh of the whole page at 12am everyday... so that the sign in button day tracker also gets refreshed" — the Day X/180 program-day badge is the other visibly date-dependent element, shown outside Focus Mode). Every date-derived display on this page (`realTodayIso()`/`todayIso()`-based — the exam countdown, the Day X/180 tracker, anything else computed from "today") was only ever recomputed when something happened to trigger a re-render — a tab left open overnight (this page's own docs already note "students use this page 12-14 hours daily") would silently keep showing yesterday's numbers until the student manually refreshed. Rather than hand-patching each individual date-dependent element with its own targeted DOM update (fragile — easy to miss one, and two mechanisms to keep in sync forever after), `scheduleMidnightReload()` (`progress.js`, called once from `init()`) schedules a plain `location.reload()` for 5 seconds past the next local midnight — one page-wide refresh covers every such element at once, present or future, with zero per-element code. **Deliberately reuses the exact same reload mechanism `checkClientVersion()` already uses a few lines above it**, including its settings-panel-open guard (don't yank an unsaved duration edit out from under someone mid-edit — retries in 60s instead of skipping a full day) — a scheduled reload is exactly as safe as that already-proven one: Focus Mode and an active/paused Pomodoro session both already restore correctly across a real `location.reload()` (see `wasHardReload`/`restorePomoActiveState` above), so this needed no new restore logic, only the trigger. Self-perpetuating with no explicit "reschedule after firing" needed on the success path — a reload re-runs `init()` from scratch, which calls `scheduleMidnightReload()` again, computing a fresh delay to the *next* midnight from whatever the current time is. Verified by intercepting `window.setTimeout` (via `Page.addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument`, before `progress.js` ever runs) and loading the real page: the actual scheduled delay (24,174,955ms) matched an independently-computed "ms until next local midnight" (24,172,257ms, the ~3s gap being real elapsed time during page load) almost exactly, and the captured callback's source text was unambiguously this new `fire` function — confirmed the real code path actually runs with the right timing, not just that the math looks right in isolation. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped — a real `progress.js` behavior change, and picked up automatically by any already-open tab via the very mechanism this feature reuses.
- **Students can now pick their own Pomodoro ring/time-text color, same day** ("give people option within the settings pane to choose the gradient of their pomodoro, keep five gradients"). Five presets (`POMO_GRADIENTS`, `progress.js`) — Sunset (the site's original pink→purple→blue brand gradient, kept as index 0/default so anyone's already-saved settings blob with no `gradient` field yet renders exactly as before), Ocean, Citrus, Mint, Berry — each a 3-stop gradient plus its own fitting glow-shadow color (an amber glow for Citrus, not the original purple one left over from Sunset, so a picked gradient never looks like it's glowing the wrong color). **Mechanism**: `.pomodoro-time`'s CSS and the ring's SVG `<linearGradient>` `<stop>`s (switched from hardcoded `stop-color="#hex"` attributes to `style="stop-color:var(--pomo-g1)"` etc. — a real, broadly-supported CSS/SVG interop feature, not a hack) both now read from `--pomo-g1`/`--pomo-g2`/`--pomo-g3`/`--pomo-glow` custom properties set on `#pomo-card`, so picking a swatch only ever needs one `applyPomoGradient()` call (four `style.setProperty` writes) to update both the ring and the countdown number in lockstep — no re-render, so a phase already in progress (`pomo.running`/`secondsLeft`/etc.) is completely untouched by a cosmetic pick mid-session. Persisted in the existing `taai_pomo_settings` localStorage blob (same per-device, no-backend pattern as the duration/cycle settings already there) as a `gradient` index (0-4), validated the same way (`clampPomoGradient`, falls back to 0 for anything out of range or garbage). Break's own gradient (green/cyan) is completely untouched by any of this regardless of what's picked for work — stays a fixed, separate color path, so a break phase never gets confused for a work phase just because they happen to share a hue. Swatches themselves need no separate color-swap CSS — each `<button>`'s own inline `style` is just that preset's gradient as a solid preview, with only a `.selected` ring-border state needing real CSS. Verified end-to-end against a real (then-deleted) disposable test student: picking a swatch instantly recolors both the live ring and countdown text (screenshotted before/after selecting Citrus); the pick survives a genuine page reload (checked the real `localStorage` value directly, then reloaded and re-entered Focus Mode fresh, confirming `--pomo-g2`'s computed value matched the picked preset's real hex, not a stale default).
- **The gradient picker above got a same-day follow-up round, once real screenshots surfaced a rendering bug and the theming was extended past just the ring/time text** ("the colours look square inside a circle" / "colour the buttons according to the colour... i mean start reset skip" / "so also the save changes button but with visible text inside"). **Real bug, not a style nitpick**: each `.pomo-gradient-swatch` is a plain `<button>`, and Chrome/Safari's default button chrome (a faint rectangular highlight/shadow) survives underneath `border-radius:50%`'s outer clip — the circle's outline was clean but its own native background chrome still painted a squarish highlight inside it. Fixed with `appearance:none`/`-webkit-appearance:none`/`-moz-appearance:none`/`outline:none` on the swatch — confirmed via a real zoomed screenshot before/after that the artifact is gone, each swatch is now a flat, true circle of its own gradient. **Then extended the same `--pomo-g1`/`--pomo-g2`/`--pomo-g3`/`--pomo-glow` custom properties (already set on `#pomo-card` by `applyPomoGradient`) to the Start/Save-changes button (`.pomo-btn-primary`, both share the class) and, more subtly, Reset/Skip (`.pomo-btn-secondary`)** — `.pomo-btn-primary`'s `background`/`box-shadow` switched from the old hardcoded `#FF7FB7`/`var(--purple)`/`#4D8BFF`/`rgba(139,92,246,...)` values to the same properties **with those exact values kept as the CSS fallback**, so the Sunset preset (index 0, the default) renders byte-identical to the original design — confirmed via `getComputedStyle` showing `--pomo-g1`/`--pomo-g2` resolve to `#FF7FB7`/`#A78BFA` at default, matching the old hardcoded gradient exactly, and via screenshot that Start/Save look unchanged from before this round. `.pomo-btn-secondary` (Reset/Skip) deliberately keeps its original neutral background/border at every preset — only `.pomo-btn-icon`'s color inside it picks up `var(--pomo-g2)` — a lighter touch than a full recolor, since these are secondary actions and a full gradient fill on all three buttons read as too busy; this does mean the Reset/Skip icon shifts from flat gray to a soft tint of the chosen color even at the Sunset default (Sunset's `--pomo-g2` is `#A78BFA`, i.e. `var(--purple)` itself, so this reads as "the site's existing accent purple," not a foreign color). Verified white button text stays legible against all 5 presets, including the lightest (Mint, green→cyan) — screenshotted directly rather than assumed, since a couple of the presets (Citrus, Mint) are noticeably lighter than the original Sunset/Ocean/Berry tones. Save changes needed no separate CSS at all — it already shares `.pomo-btn-primary`, so it re-themed for free.
- **A third same-day round extended the theming to the exam-countdown chip's digits and the break phase's own colors, and caught a real regression along the way** ("have you made sure the complementary colours for the break period?" / "also the focus and break texts should also be taken into account" / "make the colour of the digits according to the theme as well"). **The countdown chip's number and icon now read `--pomo-g1/g2/g3` too** (`.exam-countdown-num`'s background and the icon's SVG `<linearGradient>` stops, both switched from hardcoded hex to `var(--pomo-g1,#FF7FB7)` etc. — same fallback-equals-original-value pattern as the buttons) — but this chip sits *outside* `.focus-card` as a sibling (see the exam-countdown-chip-separation entry above), not a descendant of `#pomo-card`, so it can't see vars scoped only to that element. `applyPomoGradient()` was widened to also write onto `#app` (the shared ancestor of both), and `renderCalendar()` now calls it once right after `app.innerHTML = html` whenever `state.focus` is true, so the chip picks up a saved non-default preset correctly from the very first paint of Focus Mode, not just after a swatch click. **Break now gets its own per-preset color pairing instead of one fixed green/cyan for all five** — each `POMO_GRADIENTS` entry gained `breakColors`/`breakGlow`. Sunset/Citrus/Berry's own *work* colors are warm-to-purple, so the original green→cyan break already reads as a clear Focus→Break shift and was left alone; Ocean and Mint's work colors are themselves cool blues/greens, so pairing them with the same cool green/cyan break would sit right next to the work gradient on the color wheel and barely register as a change — those two get a warm break pairing instead (amber→rose for Ocean, orange→pink for Mint). Threaded through the ring's `#pomo-ring-gradient-break` SVG stops, `.pomodoro-card.on-break .pomo-ring-progress`'s glow, and `.pomodoro-card.on-break .pomodoro-time`'s text gradient — all switched from hardcoded `#4ade80`/`#22d3ee` to `var(--pomo-break-g1)`/`var(--pomo-break-g2)` with those same original hex values kept as the fallback. **The FOCUS/BREAK label itself (`.pomodoro-mode`) also now themes** — `color: var(--pomo-g2, var(--purple))` for Focus, `var(--pomo-break-g1, var(--green))` on `.on-break` — previously a flat `var(--purple)`/`var(--green)` regardless of the picked preset. **A real regression, caught by screenshot rather than assumed correct**: widening `applyPomoGradient()` to target `#app` instead of `#pomo-card` initially *replaced* the write to `#pomo-card` rather than adding to it — since a descendant's own local custom property always wins over an ancestor's value in the CSS cascade, `#pomo-card`'s inline style (written once at render time by `renderPomodoro()`, still holding whatever preset was active at that render) kept silently shadowing every live update from `#app` afterward. Picking Ocean and screenshotting the Save-changes button showed it still rendering Sunset's pink→blue, not Ocean's cyan→blue — confirmed the bug, not a false alarm. Fixed by having `applyPomoGradient()` write the same values to *both* `#app` and `#pomo-card` on every call, re-verified with the same Ocean pick afterward showing the correct cyan→blue button and, deeper in the same test, a forced Skip into Break showing the amber→rose break pairing on the ring/label/time-text simultaneously with the still-correct cyan/blue countdown chip above it. Verified end-to-end against a real (then-deleted) disposable test student for the Ocean preset specifically (the one most likely to expose a break/work color clash): screenshotted the countdown chip, the ring mid-Focus, and the ring/label/time-text after a forced Skip into Break, all in the same session.
- **Two real persistence bugs found and fixed the same day** ("it's still square inside circle colours" turned out to be a red herring investigation-wise, but "whenever i set a new colour and save and then refresh the page it comes back to the original colour" was a real, separate bug). `applyPomoSettings()` (the settings panel's Save changes handler) rebuilt `pomoSettings` from only its own four duration fields — `pomoSettings = { work, shortBreak, longBreak, cycle }` — silently dropping `gradient` from the object entirely; the very next `savePomoSettings()` call then wrote that incomplete blob to localStorage, so `loadPomoSettings()` read back `gradient: undefined` on the next page load and fell back to the default (Sunset). A second, actually-more-frequent copy of the identical bug lived in `loadMonth`'s cross-device settings merge (the block that overlays a signed-in student's server-saved durations onto whatever localStorage/defaults loaded at module-init) — same "rebuild the object from a fixed field list, forget gradient" pattern, but this one runs on *every single page load* for any signed-in student who has ever saved duration settings, not just after clicking Save. Both fixed by explicitly carrying `gradient: pomoSettings.gradient` into the rebuilt object — gradient itself was never sent to update `saveRemotePomoSettings()`/cross-device sync deliberately stays out of scope there.

- **The swatch's square-inside-a-circle report turned out to be a genuine Safari-only bug, not a stale-cache or already-fixed misunderstanding** — confirmed via direct follow-up questions that the report was from a hard-refreshed `localhost:8080` (ruling out both caching and the separately-discovered stale-production-deploy issue) specifically in Safari. The earlier `appearance:none` fix had already measured clean in headless Chrome (zoomed screenshot, computed-style checks), so this was a second, WebKit-specific version of the same underlying problem: native `<button>` control rendering surviving under a `border-radius:50%` clip. Real Safari couldn't be automated in this environment to pin the exact WebKit quirk (`do JavaScript` via AppleScript is gated behind a Safari Developer-settings toggle this session isn't authorized to flip unprompted), so rather than guess at a third increasingly-specific CSS override, the swatch was rebuilt as a plain `<span role="button" tabindex="0">` instead of a real `<button>` (`pomoGradientSwatchesHtml()`) — sidestepping native control rendering entirely, in any browser, rather than chasing another per-engine quirk. `bindCalendarEvents()`'s click handler was refactored into a shared `selectPomoGradientSwatch()` function, called from both a `click` listener (unchanged behavior) and a new `keydown` listener handling Enter/Space (a real `<button>` gets that activation for free from the browser; a `<span>` needs it wired by hand to stay keyboard-accessible) — verified via a real CDP-driven `.focus()` + synthetic Enter keydown correctly selecting a swatch. **Swapping to `<span>` surfaced its own real collision, caught before shipping**: `.pomo-setting-row span` (a pre-existing rule styling the stepper rows' "min" unit label spans) is a descendant selector that, at (0,1,1) specificity, actually *outranks* a bare `.pomo-gradient-swatch` class selector at (0,1,0) — meaning the swatch's own `width: 22px` would have silently lost to that rule's `width: 26px`, distorting every swatch into a 26×22px oval the instant the tag changed from `button` to `span` (this rule simply never matched the old `<button>`-tag swatches at all, so the collision didn't exist before this change). Fixed by rescoping the swatch rules to `.pomo-gradient-swatches .pomo-gradient-swatch` (two classes, unambiguously higher specificity) — confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` showing all 5 swatches genuinely 22×22px after the fix, not just visually plausible in a screenshot. A `:focus-visible` outline was added in place of the old `outline:none` (no longer appropriate once native `<button>` semantics, and their focus handling, are gone) so keyboard users still get a visible indicator.
- **The Ring color row moved to be the first field in the settings panel** ("also this colour should be the first option in settings" — clarified on request to mean the row's position, not a specific swatch's position within it) — was the second-to-last row, after all four duration fields; `renderPomodoro()`'s settings-panel markup now calls `pomoGradientSwatchesHtml()` immediately after the panel header, before `pomoStepperRowHtml('Focus', ...)`. Pure markup reorder, no CSS depends on sibling position here — confirmed via a real settings-panel screenshot showing Ring color first.
- **"still the same" after the `<span>` rebuild turned out to be a real, self-inflicted regression from that exact fix, not a stale test** — the previous swatch CSS had a blanket `outline:none`; rebuilding the swatch as a `role="button"` span (to sidestep native `<button>` chrome) added real keyboard focus via `tabindex="0"`, and the replacement CSS only handled `:focus-visible`, leaving plain `:focus` — exactly what a mouse click produces in Safari, unlike Chrome — with no override at all. Safari's native focus ring is a well-known quirk on small round elements (doesn't shrink/curve to match a tight `border-radius`), so it rendered as a squarish halo around the circle the instant a swatch was clicked, which is indistinguishable from "still square" without knowing to look for it. Fixed by restoring `outline: none` on the base `.pomo-gradient-swatches .pomo-gradient-swatch` rule, keeping the `:focus-visible` override for real keyboard nav. Not independently re-verified in real Safari (same automation limitation as the original bug — `do JavaScript` via AppleScript needs a Developer setting this session can't flip unprompted); flagged to the user to confirm on their end rather than claimed fixed outright.
- **The "square inside a circle" saga's real resolution: after three rounds chasing native-control-rendering bugs (Chrome button chrome, a Safari-specific version of the same, then a Safari focus-ring gap), a direct follow-up question revealed it was never a rendering bug at all.** "The colours should be just whole colour balls and there should be no colour shape inside" — each swatch's `135deg` 3-stop **linear** gradient shows a visible diagonal band/seam at 22px, which reads as a distinct shape sitting inside the circle rather than a smooth blend, not literally "square" in the geometric sense the earlier fixes were solving for. Fixed by switching the swatch preview specifically (not the ring/buttons/text elsewhere, never part of this complaint) to a **radial** gradient centered near the top-left (`radial-gradient(circle at 35% 30%, g1, g2, g3)`) — rotationally smooth with no seam, reading as a glossy solid orb while still previewing the same 3 preset colors. Verified with a real zoomed screenshot (captured full-viewport via CDP, then cropped/upscaled locally with ImageMagick — CDP's own `clip.scale` above 1 was returning blank captures for this crop for reasons not chased down further, so this sidesteps that rather than fighting it) showing all 5 swatches as clean, seamless glossy balls.
- **The radial-gradient fix immediately above was itself still wrong** — a follow-up zoomed screenshot of one swatch showed the radial's own bright center reads as its own distinct highlight/shape inside the ball, a clearer version of the same complaint, not a fix for it ("see there is a square inside the circle — i don't want that"). Any gradient at all risks a visible internal region at 22px. Fixed for real this time with a genuinely flat solid fill — `background: g.colors[1]` (each preset's middle/signature color, the same hue already used as that preset's "headline" stop everywhere else on the page), no gradient function at all, so there is categorically nothing that could read as a shape inside the ball. Re-verified with the same zoomed-screenshot technique — all 5 swatches now render as plain, uniform, single-color circles.
- **A full end-to-end verification pass ("verify everything before i ship") caught a real, more-reliably-reproducible sibling of the earlier pause+refresh bug, in the settings-duration-sync path rather than the beacon path.** A 44-check scripted run against a real disposable test student (swatch geometry/colors, all 5 presets' live theming, keyboard activation, gradient persistence across Save/reload, Start/Pause/Reset/Skip, break colors, a real reload of a paused session) initially reported one failure: pause a running timer → reload → displayed time snapped back to full duration, even though `localStorage`'s `taai_pomo_active` still correctly held the real paused `secondsLeft`. Isolating step-by-step traced it to `loadMonth`'s cross-device settings-sync block (`data.pomoSettings`, the same block the gradient-drop fix earlier this session already touched) — it runs on **every** `loadMonth()` call, i.e. every page load, for any signed-in student who has ever saved duration settings from any device, and its `if (!pomo.running) { pomo.totalSeconds = pomoDurationFor(pomo.mode); pomo.secondsLeft = pomo.totalSeconds; }` guard doesn't distinguish "genuinely idle, safe to recompute for a new duration" from "paused 5 minutes into a real 25-minute session" — both are `!pomo.running`. So it unconditionally overwrote whatever `restorePomoActiveState()` had *correctly* restored moments earlier in the very same `init()` call, on **every single reload**, not just an unlucky fast-refresh race like the earlier beacon bug. The **exact same pattern** existed in the local Save-changes handler (`applyPomoSettings()`) too — clicking Save while paused mid-session (even to change an unrelated field like Short break) silently snapped the paused Focus countdown back to full duration immediately, no reload needed. Both fixed with the same guard: `pomo.secondsLeft === pomo.totalSeconds` (a paused session that's actually consumed any time never has those equal) added alongside the existing `!pomo.running` check, so the reset only ever fires for a timer that's genuinely fresh/idle or just Reset. Re-verified with the exact isolated repro that first caught it (idle Save → reload → Start → wait 3s → Pause → reload) now correctly preserving `24:57` through the second reload, then reran the full 44-check suite clean (2 apparent mobile-viewport "failures" were the test's own wrong assumption, not a bug — Focus Mode's existing, documented `resize`-triggered auto-exit below 1080px correctly kicked a 390px-wide session back to the checklist, confirmed by reading that listener directly rather than assumed).
- **"Does every focus colour have its contrasting opposite break colour" — a direct follow-up question that turned out to have a real, measurable "no."** Checked properly rather than eyeballed: computed the actual HSL hue distance between every work color and every break color for all 5 presets (0°=identical hue, 180°=true opposite). Sunset — the *default* preset — was the weakest by a wide margin: its blue endpoint (`#4D8BFF`, hue ≈219°) sits only **31° from the original break cyan** (`#22D3EE`, hue ≈188°), nearly the same hue, where every other preset's worst case was 70°+ (Berry 70°, Citrus 99°, Ocean 113°, Mint 115°). Confirmed with the user before changing it, since Sunset had been deliberately protected as byte-identical to the site's original historical design throughout this whole session — this is the first intentional deviation from that. Fixed with a gold→green pairing (`#FDE047`→`#4ADE80`, ~77° from Sunset's nearest work hue, on par with Berry) — keeps the same green family the unchanged presets (Citrus/Berry) still use, and reads as a fitting "sunset glow fading into green" rather than an arbitrary swap. Sunset's *Focus* colors are completely untouched — verified via `getComputedStyle` showing `--pomo-g1/g2/g3` still exactly `#FF7FB7`/`#A78BFA`/`#4D8BFF`, and a real screenshot of the new Break state showing the gold-to-green ring/label clearly distinct from Focus's pink-purple-blue.
- **"There's too many mentions of Mission IIT leaderboard, fix the names to what they represent."** All 3 leaderboard cards on this page — Today's top 10 (`renderTodayLeaders`), the current week's top 20 (`renderLeaderboardCard`), and last week's top 5 (`renderLastWeekChampions`) — literally shared the identical generic title "Mission IIT Leaderboard", even though each already had a distinct, correct subtitle describing what it actually shows. Renamed each `.leaderboard-title` to match its own subtitle: "Today's Leaders", "Weekly Leaderboard", "Last Week's Champions". The page's own `<h1>` branding ("TAAI BATCH C - MISSION IIT", both the guest registration screen's and the main checklist's) was deliberately left alone — that's the cohort/batch name, not a leaderboard label, and wasn't part of this complaint. Verified against a real disposable test student: checklist page shows "Last Week's Champions", Focus Mode shows "Today's Leaders" and "Weekly Leaderboard" — three distinct titles, not one repeated string.
- **"In the last week's champions don't do the name dimming of people who aren't active"** — the greyed-out-non-live-name CSS rule (`.leaderboard-row:not(.leaderboard-row--live)`) had been deliberately extended to `#champions-card` earlier this session once `fetchLastWeekLeaders` started returning `is_live` (previously excluded only because the class could never exist there yet — see that entry above). Reverted at direct request: `#champions-card` is a retrospective "who did well last week" ranking, and dimming someone's name just because they're not mid-session at this exact moment doesn't make sense there the way it does for the two boards that are actually about "right now" (today's leaders, the current week) — being live has nothing to do with how well someone already did last week. Removed `#champions-card` from all 4 rule groups (dim-name/dim-time/live-name/live-time); its rows now fall back to `.leaderboard-name`/`.leaderboard-time`'s own plain default colors regardless of live status. Verified against real production data (not synthetic): a genuine mix of live and non-live students on the actual last-week board, all 5 rows confirmed rendering the exact same computed color.
- **Guests can now freely enter and look around Focus Mode — the timer, both leaderboards, settings — with the sign-up gate moved from "entering" to "clicking Start"** ("let him see the pomodoro page and whenever he tries to start a session, ask him to login"). This is the exact same "let them look, ask only when they act" pattern the page already used for a guest ticking a task off on the main checklist (`onTaskToggle`) — extending it here rather than inventing a new one. `goToFocusMode()`'s old `!state.student` branch (which redirected straight to registration before a guest ever saw Focus Mode at all) is gone; entering now behaves identically for guest and student. The gate moved into `pomoToggleRun()`'s starting path instead — a guest clicking Start gets the registration form (reusing the existing `pendingTask`-style pending-action replay machinery, renamed `pendingFocusMode` → `pendingPomoStart` since its old meaning, gating *entry*, no longer exists) rather than the timer actually starting; Reset/Skip stay ungated, since neither creates anything worth losing for a guest. **Deliberately does NOT auto-replay the Start click after a successful registration**, unlike the existing task-completion replay: `pomoToggleRun()`'s notification-permission request (`Notification.requestPermission()`, for a first-time guest whose permission is still `'default'`) needs a genuine synchronous user gesture to reliably show the browser's real prompt, and the registration form's success handler is a `fetch().then()` callback running after a real network round-trip — not guaranteed to still count as one. Silently failing to prompt would leave a first-time guest stuck with no visible feedback and no idea why nothing happened. Instead, a newly-registered guest lands back on an idle, now-logged-in Focus Mode screen and clicks Start once more themselves — one extra click, but the permission flow works correctly every time instead of intermittently. That re-render uses `renderCalendar()`, not `enterFocus()` — `pendingPomoStart` can only ever be set from inside Focus Mode in the first place (the Start button that sets it only exists there), so `state.focus` is already `true` by the time registration succeeds, and calling `enterFocus()` again would push a second, unwanted `history` entry on top of the one already there (confirmed this doesn't happen: a real end-to-end test pressed Back exactly once after a guest→register→Start flow and correctly exited Focus Mode in a single press, not two). Verified end-to-end via a real disposable test student, 13 checks: a genuine guest (no cookie at all) enters Focus Mode directly with no gate, sees the Start button, clicking it shows registration with a session-specific prompt ("Sign up to start your Focus session"), the "keep browsing without an account" cancel link returns to an untouched idle timer, and registering through the gate correctly lands back in Focus Mode (still idle) with a real cookie set — followed by a genuine follow-up Start click actually starting the timer.
- **A real, separate bug found the same round: "if someone presses refresh on a paused timer it resets it back."** Two clean Chrome tests (a normal pause-then-reload, and a zero-delay reload racing the async save) both showed correct restoration, which pointed at a genuine race rather than a broken restore path — confirmed by reading `pomoToggleRun`'s Pause branch: it calls `savePomoActiveState()`, whose remote half (`savePomoActiveRemote`) is a plain `fetch()` with no `keepalive`, unlike `sendPomoStoppedBeacon` (`pagehide` + `navigator.sendBeacon`) which the codebase had *already* built for the parallel "tab closed while running" case specifically because a plain `fetch()` can be silently cancelled by the browser mid-navigation. A refresh a few seconds after Pause can cancel that in-flight POST before it reaches the server, leaving `pomo_active_session` exactly as it was before the pause (e.g. still `running:true` from Start) — the *next* load's cross-device reconciliation then has nothing wrong to protect against on its own terms (it correctly compares timestamps and prefers newer local state), because "newer" doesn't help when the server was simply never told about the pause at all. Fixed by widening `sendPomoStoppedBeacon`'s guard from `!pomo.running` (fired only while genuinely running) to fire on *every* teardown, always forcing `running:false` — covering the already-paused case too, as a guaranteed-delivery resend of state a plain `fetch()` might have lost. **A real regression this could have introduced, caught before shipping, not after**: firing unconditionally would also fire for a completely fresh device that's never touched the Pomodoro locally (`pomo` still at its untouched module-load defaults) if navigated away from in the narrow window before `loadMonth`'s cross-device reconciliation resolves — wrongly beaconing "not running, full duration" and clobbering a session that's genuinely running on a *different* device. Fixed with a new `pomoStateKnown` flag, set inside `savePomoActiveLocalOnly()` (the one choke point both real local mutations — Start/Pause/Reset/Skip — and the restore/reconcile path funnel through), gating the beacon so it only ever fires once `pomo` demonstrably holds real state. Verified both halves directly against the real server row (not just the displayed UI): (1) start → run 5s → pause → reload with zero delay → `pomo_active_session` correctly shows `running:false` with the right `seconds_left`, matching the displayed time after reload; (2) seeded a fake `running:true` row (simulating a real session on another device) → loaded the tracker fresh on a browser that had never touched this student's Pomodoro locally → navigated away 50ms later, well before reconciliation could resolve → the seeded running row survived untouched. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped — a real `progress.js` behavior change.

- **The "days till GATE" chip's calendar icon was enlarged and given a real loop animation + brand-gradient stroke the same day** — was a static 14px `currentColor` (flat `var(--purple)`) icon in a 32px flat-purple-tinted badge. Badge grew to 40px (`.exam-countdown-icon`, `border-radius` 9px→11px) with the tint switched from a flat `rgba(139,92,246,0.14)` to a soft 3-stop gradient tint (`rgba` versions of the same pink/purple/blue used elsewhere on the page) so the badge itself reads as part of the same brand-gradient family, not just a generic purple chip. The icon SVG itself grew 14px→19px and switched from `stroke="currentColor"` to `stroke="url(#exam-icon-grad)"`, a real inline `<linearGradient>` matching `.exam-countdown-num`'s own gradient stops exactly (`#FF7FB7` → `#A78BFA` → `#4D8BFF` — `#A78BFA` used as a literal hex here rather than `var(--purple)`, since SVG presentation attributes don't reliably resolve CSS custom properties the way the `style="stop-color:var(...)"` trick used for the Pomodoro ring's gradient does; confirmed `--purple`'s actual value first so the literal stays byte-identical). The looping animation (`@keyframes exam-icon-wiggle`, 2.6s ease-in-out infinite — a rotate+scale wobble, not a plain spin) is applied to the `<svg>` itself, not the badge box around it — animating the badge would have distorted its rounded-square shape on every cycle, where the icon alone has room to wobble inside a stable frame. Verified via a zoomed screenshot mid-animation (caught the icon visibly rotated) and by reading the computed `animationName` directly rather than assuming the CSS applied.
- **The Pomodoro settings pane was substantially reworked the same day** ("enhance the pomodoro settings pane make it better"). Was a bare dashed-line divider (`border-top: 1px dashed`) over a plain list of small `<input type="number">` fields with no visual identity of its own. Now: (1) a real header ("⚙️ Timer settings"); (2) the panel itself is a distinct recessed card (`background: var(--bg-soft)` — one shade off the surrounding `.pomodoro-card`'s own `var(--card)` — plus a border and rounded corners, reading as "a panel sitting inside the card" without needing a shadow); (3) each of the four fields (Focus/Short break/Long break/Sessions-per-long-break) gained a real stepper control — a `−`/`+` button pair either side of the number, all three sharing one pill-shaped bordered container (`.pomo-stepper`) — instead of a bare field relying on someone noticing it was typable or trying the keyboard arrows; (4) the two test buttons (sound/notification) moved from stacked full-width blocks to a side-by-side row; (5) Save went from a small centered pill to a full-width primary button, matching how big a "confirm these settings" action should read. `pomoStepperRowHtml()` (new, `progress.js`) factors out the one bit of markup repeated across all four rows. **The stepper buttons dispatch a real `input` event after setting `.value`, not just set it directly** — needed so the Focus field's existing real-time 120-minute-cap alert (bound to that field's own `'input'` listener) still fires correctly when the limit is approached via the stepper, not only from typing; in practice the stepper's own min/max clamp (read straight off the input's own `min`/`max` attributes) stops it at 120 before that alert would ever need to fire, which is arguably better UX than needing the alert message at all for this specific path. Verified directly: incrementing/decrementing the Focus stepper moved the value correctly (25→26, then three decrements to 23), and repeatedly clicking the Sessions-per-long-break `+` button 20 times correctly clamped at its real max of 12 rather than climbing past it — checked via `Runtime.evaluate`'s `exceptionDetails`, not just eyeballing a screenshot, after an earlier looser check silently missed nothing (a bad element selector, not a real bug — the retest with proper exception-checking confirmed the feature works).
- **The Pomodoro settings gear scrolls its panel into view on open** (`pomo-settings-toggle` click handler, `progress.js`, fixed 2026-09-03) — the panel sits at the very bottom of `.pomodoro-card`, below the ring/dots/controls/notify-tip, so on a short viewport it could open entirely below the fold with nothing visibly changing on screen; a real report ("people who don't scroll are confused where did it come"). `panel.scrollIntoView({ behavior: 'smooth', block: 'nearest' })` fires only when the panel is being shown, not hidden.
- **Focus Mode has a Pomodoro timer** (circular depleting SVG ring, Start/Pause/Reset/Skip, session dots, a synthesized Web Audio chime on phase change). Durations + sessions-per-long-break are user-customizable via a settings panel, persisted in `localStorage` (`taai_pomo_settings`). Entering Focus Mode pushes a History API entry so the Back button exits it without leaving the page. A fresh navigation (typing the URL, clicking "Progress Tracker" in the nav, even to this exact page) always lands on the main checklist — the one exception is a genuine browser reload (F5/Cmd+R) while already in Focus Mode, which restores it, since a student mid-session refreshing isn't "starting fresh" the way a new visit is. Distinguishing "restore" from "land on checklist" needs *two* signals together, not just one: the Navigation Timing API's `entries[0].type === 'reload'` (specific to the actual reload action) decides *whether a reload should restore*, and a small `taai_focus_active` localStorage flag (set/cleared everywhere `state.focus` changes) decides *whether it currently should*. `history.state` alone can't do this — confirmed empirically, not assumed: Chromium can preserve/reuse the current session-history entry's pushState'd data across a fresh navigation to the exact same URL, not just an actual reload, so a stale `{focus:true}` can otherwise resurface later via the Back button from an already-exited session. For the same reason, the `popstate` handler in `progress.js` deliberately ignores `e.state` and just exits Focus Mode unconditionally whenever `state.focus` was true going in, rather than trusting whatever's attached to the entry being popped to.
  - **Max Focus-session length lowered from 180 to 120 minutes (2026-09-06)**, with an explicit requirement that any session already running at the old max keeps running/crediting normally, completely unaffected. This held with essentially no extra work because of two things already true beforehand: (1) an active phase's `pomo.totalSeconds`/`phaseEndAt` are frozen the moment it starts and never re-read from live `pomoSettings` afterward (confirmed by tracing `applyPomoActiveState`, the cross-device sync path, and `applyPomoSettings`'s own comment — "a running timer keeps counting down on the old duration"), and (2) `pomodoro-complete.js` **never trusts the client's claimed `minutes` for credit** — it always recomputes from its own stored `pomo_active_session.total_seconds`, set when that phase actually started. So a 180-minute session already in flight when this shipped is credited the full 180 minutes at completion regardless of what `pomoSettings.work` gets changed to in the meantime. `clampMinutes()`'s own semantics turned out to matter here: it validates-or-falls-back-to-default, it does **not** clamp to a boundary — passing `120` as its own `max` would have silently turned an old `180` into the default `25`, not the requested `120`. Fixed with a new `clampPomoWork()` wrapper: validate against the *old* range (`1-180`, unchanged — "was this ever a value the UI could have produced at all") first, then cap anything above the *new* `POMO_WORK_MAX_MINUTES` (120) down to exactly 120 as a separate step. Verified the two-step logic against 8 cases in isolation (180→120, 121→120, 25→25 unaffected, 200/-5/garbage→still falls back to the default 25 exactly as before) before shipping.
  - **A one-time "we lowered the cap" popup was built, then explicitly cancelled the same day** — it turned out to only ever reach students who'd already granted Focus Mode's (unrelated, pre-existing) notification permission in an earlier session, since the branch handling a brand-new grant called a full `renderCalendar()` right after inserting the notice, silently wiping it out before anyone could read it (a real bug, found investigating why it "didn't show" — see git history on `progress.js` around 2026-09-06 if this needs resurrecting). Rather than keep chasing it, the whole notice — `maybeShowPomoMaxNotice`, its `taai_pomo_max_notice_seen_v1` flag, the `.pomo-max-notice` markup/CSS — was removed outright at explicit request ("cancel this notification idea"). `pomoToggleRun`'s async permission-granted branch still uses `updatePomoDisplay()` rather than the `renderCalendar()` it originally had, independent of the removed notice — it avoids replaying the card's `.fade-in` entrance on a routine first Start click, which is worth keeping on its own merits.
  - **`clampPomoWork()` only ever corrected the in-memory value, never persisted the correction — a real student ("Sakata Gintoki") reported his hours looked miscalculated, 2026-09-16.** He'd saved a 178-minute Focus duration before the 120-minute cap shipped (legal at the time, under the old 1-180 range); `students.pomo_work_min` kept reading 178 from the server forever afterward, since `clampPomoWork()` (called from `loadMonth`'s cross-device settings merge) only ever wrote the clamped `120` into the in-memory `pomoSettings` object, never back to Supabase. Every actual session he ran from 2026-09-07 onward correctly credited 120 minutes (the crediting math itself was never wrong — `pomo_daily_sessions` showed a clean 178.0-min/session history right up to the cap's ship date, then exactly 120.0 from the next day on, zero jitter), but nothing ever told him his setting had changed, so his daily/weekly totals looked short against what he expected for the same number of sessions. **Not unique to his account** — checked directly: only 5 of 333 students total had ever saved a duration above 120 before the cap shipped, and all 5 were silently affected the same way; he was just the one who noticed and said something. Fixed by persisting the corrected value the moment `loadMonth` clamps a loaded value above `POMO_WORK_MAX_MINUTES` — `if (savedPomo.work > POMO_WORK_MAX_MINUTES) saveRemotePomoSettings();` right after the merge, reusing the settings-save path that already exists rather than adding a new endpoint. Self-limiting: this only ever fires once per affected student, since the very next load already sees a server value of 120 and the condition goes false. **Deliberately no in-app notice** — a similar one-time popup for the original cap change was already built and explicitly cancelled earlier in this same section; consistent with that call, this ships as a silent data-correction only, at the user's explicit direction, not a repeat of the cancelled notice idea. Verified against a disposable test student (seeded `pomo_work_min: 175` directly, loaded the tracker page as them via `netlify dev` + a real CDP session, confirmed the server row updated to `120` afterward, then cleaned up every row) rather than testing directly against Gintoki's real account. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped — a real `progress.js` behavior change (an extra settings POST under a specific, previously-silent condition).
  - **The "5 of 333" figure above was wrong, caught by a direct follow-up question ("many were using 180 mins session back then") — it only checked who currently still has a stale value, not who was ever actually affected.** Re-checked properly: paged through all of `pomo_daily_sessions` for any day before 2026-09-06 where a student's minutes/sessions ratio implied a >120-min session — **16 distinct students** genuinely ran sessions that long (11 of them at exactly 180), a real, much larger number than the original claim suggested. Of those 16, 14 had already self-corrected — they'd opened Settings sometime after the cap shipped and manually saved a lower number themselves, which persisted fine through the ordinary save path (no bug in play for them, `clampPomoWork()` never even needed to run since their own save already wrote ≤120). Only 2 were genuinely still stuck on a stale pre-cap value with no fix of their own: Gintoki and one other student (`radhakantpandadeveloper@gmail.com`, still at 180) — both corrected automatically by the fix above the next time either loads the page. **A separate, more fundamental issue surfaced doing this recheck**: 3 more students (`pratapsahil05@gmail.com`, `0xgwish08@gmail.com`, `mahasayrajesh@gmail.com`) currently hold a stored value above 120 despite never having run a session that long *before* the cap — meaning it was written to the server *after* the 120-min cap already existed, which shouldn't have been possible. Root cause: `pomo-settings.js`'s own POST handler still validated `clampMinutes(body.work, 1, 180)` — the 120-minute cap only ever existed on the client (the stepper widget's own `max` attribute plus `clampPomoWork()`); the server itself would still accept and persist anything up to 180 from any caller that sent it directly, with no shared constant enforcing the same limit on both sides. Fixed by changing the server's own clamp to `clampMinutes(body.work, 1, 120)`, matching `POMO_WORK_MAX_MINUTES` — no shared constant exists across the browser/Node boundary, so this needs to be kept in sync by hand if the cap ever changes again, same maintenance burden already accepted elsewhere in this file (e.g. `DEFAULT_BODY_BY_SOURCE`). Verified directly: POSTing `work: 180` for a disposable test student now returns/persists `120`, not `180` — confirmed both in the API response and by re-reading the row from Supabase, then cleaned up. No `CLIENT_VERSION` bump needed — server-only validation change, response shape unchanged. The 3 real students with the leftover stale value aren't manually touched — the existing client-side self-heal from the fix above already corrects each of them automatically on their next page load, same as Gintoki.
  - **A final head-to-toe grep before shipping found one more `180`: `pomodoro-complete.js`'s `MAX_MINUTES_PER_SESSION`**, the ceiling used when actually crediting a completion (`Math.min(MAX_MINUTES_PER_SESSION, Math.round(session.total_seconds / 60))`) — its own comment claimed to match "the Focus settings panel's own max," which was no longer true once the panel's real max became 120. Not a live bug on its own (both fixes above now mean `total_seconds` can't legitimately exceed 120 min for any new session, so this ceiling was already inert in practice) — but it was the last place that would still credit up to 180 if `total_seconds` ever ended up above 120 some other way (corrupted data, a bypass of the other two checks). Tightened to 120 to match, after explicitly checking first whether either of the 2 still-stale students (Gintoki, `radhakantpandadeveloper@gmail.com`) had a genuinely in-flight session right now that this could clip real credit from — confirmed neither did (Gintoki's active row already reads `total_seconds: 7200`/120 min and isn't running; the other student's `running: true` row has a `phase_end_at` from 2026-08-29, nearly 3 weeks stale, not a real in-progress session) — so tightening this immediately was safe, unlike when the original 120-min cap first shipped and this same value was deliberately left at 180 specifically to protect a real in-flight 180-minute session from that exact clipping.
  - **What actually ships instead: real-time feedback in the settings panel only.** Typing anything over 120 into the Focus field snaps it back to 120 immediately and shows a small message ("⏱️ Max session limit is 120 minutes.") right under the field, self-dismissing after 2.5s (`#pomo-work-max-alert`, a plain `input` listener on `#pomo-set-work`). Toggled via a `.visible` CSS class with real `opacity`/`max-height` transitions in both directions, not the `hidden` attribute — `hidden` forces `display:none` instantly with nothing to animate, which is exactly why an earlier version's fade-*out* looked abrupt even though the fade-*in* (via a one-shot keyframe) looked fine. Fires every time someone tries to type past the cap, not gated by localStorage — a live input constraint, not a one-time announcement.
  - `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped for the 120-minute cap itself (a real `progress.js` behavior change) — safe for the same reason as the leaderboard-poll widening: the resulting auto-reload correctly restores an active session's real state from its own saved record, not from `pomoSettings`.
  - **The ring and countdown number got a gradient redesign the same day** (the old flat `var(--purple)`/`var(--green)` stroke and text color read as "stale" — direct feedback) — both now use the site's own established brand gradient (pink → purple → blue, the same recipe `.btn-primary`/`.focus-toggle` already use) for the work phase, a green → cyan gradient for break. The ring's gradient is a real SVG `<linearGradient>` in `<defs>` (inline in `renderPomodoro()`'s markup, referenced via `stroke: url(#pomo-ring-gradient-work|break)` in CSS) plus a soft matching `drop-shadow` glow; the countdown number reuses the page's existing animated-gradient-text treatment (`background-clip: text` + the shared `@keyframes streak-grad` that `.streak-number`/`.program-day-num` already use) rather than a new animation. `renderPomodoro()` only ever renders once per page, so the gradient `id`s can't collide.
  - **Reshaped from a full circle to a semi-circle gauge the same day** ("something more appealing" than the plain ring, at explicit request — chosen from a few shape options, including a squircle ring and a horizontal battery-style bar, that were presented before building anything). The arc is a real SVG `<path>` 180-degree sweep (`M 25 95 A 75 75 0 0 1 175 95`), not a `<circle>` with half its stroke color hidden. `POMO_RING_CIRCUMFERENCE` is now this arc's real path length (`πr`, half a circle's `2πr`) — the same `stroke-dasharray`/`stroke-dashoffset` countdown technique works identically, since SVG measures dash length along the path itself rather than assuming a full circle. The SVG `viewBox` shrank from a 200×200 square to 200×160, and `.pomodoro-ring-wrap` (190×190 → 220×176, keeping the matching `.pomo-today-row` override at 280×224) follows the same 5:4 ratio — a semicircle doesn't need to reserve height for a bottom half it no longer draws. The old full-circle version's `.pomodoro-ring { transform: rotate(-90deg) }` is gone too — that rotation only existed to make a `<circle>`'s naturally-3-o'clock start point read as 12 o'clock; the new arc is authored left-to-right through the top already, so no rotation is needed (confirmed the sweep direction by hand: `sweep-flag=1` from the left point moves clockwise on screen, i.e. up through the top toward the right point, exactly like a clock's 9→12→3).
  - **The mode/time readout's position was corrected from a real screenshot, not guesswork** — first shipped centered in the space below the arc's flat diameter line, which a screenshot showed leaves the entire dome (the space the arc actually encloses) empty, with the text floating below it instead of inside it. `.pomodoro-ring-center` now anchors to the BOTTOM of the dome instead (`bottom: 41%`, matching the diameter line, `justify-content: flex-end` so content packs upward from there) — that's deliberately the bottom of the dome specifically, not vertically centered within it, since the dome narrows toward its peak and the bottom is where there's the most horizontal room for the time text.
  - **Moving the text inside the dome then surfaced a second real bug from the next screenshot: the time digits clipped past the arc's left edge.** The SVG dome scales with `.pomodoro-ring-wrap`'s physical size, but `.pomodoro-time`'s `font-size` is a fixed px value that doesn't — so the fix wasn't "make everything bigger" for its own sake, it was making the *box* grow faster than the *font*: `.pomodoro-ring-wrap` 220×176 → 280×224 (+27%) alongside `.pomodoro-time` only 40px → 44px (+10%, `.pomo-today-row`'s wide-viewport variant 280×224/60px → 340×272/62px the same way) — the gap between those two growth rates is what actually buys back clearance from the arc, not the size increase alone.
  - **Settled into its final split layout at explicit request**: the mode label (FOCUS/BREAK) stays genuinely inside the dome, but the countdown itself moved back below the arc's flat diameter line — into the wide-open area with no dome-narrowing width constraint at all, which sidesteps the clipping problem at its root rather than just giving the text more room to clip less. `.pomodoro-mode-wrap` (inside the dome, `bottom: 41%`) and `.pomodoro-time-wrap` (below it, `top: 59%`) replaced the single shared `.pomodoro-ring-center` that used to stack both — same `#pomo-mode`/`#pomo-time` element IDs underneath, so `updatePomoDisplay()`'s per-tick patching needed no changes at all.
  - **Session dots not resetting after midnight, fixed 2026-09-07** — a real bug, reported directly from a screenshot showing "4/5 sessions" still filled the next day. Root cause: `pomo.completedSessions` is restored via `Math.max(pomo.completedSessions, saved.completedSessions)` in two places (`applyPomoActiveState`'s local-restore and cross-device-sync callers), both deliberately designed so a slightly-stale response can't erase real progress already made *earlier in the same day* — but neither ever checked *which day* `saved` was actually from, so a count saved right before midnight kept winning that merge forever afterward (Math.max can only ratchet a number up, never back down to 0 for a genuinely new day). The server's own daily count (`tracker-data.js`'s `fetchPomoSessions`, correctly scoped by `todayIST()`) was never the problem — confirmed directly before touching anything — it just kept losing the merge against the already-corrupted client-side value. Fixed with `pomoSavedIsFromToday(saved)`, checking whichever timestamp field the blob actually has (`savedAt`, an epoch-ms number for the local path; `updatedAt`, an ISO string for the cross-device path) against `todayIso()`; a same-day blob's count is merged in as before, a stale one contributes 0 instead. Deliberately scoped to `completedSessions` only — `mode`/`running`/`phaseEndAt`/`totalSeconds` restore exactly as before regardless of which day `saved` is from, since an actual active phase can legitimately span midnight (a late-night session shouldn't get killed just because the calendar date ticked over). Verified against 5 scenarios in isolation (stale-yesterday blob correctly zeroes, same-day blob correctly preserved, the original stale-lower-response protection still holds) before shipping. **Known residual gap, not fixed**: a tab left open continuously *through* midnight with no reload never re-runs the restore path at all, so it could keep counting up live past midnight until something (a click, a version-bump reload) triggers a fresh restore — judged a much smaller, more tolerable edge case than the persistent multi-day staleness this fix addresses, and not pursued further without evidence it's actually happening in practice. Verified the fix doesn't break a session that genuinely spans midnight (starts 11:50pm, completes 12:15am): `mode`/`running`/`phaseEndAt` restore unconditionally regardless of which day `saved` is from, so an in-progress phase always resumes and completes normally; both the client's live `pomo.completedSessions++` (in `pomoAdvance`, fires at actual completion time) and the server's crediting (`pomodoro-complete.js`, using `todayIST()` computed when the completion request is processed) already agree on "which day a session belongs to" — the day it *finished*, not the day it started — so no special-casing was needed for this case at all.
  - **The "known residual gap" above turned out to be a real, currently-live problem, not just a theoretical edge case — fixed 2026-09-21, after the user asked directly whether it had actually been fixed.** Checked production before answering: `sandipbehera321@gmail.com`'s `pomo_active_session.completed_sessions` had grown from 1026 (first noticed earlier this same day) to **1033** and was still climbing — hard proof the gap was real and ongoing, not hypothetical. Root cause, once traced precisely: `pomoSavedIsFromToday()`'s restore-time check only asks "was this blob's own SAVE timestamp today," which is a fundamentally different question from "does this blob's completedSessions VALUE represent only today's real sessions." A tab that's continuously active (real completions keep happening, so it never goes through a reload/restore) keeps re-saving with a perfectly legitimate "saved today" timestamp on every single real completion, forever — so that check can never flag the underlying count as stale, no matter how many real calendar days it's been silently accumulating across. The 2026-09-07 fix only ever covered "midnight passes while the tab sits idle, then a stale save gets read later" — not "midnight passes while the tab is actively ticking, with no restore ever happening to catch it." **Fixed with a second, independent mechanism, checked live rather than only at restore time**: `pomoResetSessionsIfNewDay()` (new) compares a new `pomo.completedSessionsDate` field against `todayIso()` and resets `completedSessions` to 0 the moment they diverge — called from `pomoAdvance()` (before every real completion's increment, so day-crossing sessions are attributed correctly going forward) and from `updatePomoDisplay()` (every per-second tick while a phase is running, so the *display* self-corrects live even before the next real completion, for a tab that's open and ticking across a midnight it happens to sit through). **A second piece was needed for the already-corrupted existing data** (SANDIP's real 1033, and presumably others): `pomoResetSessionsIfNewDay()` alone doesn't retroactively fix a value that's already wrong, and worse, the *first* restore under the new code would otherwise adopt that already-corrupted number one more time (with a perfectly today-dated timestamp) and then, having just set `completedSessionsDate` to today itself, never flag it as stale again until the *next* real midnight — silently "locking in" the bad number instead of correcting it. Added `POMO_SESSIONS_SANITY_CAP` (60 — generous enough that no real single day of genuine focus sessions could plausibly exceed it, purely a corrupted-data detector, not a feature limit): in `applyPomoActiveState()`, a restored `completedSessions` value above this cap is discarded as corrupted historical data regardless of its own "saved today" timestamp, the same way an actually-stale blob already is. `pomo.completedSessionsDate` is now also explicitly kept in sync after both restore paths (the local/cross-device merge, and the separate server-`pomo_daily_sessions`-day-count merge in `loadMonth`) rather than left implicit. **Verified against real, disposable test data, not just reasoned about**: (1) seeded a test student's `pomo_active_session` with SANDIP's exact shape — `completed_sessions: 1033`, a perfectly recent `updated_at` — loaded the real page as that student and confirmed the display now reads `0 / 4 sessions`, not 1033-mod-anything; (2) separately seeded a legitimate small value (`completed_sessions: 3`, recent timestamp, well under the sanity cap) and confirmed it's still correctly adopted (`3 / 4 sessions`) — proving the fix doesn't over-correct and break the normal case; (3) the core day-rollover algorithm itself (reset-then-increment vs. no-reset-same-day) verified in isolation with a standalone logic check, both branches behaving exactly as intended. A browser-based real-time day-boundary simulation (faking `Date` mid-session to force a rollover) was attempted but abandoned as unreliable — its own clock-faking interacted unpredictably with the test machine's local timezone offset, a test-harness limitation rather than anything about the shipped code, which the three checks above already verify directly. **Real, disposable test student and all its rows deleted immediately after**, same discipline as every other live-data test this session. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped.
  - **FOCUS/BREAK label made bigger with a pulse, scoped to only run during an active session** (same day, follow-up request) — `.pomodoro-mode` grew 12px → 14px (18px on the `.pomo-today-row` wide-viewport variant, up from 16px) with a 2.2s breathing `@keyframes` (opacity/scale). The pulse itself is gated by a `.pomo-active` class, not baked into the base rule — `renderPomodoro()`'s initial markup sets it from `pomo.running` at render time, and `updatePomoDisplay()` keeps it in sync afterward via `modeEl.classList.toggle('pomo-active', pomo.running)`, the exact same pattern the Pause/Start button label already uses. Verified the classList logic in isolation (idle → running → paused → resumed all toggle correctly) after a live-browser check produced a confusingly "already running" result on a supposedly-fresh test student — root-caused to test-environment session contamination from earlier testing in the same reused browser profile, not a real bug (confirmed by querying the student's actual `pomo_active_session` row directly, and by the fact that the *existing*, already-proven Pause/Start button text showed the identical "already running" artifact — proof it wasn't specific to this new code).
- **Malpractice detection for the anti-cheat check, added 2026-09-22 after a real student (Puneet, "SheSaidNo" — see his own Discord confession) proactively disclosed deliberately testing browser-clock manipulation against the Pomodoro timer.** Investigated first before building anything: `pomodoro_credit_failures` showed 20 real rejected attempts from him, all `insufficient_elapsed`, all for a 10-minute session, real elapsed time only ~10-85 seconds each — genuinely deduping to **7 distinct attempts** once grouped by `phase_started_at` (the client's own retry logic, up to 2 extra attempts per doomed request, was inflating the row count 3x). Also established directly from the code that a rejected completion was, and had always been, **completely silent client-side** — no error, toast, or banner anywhere — so his own "I think the website detected me" was pure inference from watching his stats not move, not any actual on-screen alert.
  - **Detection is deliberately scoped to `insufficient_elapsed` alone**, never any other `pomodoro_credit_failures` reason — every other reason (`phase_end_mismatch`, `no_active_session`, etc.) has a documented legitimate cause elsewhere in this file (e.g. two genuine requests racing each other), where `insufficient_elapsed` requires claiming far more real time passed than actually did, well past the endpoint's own 15s grace window — not something normal usage or network flakiness can trigger by accident. This is what keeps the whole feature safe for real students: it structurally can't false-positive off ordinary latency or a page reload mid-session.
