  - **Three new `students` columns** (`malpractice_incident_count`, `malpractice_offense_count`, `malpractice_frozen_until`) and `record_malpractice_incident` (schema.sql) — an atomic claim-and-escalate RPC, called once per genuinely NEW distinct incident (never per retry: `pomodoro-complete.js`'s `recordMalpracticeIfNewIncident` dedupes by checking whether this exact `phase_started_at` was already logged, BEFORE its own `logCreditFailure` insert runs, so that row is never mistaken for prior evidence of itself). Escalation ladder, matching the design agreed on: **1st distinct incident is silent** (logged only, benefit of the doubt for a genuine one-off); **2nd-3rd shows a "⚠️ Malpractice Detected" warning banner, no freeze**; **4th and every one after actually freezes new Focus sessions**, duration escalating per freeze triggered — 6h → 24h → 72h → 7 days, capped at the last tier. A plain `now() + tier`, not stacking onto remaining time on an existing freeze — structurally impossible to need, since a student physically can't generate a NEW incident while already frozen (Start is blocked entirely during a freeze).
  - **Enforcement is genuinely server-side, not just a UI gate**: `pomo-active.js` checks `malpractice_frozen_until` before allowing a new WORK phase to start (`isNewPhase && mode==='work' && running` — never Pause/Reset/a break phase, since break earns no credit anyway and freezing it would accomplish nothing), rejecting with `403 { error: 'malpractice_frozen', frozenUntil }`. The client-side check in `progress.js` (`pomoIsFrozen()`, checked in `pomoToggleRun()` before `pomoActuallyStart()` ever runs) is what makes Start visibly refuse to do anything instead of starting the local timer and only failing invisibly later at the server — but it's a convenience, not the actual boundary; a determined user hitting the API directly still hits the same server-side block.
  - **A real fault-tolerance bug caught and fixed before shipping, not after — the exact same class of mistake already made once this session for the all-time-minutes feature.** The first pass added the freeze check directly into `pomo-active.js`'s main flow with a hard `if (studentError) return json(500, ...)`, and added the three new columns straight into `team-students.js`'s main `students` select. Since the migration hadn't run yet in production, both would have broken a *lot*: a "column does not exist" error would have 500'd **every single Start/Pause/Skip/Reset sync** in `pomo-active.js` (the routine cross-device state sync every Focus session already depends on), and would have failed the *entire* `/team` Students view (every student's name, streak, everything) in `team-students.js`, not just the new malpractice fields. Fixed by wrapping `pomo-active.js`'s freeze check in its own try/catch (a lookup failure just means "not frozen," never a 500) and pulling `team-students.js`'s malpractice columns into their own separate, best-effort query merged in afterward — the exact same pattern already established for `fetchAllTimeMinutesByEmail`. Verified directly against real production, pre-migration: registered a real disposable test student, confirmed `/pomo-active` still succeeds normally (`{ok:true}`), `/tracker-data` degrades to `{incidentCount:0, frozenUntil:null}`, `/pomodoro-complete`'s rejection response is unchanged, and `/team-students`'s response (335 real students) still returns correctly with `malpractice_incident_count: 0` for everyone — nothing broken by shipping ahead of the migration.
  - **Puneet specifically was NOT retroactively penalized** — his 7 real distinct incidents all predate any enforcement existing, and he disclosed them unprompted rather than hiding them; applying a freeze after the fact would punish someone for testing something that had already correctly failed with zero actual benefit to him. Per direct instruction, he instead gets a one-time manual bump to `malpractice_incident_count = 3` (landing him in the warning-shown-no-freeze band) via a standalone SQL statement, not baked into schema.sql itself (a targeted one-account data correction, not a schema change — same category as the earlier "add 4 hours to dipta" manual credit, kept as a live one-off action rather than committed migration code). No other student's historical `pomodoro_credit_failures` rows are backfilled into this — the counter only starts counting incidents that happen *after* this ships, so everyone else is grandfathered for free by construction, not by a special case.
  - **Admin visibility (backend only so far)**: `team-students.js` now returns each student's `malpractice_incident_count`/`malpractice_offense_count`/`malpractice_frozen_until`, plus a `summary.malpractice_flagged` count (students with at least one incident) — verified returning correctly against real production data. **Not yet wired into `/team`'s actual UI** — `team/team.js`/`team/index.html` are this project's standing uncommitted `/team` feature work (Telegram support, `dispatch_order`, etc. — see "Git remotes"/earlier entries), which must never be touched by unrelated work without being asked first; the data is live and fetchable, but rendering it as an actual column/badge in the Students table still needs that explicit go-ahead.
  - Requires the same standing manual SQL-editor step as every other schema change in this project (the `ALTER TABLE`/`record_malpractice_incident` block in `supabase/schema.sql`, right after `scheduled_posts.dispatch_order`) before any of this actually activates — until then, every piece already verified to degrade to its safe no-op default rather than breaking anything. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped.
  - **Migration run, then a real regression caught from a real screenshot the same day, immediately followed by a UX redesign request that turned out to fix it properly.** The warning banner (below-the-controls placement, see above) was the first thing tested for real — seeded `dipta.taai@gmail.com`'s own `malpractice_incident_count` to 2 for a genuine local hover/render check, and the resulting screenshot showed "Today's Leaders" badly broken: severe word-by-word text wrapping ("Top / 10 / by / hours / logged"). **Root cause, traced precisely, not guessed**: `.pomo-today-row .pomodoro-card` has `flex: 0 0 auto` with no explicit width — an implicit, fragile assumption that the 340px ring would always be the widest thing inside the card, driving its shrink-to-fit size. The warning notice's one long, unwrapped sentence became a *wider* piece of content than the ring, so the card's auto-width ballooned to ~900px, and its flex-shrink:1 sibling (`#today-leaderboard-card`) got squeezed down to a measured **32px**. Fixed at the root, not just by capping the one new element: `.pomo-today-row .pomodoro-card` now has an explicit `width: 380px` (340px ring + the card's own 20px each-side padding, border-box) — nothing inside it, however wide its text, can widen the card again; text just wraps, as it always should have. **The same message arrived, separately, as a direct redesign request** — "can the message come in place of the clock and then you press okay to go back to the clock?" — restructured from a small notice below the controls into a real gate that replaces the ring/dots/controls entirely (`renderPomoMalpracticeGateHtml()`, a new `.pomo-clock-wrap` wrapping everything it replaces). Both blocks are always rendered in the DOM — never conditionally swapped in the HTML string — with one starting `hidden`, exactly the same pattern the settings panel already uses; an "Okay" button (warning band only — an active freeze has nothing to "go back to," since Start still refuses to do anything underneath it) just toggles which one is hidden via plain DOM manipulation in `bindCalendarEvents()`, not a re-render, so it doesn't replay the card's `.fade-in` entrance. **A second real bug, caught from a screenshot again, not assumed**: the first version of the gate's CSS set `display: flex` directly on the class, which — at equal specificity — beats the browser's own `[hidden]{display:none}` UA rule; clicking Okay correctly flipped the `hidden` *property* (confirmed via `element.hidden === true`) but the box stayed visibly on screen anyway, stacked above the now-also-visible clock. Fixed with an explicit `.pomo-malpractice-gate[hidden] { display: none; }` override. Re-verified end-to-end against the real page: leaderboard card back to its correct 360px, gate visible with clock hidden before Okay, gate hidden with clock visible (and fully interactive — Start/Reset/Skip, dots, ring) after Okay — a real click-and-measure test, not just re-reading the CSS. `dipta.taai@gmail.com`'s `malpractice_incident_count` reset back to 0 immediately after, same disposable-test discipline as every other live-account check this session. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
  - **Two more follow-ups, same day, sent mid-turn while the above was still being verified**: (1) "temporarily" dropped from the warning text — "Continued attempts may result in Focus sessions being frozen," matching the actual freeze-state wording's own tone rather than softening it. (2) A real dismiss animation instead of an instant `hidden` swap on Okay — `.pomo-malpractice-gate--dismissing` (opacity + `transform: scale(0.95)`, a 0.25s transition) added to the gate on click, with the actual `hidden`/reveal swap deferred via `setTimeout` to match. Safe to animate `transform` directly here since the gate has `border-radius` but no `box-shadow` — doesn't trip this file's own known Chromium DOM-node-leak gotcha, which needs both together. Deliberately a `setTimeout` matched to the CSS duration, not a `transitionend` listener — under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, the CSS's own `transition: none` means no transition ever runs for `transitionend` to fire on, so that approach would hang forever for those users; the existing reduced-motion JS check (same pattern `animateExamCountdown` already uses) skips straight to the instant reveal instead. Verified against the real page with dipta's account re-flagged once more (then reset again after): confirmed the exact wording change, and sampled the gate ~120ms into the animation showing genuinely in-progress values (`opacity: 0.556`, `transform: matrix(0.978,...)` — between 1 and the 0.95 target, not an instant jump), with the full hide/reveal completing correctly afterward. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
  - **"So how will it go and he get back to normal?" — a real, direct product question exposing a genuine gap: the warning gate had no path back to normal at all.** `malpractice_incident_count` only ever increases (a deliberate, permanent record — see its own comment), meaning as originally shipped, the gate would nag on literally every single Focus Mode visit forever, even for someone who never repeats the behavior. Presented three options (sticky one-time dismiss, time-based decay, admin-only manual reset); picked: **"sticky dismiss but nag 3 times"** — the gate shows at most 3 times for a given incident count, then goes quiet on its own, without the underlying incident_count (the permanent admin-visible record) ever having to decrease. New `malpractice_warning_ack_count` column + `increment_malpractice_warning_ack` RPC (schema.sql) — incremented once per Okay click, capped at 3 server-side too (not just relying on the client to stop asking). `record_malpractice_incident` now also resets this to 0 on every genuinely new incident, so a fresh incident always gets its own fresh 3-nag budget rather than inheriting an already-exhausted one from an earlier tier. New `netlify/functions/malpractice-ack.js` (POST, fire-and-forget from the client, same non-critical tolerance as `pomo-active`/`pomo-settings`) is what the Okay button now calls; `progress.js`'s `pomoMalpracticeWarningActive()` centralizes the "still worth showing" check (`incidentCount >= 2 && warningAckCount < 3`), used both by the render gate and the visibility computation in `renderPomodoro`. The ack count is also updated in `state.malpractice` immediately client-side (not just fired off to the server) so a same-session re-entry into Focus Mode without a reload already reflects the new count. **A real, live-caught regression, the same class of mistake already made once this session for the all-time-minutes feature — caught from testing, not assumed**: the first pass added the new `malpractice_warning_ack_count` column straight into `fetchMalpracticeStatus`'s existing combined select (alongside the already-migrated `incident_count`/`frozen_until`) — since a single PostgREST select fails ENTIRELY if even one requested column doesn't exist, this silently masked dipta's real, already-correct `incident_count: 2` behind the safe-default `0` fallback the moment the new column didn't exist yet (confirmed directly: the gate stopped appearing at all for her real, unmigrated-column-blocked account). Fixed by pulling the ack-count read into its own separate, independently-fault-tolerant query — re-verified afterward: `/api/tracker-data` correctly returns `incidentCount: 2` again even with the new column still missing. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
  - **"The warning fade out animation can be better" — sent mid-turn while the sticky-nag feature above was still being built.** The dismiss was a plain fade+shrink with a linear-ish ease, and — more noticeably — the clock reappeared as an instant pop the moment the gate finished hiding, a mismatched handoff between a smooth dismiss and an abrupt reveal. Rebuilt with: a nicer `cubic-bezier(.4,0,.2,1)` ease on the gate's own dismiss (was plain `ease`), a touch more motion (`scale(0.92)` + a 10px upward drift, was just `scale(0.95)` with no drift), and — the actual fix for the "better" ask — a coordinated fade-in on `.pomo-clock-wrap` itself: it now starts at `opacity:0, scale(0.98)` the instant it's revealed, then transitions up to normal a frame later, using the exact double-rAF "let the browser actually paint the starting state first" pattern this page's own `animateHourlyBusyMeter` already established (a single rAF can land in the same paint as the initial state change and skip the transition entirely on some browsers). No `box-shadow`/`border-radius` on `.pomo-clock-wrap`, so animating `transform`+`opacity` on it doesn't trip this file's own Chromium DOM-node-leak gotcha either. Verified with a real frame-by-frame sample across the whole sequence (not just before/after): `t=80ms` showed the gate mid-fade (`opacity: 0.85`, `transform` scale ~0.988 + ~1.5px lift, genuinely progressing); `t=240ms` showed it nearly gone (`opacity: 0.07`, scale ~0.926, ~8.6px lift — close to its `scale(0.92) translateY(-10px)` target); `t=360ms` (just past the 320ms dismiss duration) showed the gate now hidden and the clock visible but still at `opacity: 0` — the exact moment its own fade-in begins, not an instant pop; `t=660ms` showed everything fully settled. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
- **The weekly-leaderboard "streak balls" got milestone tiers plus a flame badge for the overflow count, tied to the program's own 180-day length ("give me ideas to better it," then "do 1 and 2 knowing the fact that the program is of 180 days and started from august 1st," 2026-09-16).** Was one flat pink-purple gradient for every filled ball regardless of streak length, and a plain gray "+N" pill once past `STREAK_BALLS_CAP` (5) — a 3-day and a 47-day streak (the real current maximum this early in the program, since nobody can be further into a streak than the program itself has run) rendered identically, which was the actual complaint: nothing visually distinguished "just started" from "the best anyone can currently be." `STREAK_TIERS` (`progress.js`, next to `streakBallsHtml`) derives its thresholds from the program's own structure rather than arbitrary habit-app numbers — Bronze (7d) and Silver (30d) are reachable early, **Gold sits at `PROGRAM_LENGTH_DAYS / 2` (90) — the program's actual halfway point, not a round number chosen in isolation** — and **Diamond at exactly 180 is the full program length**, i.e. finishing it without ever breaking streak, deliberately the hardest thing this product can recognize rather than an arbitrarily-picked "high" streak. `streak-ball--base` is the original gradient byte-for-byte, so the common case (days 1-6, before any tier applies) is visually unchanged. The old flat "+N" overflow pill became a `streak-flame-badge` (a small inline SVG flame + count, tier-colored) — the Duolingo/Snapchat streak-flame pattern, reframing "too many to fit as balls" as a status symbol instead of a truncation notice, which is exactly backwards from what a flat gray pill communicated for the streaks that most deserved to stand out. Diamond gets a genuinely different treatment, not just another flat color — a slow `background-position` shimmer across the site's own brand gradient (pink→purple→blue) — animating position on an oversized gradient rather than `transform`/`box-shadow` directly on this element, since it already carries both `box-shadow` and `border-radius` and this file's own known Chromium DOM-node-leak gotcha applies to exactly that combination; respects `prefers-reduced-motion` (freezes at a fixed position). Verified against 4 disposable test students seeded one per tier (streak 15/45/120/200) at the top of a real weekly leaderboard load: correct tier class on every ball, correct flame-badge math (e.g. 200 → 5 diamond balls + "195"), and a real screenshot confirming the four tiers read as clearly distinct at a glance — gold in particular stands out immediately — while real students already at the current maximum (several at 47, correctly landing in Silver) rendered correctly alongside the synthetic ones. Gold/Diamond have no real students yet (46 days into the 180-day program at ship time, so 47 is genuinely the highest any streak could be) — verified via the seeded test accounts instead, same as any other not-yet-reachable milestone. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped — a real `progress.js`/leaderboard-rendering change.
  - **Two rounds of direct follow-up feedback the same day: "the silver looks too bleak also what is the emoji beside the numbers, i don't like them."** The flame SVG inside the overflow badge read as an unwanted emoji-like decoration rather than the intended status-symbol reframing — removed outright (`STREAK_FLAME_SVG` deleted, badge is now a plain tier-colored number, no icon); the tier coloring alone still distinguishes it from the original flat gray "+N," which was the actual point. Silver's first pass (`#f8fafc` → `#cbd5e1` → `#64748b`) was two adjacent pale grays that barely registered against the dark page background — rebuilt with a genuinely bright near-white highlight against a deeper, more saturated slate base (`#ffffff` → `#e2e8f0` → `#94a3b8` → `#475569`) plus a stronger, more saturated glow, for real polished-chrome contrast instead of flat pale-on-pale; the badge's silver got the same treatment (near-white text on a more saturated slate background, up from pale-gray-on-pale-gray). Re-verified with the same 4-seeded-test-student technique as the original ship — confirmed the badge HTML is plain text with no markup, and a fresh screenshot showing silver now reads as a distinct, shining tier rather than blending into the background.
  - **The 30d/90d thresholds' tier *colors* were swapped the next day ("replace silver with gold" → clarified as "replace the silver criteria for gold and vice versa," 2026-09-17)** — 30d is now Gold, 90d (`PROGRAM_LENGTH_DAYS / 2`) is now Silver, thresholds themselves unchanged. Rationale: real top performers are all currently in the 30-89 day range (47 days, the actual current maximum this early in the program) — that's the tier that's actually visible today, so it's the one that should read as a real achievement rather than the color that happened to land there first. The 90+ range nobody's reached yet gets the (now much-improved, but still one notch down from gold) silver treatment instead. Re-verified against real production data, not just reasoned about: loaded the live leaderboard as Gintoki and confirmed all four real 47-day-streak students (Gintoki, Soumyajit, Karan Rajpurohit, Baction) now render `streak-ball--gold`, where they previously rendered `streak-ball--silver` — a real screenshot showing their rows now popping gold.
  - **A real, confirmed rendering bug plus a new legend feature, same follow-up round ("why are there different shades of gold, where is the silver... have a legend of the colours").** The "different shades" report was genuine, not perception — `getComputedStyle` on two real gold badges (Gintoki's row, which carries the highlighted `leaderboard-row--top leaderboard-row--me` background, vs. Soumyajit's plain row) showed byte-identical CSS (`rgba(251,191,36,0.16)`) rendering as visibly different pixels, because a low-alpha badge background blends with whatever row background happens to sit behind it — the exact same tier looked different purely from which row it landed in. Fixed by bumping every tier badge's background to a near-opaque alpha (0.55-0.85, up from 0.14-0.32) so the badge's own color dominates regardless of context; re-verified with the same getComputedStyle technique showing byte-identical rendered backgrounds across rows with different contexts. "Where is the silver" was expected, not a bug — it needs 90 days and the program (started Aug 1) was only 47 days in at the time, so nothing could be there yet; explained rather than treated as something to fix. **The legend**: a small "?" toggle button added directly next to the Streak column header (`streak-legend-toggle`) opens a `position:absolute` popover (`streak-legend-popover`, closes on any outside click) listing all 5 tiers as swatch + name + day range — "meaningfully positioned" at the exact point of the reported confusion, and "smart use of negative space" since the collapsed toggle costs nothing in the header's own layout until actually clicked, rather than a permanently-visible block competing with the leaderboard rows for room. `streakLegendHtml()` builds its rows directly from `STREAK_TIERS` (the same array `streakTierFor()` already uses) rather than a separately-typed list of day ranges — given the tier thresholds/colors had already been swapped once this same day, a second hand-maintained copy would have been very likely to silently drift the next time this changes. Verified end-to-end: toggle click opens it, a second click on an unrelated element closes it, and the rendered day ranges (0-6/7-29/30-89/90-179/180+) exactly match the live `STREAK_TIERS` thresholds — plus a full-page screenshot confirming the popover renders cleanly below the header with no overlap or clipping. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped for both fixes together.
  - **Two more follow-ups the same round: "the legend is not viisble much" and "can we have the start animation for the clock for every action including pause reset skip etc," plus "gold should be at a higher day count than silver."** The legend toggle grew from a 13px near-transparent outline to a 16px filled purple circle with a real background (`rgba(139,92,246,0.45)`, up from `0.06`) — it read as blending into the header text rather than as a tappable control. **The ring-flash widening was a real architectural change, not just a CSS tweak**: the existing flash only fired incidentally, inferred from `updatePomoDisplay()`'s icon-state-transition guard (`desiredIconState === 'pause'`) — which only ever catches a genuine Start/Resume, since Reset and Skip frequently don't change the Start/Pause icon at all (e.g. Skip while already running keeps the icon on "pause" throughout, so the old guard's diff would never see a change to trigger on). Replaced with a standalone `flashPomoRing()` helper, called *explicitly* from all four action points (`pomoActuallyStart` — covers both Start and Resume since both funnel through it, the Pause branch of `pomoToggleRun`, `pomoReset`, `pomoSkip`) instead of being inferred from a side effect of something else — the only way to reliably guarantee "every action" rather than "whichever actions happen to also flip the icon." Verified against a disposable test student with real `animationstart` event tracking (not just visual inspection): exactly one flash fired per action across Start → Pause → Reset → Start → Skip, zero missing, zero double-fires. **The gold/silver order was reverted the same round** — see the tier-swap entry above for why (gold below silver breaks the universal Bronze < Silver < Gold expectation; the original "bleak silver" complaint was actually about color quality, already fixed independently of tier ordering). Re-verified against Gintoki's real account: back to `streak-ball--silver`, as expected for a real 47-day streak once Gold requires the halfway point again. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped.
  - **One more the same round: "the ? beside streak is not sitting with the text beside it, make it better yet attention grabbing at the same time."** `.leaderboard-col-streak` was a plain inline `<span>` — "Streak" and the `?` toggle sat on the browser's default text-baseline relative to each other rather than being deliberately centered, which visibly offset the small round button against the text's cap-height. Fixed with `display: inline-flex; align-items: center` on the cell itself (inline-flex specifically, not flex, so the cell still sizes to its content instead of stretching to fill its grid track like every sibling header cell). Verified precisely, not just by eye: measured the "Streak" text node's own bounding-rect center (via `Range.getBoundingClientRect()`, not the parent span's, which would include the button) against the toggle button's center — 0.36px apart, i.e. genuinely centered, not just visually close. **Attention-grabbing, added carefully around this file's own known Chromium DOM-node-leak gotcha**: the toggle already carries both `box-shadow` and `border-radius` (a circular glowing badge), which is exactly the combination that gotcha warns against animating `transform`/`box-shadow` on directly — so the breathing pulse animates `opacity` alone instead (1 → 0.55 → 1, 2.2s), a property that gotcha doesn't cover and reads as an equally noticeable pulse at this size regardless. Pauses on hover and respects `prefers-reduced-motion`. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped — this is a pure-CSS change in `gate-da-progress-tracker.html`, but still needs the version bump to reach an already-open tab, since new CSS only loads on an actual page reload and this codebase's reload check is what triggers that for a tab that's been sitting open.
  - **"Center align all the column names according to their content within" (2026-09-21)** — before touching anything, measured every header's real position against its column's actual content via precise CDP `Range.getBoundingClientRect()`/child-element measurements (not a screenshot-only check): Rank/Status/Timer/Minutes headers were already centered and matched their content within sub-pixel to ~2px, so nothing there needed changing. Name and Streak were the two genuinely off — both still `text-align: left` from before the rest of the row got centered, the actual "odd ones out" against the other four. Confirmed the intended scope with a direct follow-up question rather than guessing again after an earlier wrong guess this same session (the "names not visible full" truncation report, initially misread as a spacing request) — centering just Name/Streak's own header labels, not forcing row *content* (names, streak balls) to re-align, since that content is deliberately left-anchored on its own merits (see the streak-ball comment above — "every row's first ball now starts at the exact same x"). `.leaderboard-col-name` dropped its `padding-left: 13px` (which existed only to match the old left-alignment to the live-dot offset) for `text-align: center`; `.leaderboard-col-streak` gained `justify-content: center` alongside its existing `text-align` (now also `center`) — needed since it's a flex container, where `text-align` alone doesn't reposition the flex children. Verified against the real live board (not synthetic): both header boxes' own centers now land within ~0.01-2px of their own column's true center (computed independently from the row content's own bounding box), and a real screenshot shows both "NAME" and "STREAK ?" now visually centered over their columns like the other four headers, with the underlying row content correctly untouched.
    - **Immediately reverted for Streak specifically, caught from a direct follow-up screenshot** — centering "STREAK ?" over the full column left a real, visible gap to where the balls actually start: Streak's content is often just a single ~14px ball sitting at a fixed left x inside a much wider flexible column, unlike Name's content (real display names), which typically spans most of its own column and reads fine centered. Reverted `.leaderboard-col-streak` back to `text-align: left; justify-content: flex-start` (its original, pre-this-session values) — matching the same left anchor the row content itself uses, confirmed via the same precise measurement technique: header text's left edge now lands at the exact same x as the first streak ball's left edge on every row checked. Name's header stayed centered, unaffected by this — the two columns needed different treatments because their content shapes are fundamentally different (near-full-width text vs. a small fixed-width cluster), not because the original "center everything" framing was wrong in general.
    - **One more attempt at centering Streak specifically ("can't we center align streak?"), also reverted the same round.** Tried a narrower middle ground before giving up on centering entirely — instead of centering over the full ~372px flexible column (already known bad), gave the header a fixed 160px sub-box (approximating a typical multi-ball row's real span) and centered "STREAK ?" within just that, still positioned flush against the column's own left edge same as before. Measured against real rows before shipping: landed close to a typical 2-3-ball row's center (header center ~609 vs. content centers ~581-634 across sampled rows) — better than the full-column attempt, but still visibly off from a single-ball row (~534). Reverted anyway at direct follow-up ("nah left align it back") before it ever shipped — no `CLIENT_VERSION` bump needed, since the working tree was restored to the exact same CSS already live from the prior commit, just with an updated comment recording the extra attempt. **Streak's header stays left-aligned** — three attempts at centering it (full-column, bounded-box, and the original ask) all landed worse than simply matching the content's own left anchor, which is the version that's actually shipped.
  - **Minutes centered too, later the same day ("the minutes column name is not centrally alllgined witht it's contents")** — a real, precisely-measured check first (`Range.getBoundingClientRect()` on the header text vs. every sampled row's number) found the header and content were already byte-identical right-aligned (1133.85px on both, zero delta) — not a bug. A clarifying question confirmed the actual ask: center the "MINUTES" header within its own column instead, matching Rank/Status/Timer, deliberately diverging from the right-aligned numbers below it rather than trying to match them (unlike Name/Streak earlier, where matching content was the right call). `.leaderboard-col-time` switched `text-align: right` → `center`; `.leaderboard-time` (the actual "540m" values) stays right-aligned, untouched. Re-measured after shipping: header's own text center now lands within ~0.01px of its column's true center.
    - **Immediately corrected on the very next message ("the minutes is not properly alligned"), from a screenshot showing the now-centered header sitting visibly left of the still-right-aligned numbers below it.** A direct follow-up question confirmed the fix this time: keep the header centered, and center `.leaderboard-time` (the actual "540m"/"410m" values) too, rather than reverting the header back to right-aligned — the opposite resolution from the earlier Streak saga, but the same underlying lesson (header and content need to share ONE alignment, not each pick a different one). `.leaderboard-time`'s `text-align` switched `right` → `center`; harmless on the narrow Today's-Leaders companion card that shares this same class (that column is auto-width/content-sized with no header of its own, so centering vs. right-aligning inside a box exactly as wide as its own text has no visible effect there). Re-measured after the fix: header text center and every sampled row's number center now land within 0.01px of each other.
- **The Today card (main checklist page) split into two halves — checklist on the left, a new "when does the batch study" 24-hour bar chart on the right, 2026-09-21.** ("can this section be divided in two parts from middle, while on the left side it stays the same while on the [right] side it contains a graph of 24 hours in a day when showing the active times of students," scope confirmed via a clarifying question — the Today checklist, not any leaderboard card — and via a second question on what the graph should actually show: an aggregate of how many students are active at each hour, not the viewer's own pattern or a live-only view). **No existing data could power this** — every prior table tracked focus minutes per day or per week, never per hour-of-day, so this needed new server-side aggregation, not just a frontend chart. `pomo_hourly_activity` (new table, `supabase/schema.sql`) is a deliberately tiny, fixed 24-row table (one row per hour 0-23, IST) rather than a new row per student per day — it's a single shared, batch-wide histogram, so it never grows and never needs cleanup regardless of student count or elapsed time. `increment_hourly_activity` (new RPC) follows the exact same atomic-UPSERT pattern already established by `increment_pomodoro_stats`/`increment_pomo_daily_sessions` (row-level locking during the UPDATE, no read-then-write race). `pomodoro-complete.js` calls it right after crediting a session, attributing the WHOLE session to the hour it STARTED in (`hourIST(session.phase_started_at)`, a new helper in `lib/supabase.js` mirroring `todayIST()`'s Asia/Kolkata reasoning) — not the hour it finished, so a session straddling an hour boundary still counts exactly once, toward when someone actually sat down. Deliberately best-effort: wrapped in its own try/catch so a failure here can never turn an already-successful completion (the week/day increments already landed by this point) into an error response, same reasoning `logCreditFailure` already established. `fetchHourlyActivity()` (`lib/supabase.js`) always returns a plain 24-length array (index === hour, zero-filled for any hour with no data) rather than a sparse map, so the frontend never needs to guard against a missing hour. Fetched unconditionally from `tracker-data.js`'s page-load batch (same as `schedule`/`lastWeekLeaders` — batch-wide data, not gated on being a signed-in student, so guests see it too), with its own `.catch(() => ({ hours: [] }))` fallback matching every other non-critical piece of that batch. **Frontend**: `renderTodayCard()` now builds `.today-card-left` (the untouched checklist markup) and `.today-card-right` (`pomoHourlyActivityHtml()`, new) inside a `.today-card-split` flex container — flex-wrap rather than a fixed-pixel media-query breakpoint, since `.today-card`'s own width already varies a lot depending on whether the page-grid's sidebar is showing, so wrapping to a stacked layout once either side drops below its own comfortable min-width is more robust than guessing one "mobile" number. Bars are a plain static CSS bar chart (heights set once at render time via inline `style`, no continuous animation), each hour's fill height as a percentage of that day's max hour — genuinely empty (max minutes across all 24 hours is 0, the real state immediately after shipping, since there's no historical time-of-day data to backfill from) shows a "Fills in as students complete focus sessions" message instead of a flat empty grid. Verified locally: with the table not yet existing in production (confirmed — the manual SQL migration step below hasn't run yet), the whole page still loads cleanly with zero console errors, the checklist itself is completely unaffected, and the empty-state message renders correctly via the same catch-fallback path every other tracker-data.js piece already relies on; separately verified the populated-chart rendering by intercepting the real `/tracker-data` response via CDP `Fetch` and substituting a synthetic realistic hourly pattern (quiet overnight, a morning bump, a big evening peak) — the bars, gradient fill, and every-3rd-hour labels (12a/3a/6a/9a/12p/3p/6p/9p) all rendered cleanly beside the checklist with a real screenshot. **Requires the same standing manual step as every other schema change in this project**: `pomo_hourly_activity`/`increment_hourly_activity` need to be run once via the Supabase SQL editor against production before any real data can flow in — until then, `fetchHourlyActivity`'s catch fallback keeps this a harmless no-op (empty-state message only), not a broken page. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped — a real `progress.js` rendering change.
  - **Migration run confirmed against production, then verified end-to-end with real seeded data (explicitly authorized, since the table was confirmed still empty — 0 rows — at the time)**: rather than the earlier CDP-response-mocking approach (which never touches the real database), a one-time script wrote 24 real rows via a plausible daily pattern (quiet overnight, morning bump, evening peak) directly into the actual table, then a real page load against the real endpoint (no interception) confirmed the whole pipeline — table → RPC-shaped read → `tracker-data.js` → `progress.js` → render — end to end. This mock data stays in the table only until explicitly told to remove it, per direct instruction ("mock up data so that i can see and then delete it permanently when i say") — not yet deleted as of this writeup.
  - **The first shipped design got direct, blunt negative feedback on all three fronts at once ("the heading is terrible, the graph looks terrible and there is a lot of negative space below")** — substantially reworked, not just tweaked. **The graph**: was 24 individual hour-bars crammed into a ~200px column (each bar only 6-7px wide, only every 3rd hour labeled to avoid worse crowding) — reworked into 8 three-hour buckets instead, each summed from the underlying 24 hourly values (storage/increment logic untouched, this is purely a display-time aggregation), giving bars wide enough to read as an actual chart with a label under every single one. **A second, more specific bug in the original bars, not just "too many/too thin"**: the fill was a 3-stop `linear-gradient(180deg, pink, purple, blue)` sized to each bar's OWN height, not the chart's — a 100%-height bar showed a smooth 3-color gradient, but a 4%-height bar showed the whole 3-stop gradient compressed into a sliver, reading as a muddy, inconsistent smear that looked nothing like its taller neighbors. Fixed by dropping to a tight 2-stop gradient of adjacent brand hues (`#C99CFB` → `var(--purple)`), which blends cleanly at any bar height since there's no third, distant hue to compress. The single busiest bucket gets its own highlighted pink gradient + glow, plus a "Busiest: <range>" caption underneath — both new, giving the chart an actual point of focus instead of 24 (or 8) undifferentiated bars. **The heading**: dropped the 📊 emoji and "When the batch studies" wording for plain "Activity by hour", matching the sidebar's "Progress by subject" heading's exact styling (uppercase, `--ink-soft`, no icon) for visual consistency with the rest of the page instead of looking like a one-off decoration. **The negative space**: `.today-card-right` was originally just top-aligned inside the split row, so on a viewport where the checklist (left) is naturally taller, the whole right column — heading included — sat pinned at the top of a much taller box with a large dead gap below. First fix (`align-items:stretch` on the row + `justify-content:center` on the whole right column) over-corrected into a NEW problem, caught by a second round of direct feedback ("the labels are terrible and the heading position is terrible") — centering the *entire* block, heading included, threw "Activity by hour" out of vertical alignment with "Today" on the left, the two column headers no longer starting at the same height. Fixed by only centering what's below the heading: `.hourly-activity` is now its own `flex-direction:column` box spanning the full stretched height, with the title pinned at its top (naturally aligned with `.today-tag` on the left) and a new `.hourly-activity-body` wrapper (`flex:1; justify-content:center`) holding just the bars + peak caption, centered in the leftover space beneath the fixed title — headings align across both halves, dead space is minimized without needing a second round of feedback to notice it moved rather than disappeared. Verified visually at each step against the real seeded production data above, not reasoned about in the abstract — a real screenshot after the final fix confirms "ACTIVITY BY HOUR" and "TODAY" now sit on the same horizontal line. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped twice more (once per round).
  - **A third round the same day: "the hour labels still look terrible and also add some effects to the busiest hour so that it's catchy to the eye."** The bucket labels were the full range ("12a–3a", "3a–6a", ...) crammed under an already-narrow ~55px bar column — dropped to a single tick per bar (just the bucket's start hour: "12a", "3a", "6a"...), the normal way a chart axis reads, with the full range still available as the bar's own hover tooltip for anyone who wants it. Label font bumped 9px→10.5px/600 weight now that there's less text to fit. **The peak effect**: a 🔥 emoji positioned directly above the busiest bar (`position:absolute`, anchored to `.hourly-bar-col`, since that bar's fill is always exactly 100% height by construction — it IS the max — so a fixed offset above the track's top edge lines up correctly with no per-row calculation needed) plus a slow breathing glow on the bar itself. **The glow is opacity-only, not a transform/box-shadow animation, deliberately** — the peak bar's fill already carries both `box-shadow` (its glow) and `border-radius` (its rounded top corners), exactly the combination this file's own known Chromium DOM-node-leak gotcha warns against animating `transform`/`box-shadow` on directly; opacity isn't covered by that gotcha and reads as an equally noticeable pulse at this size, the same reasoning already applied once before on this same page (the streak-legend-toggle's own breathing pulse). The peak bar's own label also picks up the same pink accent color and a heavier weight, and the "Busiest: <range>" caption gained a matching 🔥 prefix — three small, consistent touches rather than one loud effect. Verified against the same real seeded production data: a real screenshot shows the flame sitting cleanly above the peak bar with no clipping (checked specifically, since `.today-card` itself has `overflow:hidden` and the flame's `-20px` offset needed enough clearance above it to not be cut off), and confirms `prefers-reduced-motion` is respected (the pulse `@keyframes` is disabled under that media query, matching every other decorative animation on this page). `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
  - **A fourth round the same day, three asks together: "the busiest time period ... beside the text can be better", "the graph can look better and dynamic", and "mention batch c in the heading" beside the tasks.** **The "Busiest" line** went from a plain caption to a real pill badge — bigger padding, a tinted pink/purple gradient background with its own glow border, and the time range itself now reuses `@keyframes streak-grad`, the exact animated pink→purple→blue gradient-text treatment every other genuine "stat" number on this page already uses (`.streak-number`, `.exam-countdown-num`, `.pomodoro-time`) — ties this into the same established "this is a real achievement" visual language rather than inventing a new one-off style, which is also what made it read as "rewarding" per the follow-up ask rather than needing a bespoke celebratory design. A direct follow-up ("increase the spacing... make it somewhat bigger") bumped the badge's own top margin (12px → 28px) and its type scale (11.5px plain text → 18px bold gradient time + a separate 10.5px uppercase "BUSIEST" label). **The graph itself**: bars now grow in from 0 on first paint instead of popping in at full height — a `@keyframes hourly-bar-grow` animating `height` (a layout property, not `transform`, so this doesn't implicate the box-shadow+border-radius DOM-leak gotcha regardless of which bars carry those) from 0 to a `--bar-h` custom property set inline per bar, staggered 70ms apart via a `--grow-delay` custom property — same one-shot-cascade idea already used elsewhere on this page (the daily/weekly leaderboard row entrances), just applied to bar height instead of opacity+transform. The peak bar needed both this one-shot grow AND its existing continuous pulse running together — since the CSS `animation` shorthand doesn't merge across cascaded rules (a more specific selector's `animation` fully replaces the base rule's, it doesn't add to it), the peak-specific rule redeclares both animations together with two comma-separated `animation-delay` values, the grow's staggered and the pulse's fixed at `0s`. Bars also gained a static (never animated, so no leak risk) inset top highlight for a bit of glossy depth, and a hover brightness bump. **Batch C**: `.today-tag` (the small label above "Mon, Sep 21") changed from plain "Today" to "Today · Batch C", matching the existing "TAAI BATCH C" branding used elsewhere on this page's own `<h1>`. Verified against the same real seeded production data with a screenshot taken 1.2s after page load (past the ~1.2s total grow-in+stagger window) confirming bars at their correct final heights, the badge showing "🔥 BUSIEST 9p–12a" with the gradient-text time clearly distinct from the plain "BUSIEST" label, and "TODAY · BATCH C" in the heading. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
  - **A fifth round, reversing most of the "reward"/celebratory direction from the round above: "don't use fire emoji in the bar, also keep the bars of the same colour but with increasing intesity of gradient, also the busiest [pill] is just information not a celebratory something so keep dunamic like it's chill or it's intense."** Direct, explicit pushback on the exact treatment the previous round had leaned into — celebratory styling wasn't actually wanted, informational-but-alive was. **Bars**: the 🔥 spark and the busiest bar's distinct pink gradient are both gone — every bar now shares the same one purple gradient, and a new `--intensity` custom property (`0.35 + 0.65 * pct/100`, set inline per bar alongside `--bar-h`/`--grow-delay`) drives `opacity` instead of color, so quieter hours render as a muted, faded version of the same hue and the busiest hour reads as the most vivid — automatically, with no per-bar special-casing, since the busiest bar's `pct` is always exactly 100 by construction (it IS the max the others are measured against). The peak bar's continuous pulse animation is gone entirely along with it. **The caption**: reverted from a glowing pill with animated gradient text back to a single plain line ("Busiest: **9p–12a** Intense") — no background, no border, no glow, no animation. Its "dynamic" quality per the follow-up ask ("keep dynamic like it's chill or it's intense") is now genuinely data-driven rather than decorative: a new `spikeRatio` (the busiest bucket's total vs. the *average of the other 7 buckets*, not a fixed raw-minutes threshold) decides the word — a fairly even spread across the day reads "Chill" (cool blue, `#7DD3FC`), a real concentrated spike reads "Intense" (warm amber, `#FBBF24`); ≥1.8x the other buckets' average is the current cutoff. This is a genuine read on the shape of the distribution, not just "whichever bucket happens to be biggest" — a batch that's mildly busier in the evening than the rest of the day correctly reads as Chill, not Intense, even though it still has a technical "busiest" hour. Verified against the same real seeded production data (a pattern with a real, sharp evening peak): screenshot confirms no emoji anywhere, all bars visibly the same hue at different opacities (the 9pm bar clearly more saturated than the quiet overnight ones), and the caption reading "Busiest: 9p–12a Intense" as plain text with only the word itself colored. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
  - **A sixth round the same day: "i want the busiest one like a dynamic busy meter."** The plain Chill/Intense caption became a real spectrum gauge — a fixed 3-color track (`linear-gradient(90deg, #7DD3FC, #A78BFA, #FBBF24)`, cool blue → purple → warm amber, always fully visible, never animated) with a small marker that slides to wherever today's real `spikeRatio` lands on it (`(spikeRatio - 1) / 2 * 100`, clamped 0-100 — ratio 1 = a totally flat day = 0%, ratio 3+ = a sharp concentrated spike = 100%). The track itself is static and unchanging; the marker's *position* is what's actually "dynamic" here, genuinely driven by that day's real data rather than a decorative animation layered on top of a static fact — consistent with the immediately preceding round's "informational, not celebratory" direction, just literally visualized as a gauge instead of only a word. Slides in via a one-shot `left` animation (a layout property, not `transform`, so — same as the bars' own grow-in — this doesn't implicate the box-shadow+border-radius DOM-leak gotcha; the marker's `translate(-50%,-50%)` is a fixed, never-animated centering offset, not what's moving) respecting `prefers-reduced-motion`. **A real bug caught immediately from the first screenshot, not shipped**: with the endpoint labels ("Chill"/"Intense") sitting below the track and the current read shown as a middle label between them, a maxed-out ("Intense") reading rendered as "CHILL ... INTENSE ... INTENSE" — the current-read label duplicating the right endpoint's own text whenever the marker sits at either extreme. Fixed by moving the current Chill/Intense word up into the header line instead ("Busiest: **9p–12a** — Intense"), leaving the scale row as just the two plain, unchanging endpoint labels with nothing that can duplicate. Re-verified against the same real seeded data (a sharp evening spike, `spikeRatio` clamped to the max): marker lands at exactly 100% of the track's width (measured directly, not eyeballed) and the header correctly reads "Intense" with no repeated wording anywhere. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
  - **A seventh round, a real architectural change: "the busy meter should point to the current time period and base on how many people are live currently, this calculation is different from the bar graph calculation."** Until this point the meter and the bars were computed from the exact same source — the meter just re-read the bars' own historical `spikeRatio`/peak bucket. This explicitly asked for a second, genuinely different signal: a live, right-now concurrent-student count, decoupled entirely from the bars' cumulative all-time histogram. **New data path, since nothing already fetched could answer "how many people are online this exact second"**: `fetchLiveCount()` (`lib/supabase.js`) — a plain count query against `pomo_active_session` using the *exact* same is-live definition `fetchLiveStatusByEmail` already established (`running AND phase_end_at` still in the future — reused, not redefined, so the two can't disagree on what "live" means), just counted globally instead of looked up for a specific list of emails. Folded into `tracker-data.js`'s initial batch (`liveCount`) for the first paint, plus a new standalone `live-count.js` endpoint (same "small independently-pollable endpoint" pattern as `last-week-leaders.js`) for a genuine live poll afterward — this needed its OWN poll loop (`LIVE_COUNT_POLL_MS`, 60s) rather than reusing `LEADERBOARD_POLL_MS`/`LAST_WEEK_POLL_MS`, since neither of those two existing polls covers both contexts the Today card actually renders in (`renderCalendar` calls `renderTodayCard` from both the Focus Mode branch and the plain-checklist branch — confirmed by reading the call sites directly before assuming) — `startLiveCountPoll()` runs unconditionally from `init()`, gated only on tab visibility, not on `state.focus` the way the other two are. **The meter's own calculation, `hourlyBusyMeterHtml()`, is now a separate function from the bars**, reading `state.liveCount` instead of the bars' `buckets`/`spikeRatio` — `LIVE_COUNT_INTENSITY_CAP` (10 concurrent = fully "Intense" on the gauge) and `LIVE_COUNT_VIBE_THRESHOLD` (5+ = "Intense") are both explicitly flagged as starting, tunable guesses in the code, since this project has no existing baseline for typical concurrent-live-student counts to calibrate against yet. **"Point to the current time period"**: the header now reads "Right now (**3p–6p**): **N** live — Vibe", using the *viewer's own local hour* (`hourlyCurrentBucketIdx()`, `Math.floor(new Date().getHours()/3)` — same "just trust the browser's local clock" convention `todayIso()` already relies on for this India-based audience, no server round-trip needed) rather than the historically-busiest bucket; the bars themselves also gained a small dot marking whichever bar covers this same current-hour bucket (`hourly-bar-col--now`), visually tying the two together — replacing the old peak-bucket highlight, which pointed at a different concept (historically busiest) that no longer exists on the bars now that the meter has moved off spikeRatio entirely. `#hourly-busy-meter-wrap` wraps just the meter (not the bars, not the checklist) so each poll tick patches only that piece, same "don't replay the whole card's fade-in" discipline every other poll on this page already follows. Verified directly against real production data, not synthetic: `curl`ing `/api/live-count` returned a genuine live count (12-13, fluctuating between two calls seconds apart — real students actually starting/finishing sessions in real time, not a static number); loading the real page confirmed zero console errors, the header correctly read "Right now (3p–6p): 12 live — Intense" matching the viewer's actual local hour (17:xx → bucket 5 → 3p-6p, confirmed via `new Date().getHours()` read directly, not assumed), the marker at exactly 100% of the track (12 ≥ the 10-cap), and the "3p" bar carrying the new dot — while the bars' own heights (still the historical mock data seeded earlier) were completely unaffected by any of this, confirming the two calculations are now genuinely independent, not just differently-labeled versions of the same number. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
  - **An eighth round, immediately after: "don't use the live-intense thing text. use the text busy meter and use hourly intervals to calculate not three hourly."** Two separate changes. **Wording**: the dynamic "Right now (3p–6p): N live — Intense" sentence is gone — replaced with a plain static title ("Busy meter", matching "Activity by hour"'s own uppercase label styling above the bars) plus a small subtitle carrying just the numbers. The Chill/Intense read itself wasn't removed as a concept, only as a spelled-out sentence — it still comes through via the track's fixed gradient + the marker's position + the "Chill"/"Intense" endpoint labels, same mechanism as before. `LIVE_COUNT_VIBE_THRESHOLD` (the now-unused constant that used to pick the word) was deleted outright rather than left as dead code. **Granularity**: the meter's own "right now" range switched from the bars' 3-hour bucket math (`Math.floor(hour/3)*3`, e.g. "3p–6p") to a genuine single hour (`new Date().getHours()`, e.g. "6p–7p") — a separate, more precise calculation from `hourlyCurrentBucketIdx()`, which still drives the bars' own "now" dot and deliberately stays on 3-hour buckets (that's a chart-density concern for 8 vs. 24 bars, unrelated to how precisely the meter itself should describe the current moment). Verified against the real page: title reads exactly "Busy meter", subtitle reads "6p–7p · 13 live now" (confirmed as a genuine 1-hour window, not the bars' 3-hour one), zero console errors, and a screenshot showing the redesigned meter reading cleanly under the bars with no dangling "Intense"/"Chill" sentence anywhere. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
  - **A ninth round, straight from a screenshot of that exact result: "align the heading properly, don't show the no of live users and the hourly interval. just busy meter and the meter. also make the meter animations flawless and align and space it properly according to the content above."** Four things at once. **Heading alignment**: "Busy meter" was `text-align: center`, while "Activity by hour" right above it (same card) was left-aligned by default — two titles in one card silently disagreeing on alignment, the same class of mistake already made and fixed once before on this page (Minutes/Streak column headers). Dropped the `text-align: center`. **No live count or hour range shown**: the subtitle line ("6p–7p · 13 live now") from the round just before this is gone entirely — that data still computes `meterPct` and backs the track's own hover tooltip, it's just never printed as on-page text now; just the title and the gauge itself, per the literal ask. **Width/spacing "according to the content above"**: `.hourly-busy-meter` had `max-width: 220px; align-self: center`, sitting narrower and centered under the bars above it, which already span the column's full width — dropped both, so the meter now matches the bars' own full-width footprint exactly (measured: both 248.33px wide on the real page). **The actual flaw in "make the animations flawless"**: the marker previously used a `@keyframes` `animation`, and `refreshLiveCount`'s poll handler regenerated the whole meter's HTML via `innerHTML` every 60s — which meant a brand-new marker element every poll tick, and a `@keyframes` animation replays in full on a freshly-created element regardless of what the *previous* element's position was. So a live update didn't glide from the old value to the new one, it reset to 0% and replayed the entire slide-in from scratch every single poll — the actual "not flawless" part, not a rendering bug. Fixed by switching to a plain CSS `transition: left .8s` on the marker (fires on any property change to an element already in the DOM, which a `@keyframes` animation doesn't need or use) and rewriting both the initial render and `refreshLiveCount` to target the *same, never-recreated* marker element — initial markup renders it at `left:0%` with the real value stashed in `data-meter-pct`, a new `animateHourlyBusyMeter()` (called once from `renderCalendar`'s tail via a double `requestAnimationFrame`, ensuring the browser actually paints the 0% starting state before changing it) sets the real `left` a frame later for the slide-in, and `refreshLiveCount` now just mutates that same element's `left` directly on each poll instead of regenerating any HTML — one continuous transition mechanism for both the first paint and every live update after it, not two different systems. Verified precisely, not just visually: sampled the marker's real computed `left` across several 150ms-apart frames after forcing a value change the same way a poll tick would — 248.33px → 151.06px → 84.70px → 52.78px → ~49.66px, a genuine multi-frame glide toward the new target (≈20% of the 248px track ≈ 49.6px), not an instant jump; separately confirmed `.hourly-busy-meter-title`'s and `.hourly-activity-title`'s `getBoundingClientRect().left` are now byte-identical (608.17px both) and `.hourly-busy-meter`/`.hourly-activity-bars` are the same width (248.33px both), with zero console errors and no `.hourly-busy-meter-sub` element in the DOM at all. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
  - **A tenth round, two more asks: "but why is it showing intense right now" and "make the bar graph colour gradients according to the theme selected for the pomodoro timer."** **The "why"**: the marker's position is `min(100%, liveCount / LIVE_COUNT_INTENSITY_CAP * 100%)` — that cap was set to `10` as an untested starting guess (see the round above), and production genuinely runs 12-14 concurrent live students during normal evening hours, already past that guess, so the gauge was pegged at the max essentially all the time instead of only during a real, unusually busy moment. Raised to `20` — still explicitly a guess in the code's own comment (this project has no historical time-series of concurrent-live-count to calibrate against), just a more generous one; re-verified against the real live count at the time (13) landing at a genuinely mid-scale 65%, not pinned to 100. **Theming**: `.hourly-bar-fill`'s gradient switched from a hardcoded `#C99CFB` → `var(--purple)` pairing to `var(--pomo-g1, #C99CFB)` → `var(--pomo-g2, var(--purple))` — the same per-student Pomodoro ring/timer theme custom properties (`POMO_GRADIENTS`, five presets: Sunset/Ocean/Citrus/Mint/Berry) every other themed element on the page already reads, with the bars' own original 2-stop colors kept as the CSS fallback. **A real gap this surfaced, not just a CSS change**: `applyPomoGradient()` (which sets `--pomo-g1/g2/g3` on `#app`) was only ever called `if (state.focus)` from `renderCalendar` — meaning on the plain checklist (outside Focus Mode), where the Today card and its hourly chart *also* render, those vars were never set at all, so the bars would have silently fallen back to their hardcoded default there regardless of the student's actual picked theme. Widened that call to unconditional — safe per `applyPomoGradient`'s own existing doc comment ("safe to call even when #pomo-card doesn't exist yet — becomes a no-op for whichever element is missing"), so this doesn't affect anything else that already reads these vars. Verified directly, not assumed: loaded the real page at the default Sunset preset and confirmed the bars' computed `background-image` resolves to `linear-gradient(#FF7FB7, #A78BFA)`; seeded `localStorage`'s `taai_pomo_settings` to Ocean (`gradient: 1`) and reloaded, confirming both `#app`'s live `--pomo-g1` (`#22D3EE`) and the bars' own computed gradient (`linear-gradient(#22D3EE, #3B82F6)`) changed to match — a genuine, live theme change reflected in the chart, not just a passing visual check. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
  - **An eleventh round, self-calibrating the cap instead of hand-tuning it again: "can the max be the max of all time encountered, which is updated dynamically and the min be of 0."** The hardcoded `LIVE_COUNT_INTENSITY_CAP` (10, then 20) needed a person to notice it was wrong and manually raise it each time — replaced with a genuine, server-tracked all-time high. `live_count_stats` (new, `supabase/schema.sql`) is one single row (same "tiny, fixed-size, never needs cleanup" shape as `pomo_hourly_activity`), holding `max_count` — updated via `update_live_count_max`, a `GREATEST()`-based atomic UPSERT (same row-level-locking-during-UPDATE pattern as `increment_pomodoro_stats`/`increment_hourly_activity`, so two concurrent requests observing different counts around the same moment can't race each other into recording a lower value over a higher one). `fetchLiveCount()` (`lib/supabase.js`) now calls this on every invocation — meaning the max updates itself the moment ANY request (any viewer's page load or 60s poll) happens to observe a new high, no manual intervention ever needed again. **An accepted, documented imprecision, not a bug**: this can miss a genuine peak that happens to land between two requests, since there's no continuous monitoring — judged acceptable given the existing 60s poll cadence already means this is a "close enough, frequently sampled" number everywhere else on this page too. Frontend: `state.liveCountMax` (new) replaces the constant in `hourlyBusyMeterHtml()`'s `meterPct` calculation (`liveCount / liveCountMax`, clamped to never go below the current live count or below 1, avoiding both a stale-lower-max artifact and a divide-by-zero on a totally fresh table) and in `refreshLiveCount()`'s poll handler, which now also updates `state.liveCountMax` from the response alongside `state.liveCount`. Verified end-to-end, including the pre-migration state (the table doesn't exist in production yet — same standing manual-SQL-editor step every schema change here needs): loaded the real page and confirmed it degrades cleanly to a 0%-position meter with zero console errors (via `tracker-data.js`'s existing `.catch(() => ({count:0, maxCount:0}))` fallback), rather than breaking — once the migration is run, the very next real live-count check anywhere on the site will seed `live_count_stats` with a genuine first value and the meter starts calibrating itself from there. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
  - **Confirmed working against real production data, then immediately optimized away the extra poll it required: "won't i run out of netlify or supabase resources" → "can't we parallelize with some other call?"** Explaining the live-count poll's real cost (small in isolation, but a genuinely NEW always-on background request for every viewer regardless of context, unlike the mutually-exclusive `LEADERBOARD_POLL_MS`/`LAST_WEEK_POLL_MS`) prompted the obvious fix: fold it into those two existing 60s polls instead of running a third one. Together, `pomodoro-leaderboard.js` (Focus Mode) and `last-week-leaders.js` (the plain checklist) already cover every context the Today card's busy meter can appear in — so both now also call `fetchLiveCount()` (wrapped in its own `.catch(() => ({count:0, maxCount:0}))`, same discipline as `tracker-data.js`'s fallback, so a live-count hiccup can never fail the leaderboard or champions card it's riding along on) and return it alongside their own data, exactly the same "piggyback instead of a new round-trip" reasoning `todayLeaders` already established on the leaderboard endpoint. The standalone `live-count.js` endpoint and its entire dedicated poll (`LIVE_COUNT_POLL_MS`, `startLiveCountPoll`/`stopLiveCountPoll`, the unconditional `init()` call, its own `visibilitychange` branch) are deleted outright — a new shared `applyLiveCountUpdate()` function (patches the same never-recreated marker element, same reasoning as before) is called from `refreshLeaderboard`'s and `refreshLastWeekChampions`'s own `.then` handlers instead. **One real gap this surfaced, fixed before shipping**: `refreshLastWeekChampions()` used to bail out immediately (`if (!rows) return`) whenever `#champions-rows` wasn't in the DOM (a real, if now-rare, case — no last-week data at all yet) — which would have silently skipped fetching the live count too, even though the busy meter can exist perfectly well on a page with no champions card. Widened the guard to only skip when BOTH `#champions-rows` and the meter's marker are absent. Verified directly against the real page: confirmed only one `/api/tracker-data` request fires on initial load (no separate live-count round-trip at all anymore), `curl`ed both `pomodoro-leaderboard` and `last-week-leaders` directly and confirmed each now returns a real `liveCount: {count, maxCount}` object alongside its own data, and confirmed the old `/api/live-count` path now 404s (genuinely removed, not just unused). Net effect: the self-calibrating meter from the round above ships with zero net increase in background polling — the same total request count as before this whole busy-meter feature existed. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
  - **A twelfth, small round: "can the knob on the busy meter look better?"** Was a 12px circle with a dark `--card`-colored fill and a plain 2px `--ink` border — flat, easy to lose against the gradient track underneath it. Redesigned as a proper slider-handle: a flat solid white fill (deliberately not a gradient — the gradient-swatch saga earlier this session already established that any gradient fill on a circle this small reads as a muddy "shape inside the ball," not a clean color), a dark ring (`box-shadow: 0 0 0 3px`, a zero-blur shadow used as a solid outline) for separation from the track regardless of which color it's currently sitting over, and a soft drop shadow layered in the same `box-shadow` declaration for a bit of tactile depth. Both layers are static, never animated — only `left` moves, via the existing transition — so this stays outside the box-shadow+border-radius DOM-leak gotcha despite the element carrying both. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
  - **A one-time, server-side mock-data cutover for launch, requested alongside the deploy itself: "use the mock data till 7pm tomorrow and then update it to the real data calculated in between till forever. also keep the busy meter as it is."** The busy meter needed no changes at all — it's already 100% real, live data (`live_count_stats` was never seeded with anything fake), so "keep it as it is" was already true by construction. The bar chart's mock data (seeded earlier this session so the chart isn't empty on day one) needed a real mechanism to phase itself out automatically, without depending on this conversation still being active 24+ hours later — a `ScheduleWakeup`-style "come back later" approach wasn't appropriate here since this needs to survive regardless of whether any agent session is running, the same reasoning that makes every other time-based action in this project (midnight reload, daily streak snapshot, credit-failure cleanup) a real scheduled server function, not something an agent revisits. `hourly-activity-mock-cutover.js` (new, cron `*/15 * * * *`) checks a fixed deadline (7:00 PM IST, 2026-09-22 — the day after launch) and, once past it, **subtracts** the exact known mock baseline back out of every one of `pomo_hourly_activity`'s 24 hour-buckets, rather than overwriting them with a fresh snapshot — real students' sessions increment those same rows the whole time between launch and the cutover (the increment RPC has no way to distinguish "real" from "seeded mock," nor should it), so subtracting the known baseline is the only way to recover just the real contribution without also discarding everything genuine that accumulated in between. `mockBaselineForHour()` reproduces the *exact same formula* the original seeding script used, rather than hand-transcribing 24 numbers by hand — zero risk of a transcription mismatch between what was actually inserted and what gets subtracted back out. **Idempotency, since this runs every 15 minutes and could easily overlap invocations**: `hourly_activity_cutover` (new, one row) plus `claim_hourly_activity_cutover` (new RPC) — an atomic UPSERT whose `ON CONFLICT` branch is guarded by `WHERE done = false`, so a second, racing invocation's claim attempt matches zero rows once the first has already flipped it to `true` and gets nothing back from `RETURNING`, telling it to skip the subtraction rather than risk running it twice. Verified the pre-deadline path directly against the real endpoint (`{"ok":true,"skipped":"before cutover","cutoverAt":"2026-09-22T13:30:00.000Z"}`) — **deliberately did NOT force-test the actual subtraction against real production data**, to avoid any risk of performing the real cutover early against the explicit "till 7pm tomorrow" instruction; the mechanism reuses the same atomic claim-once pattern already proven elsewhere in this codebase (`credit_pomodoro_phase`), so it ships on code-review confidence plus the confirmed-correct deadline gate, not a forced live trial run. Requires the same standing manual SQL-editor step as every other schema change here before the deadline arrives. Safe to leave running indefinitely after the cutover fires — once `done`, every 15-minute invocation is just one cheap read against a 1-row table.
  - **"Can the bar graph and busy meter have first visit scroll animations?"** Both entrance effects (the bars' grow-in, the meter's slide-in) fired the instant their markup landed in the DOM, at `renderCalendar`'s synchronous tail — completely invisible whenever the Today card sat below the fold on open, which it very often does (the same reasoning `CONFETTI_KEY_PREFIX`'s own comment already documents for the leaderboard card). By the time anyone actually scrolled down to see it, both animations had already finished playing to an empty viewport. Fixed with the same `IntersectionObserver`-on-scroll-into-view pattern this page's own `.fade-in` elements already use (`fadeObserver`/`observeFadeIns`), via a new, parallel `hourlyRevealObserver`/`observeHourlyActivityReveal()` — triggers once `.hourly-activity` actually intersects, then unobserves (a one-shot reveal, same as every `.fade-in`). **The bars**: `.hourly-bar-fill` now starts with `animation-play-state: paused` (held at the keyframe's own "from" state via the existing `fill-mode: both`) instead of playing immediately, only flipping to `running` once an ancestor `.hourly-activity.in-view` class is present — deliberately *pausing* the existing animation rather than reassigning `animation` conditionally, so the staggered `--grow-delay` only starts counting down once actually revealed, keeping the cascade looking right regardless of how long the section sat off-screen first. **The meter**: `animateHourlyBusyMeter()` is called from the observer's callback instead of unconditionally at `renderCalendar`'s tail — the function itself is unchanged, only *when* it fires moved. Verified precisely with a deliberately short 413px-tall viewport to force the section below the fold: before any scroll, `.in-view` absent, bar fill computed height `0px` with `animationPlayState: "paused"`, marker still at `left: 0px`; after scrolling the section into view and letting the animations run, `.in-view` present, a real bar height (`6.375px`, correctly proportional to that bar's own intensity), `animationPlayState: "running"`, and the marker correctly at its real target position (`216.031px`, matching its `data-meter-pct` of 87%) — a genuine reveal-on-scroll, not just reasoned about. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
  - **"Can the busy meter have equal font size as activity by hour maintaining equal spacing?"** `.hourly-busy-meter-title` was `10.5px`/`12px` margin-bottom against `.hourly-activity-title`'s `11px`/`14px` — close enough to look "roughly similar" but not the byte-identical pair they should read as, sitting one above the other in the same card. Matched both values exactly. Verified via computed styles, not eyeballed: `getComputedStyle` on both titles now returns `fontSize: "11px"` and `marginBottom: "14px"` for each, confirmed equal.
  - **"After a certain threshold of busy meter in the intense side can we have a fire emoji, on top of the knob maintaining equal spacing?"** `BUSY_METER_FIRE_THRESHOLD` (70, solidly past the midpoint, not just barely leaning intense) gates a new `.hourly-busy-meter-marker--fire` class on the knob, rendering a 🔥 via a CSS `::after` (`content: '🔥'`) rather than a separate DOM element — since it's part of the marker itself, it automatically tracks the knob's own `left` position (and its transition) with zero extra JS, rather than needing its own separate position-syncing logic. `top: -18px` deliberately reuses the same spacing the now-removed bar-chart peak flame used, for visual consistency with that earlier (reverted) pattern rather than picking a new arbitrary offset. **Wired into both places the knob's position can change**, not just the initial render: `hourlyMeterMarkerClass()` sets the class at render time, and `applyLiveCountUpdate()` (the same function that moves the knob on every 60s poll tick) now also toggles the class via `classList.toggle` — a real session starting or finishing that pushes the live count across the threshold updates the flame immediately on the next poll, not just at page load. Verified against the real page (currently sitting at a genuine 73%, already past the threshold): confirmed the class and the computed `::after` content both present, then forced the knob through a low (20%, no flame) and a high (90%, flame) position via the same DOM-patching path `applyLiveCountUpdate` uses, screenshotting both — the flame correctly appears/disappears with the knob's actual position, and isn't clipped by the card's own `overflow: hidden` at either extreme of the track. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
  - **A real bug, caught from a screenshot showing the dot on "9p" well after 9pm had passed: "this is not refreshing every hour to point to the correct hour."** `hourlyCurrentBucketIdx()` was only ever evaluated at full-render time (inside `pomoHourlyActivityHtml`, called from `renderCalendar`) — a tab left open across an hour boundary with no other reason to fully re-render (this page's own docs already note students keep it open 12-14 hours daily) would keep the dot on whichever bucket was current at the last render, not the real one. New `refreshHourlyNowIndicator()` patches the DOM directly (removes `.hourly-bar-col--now`/its dot from wherever they currently sit, adds them to the bucket that's actually current now), a no-op when the bucket genuinely hasn't changed — same "patch in place, don't regenerate a `.fade-in` ancestor for a routine update" discipline this page already uses elsewhere. **Deliberately not its own new interval** — piggybacked onto `applyLiveCountUpdate()`, called from its very top regardless of whether there's live-count data that tick, since that function already fires reliably every 60s in both contexts the Today card can appear in (Focus Mode via `refreshLeaderboard`, the checklist via `refreshLastWeekChampions`) — the same "two existing polls already cover every context" reasoning that already replaced the busy meter's own dedicated poll a few rounds back. Up to a minute of staleness right at the top of an hour is an acceptable, unnoticeable tradeoff for reusing an already-proven-reliable cadence instead of adding new scheduling machinery. Verified precisely: forced the dot onto a deliberately wrong bucket (simulating exactly the staleness reported), ran the fix's own algorithm against the real DOM, and confirmed it moved the dot to the real current hour's bucket (9pm → bucket 7 of 8) with exactly one dot left in the document — the stale one was genuinely removed, not just a second one added on top. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
  - **"Can this dot also be according to theme?"** `.hourly-bar-now-dot` was a hardcoded `var(--purple)` regardless of the student's picked Pomodoro theme, the one piece of the hourly chart the earlier theming round missed (the bars themselves already read `--pomo-g1/g2`). Switched to `var(--pomo-g2, var(--purple))` — the same var the Reset/Skip button icons already use for their own accent color, with `--purple` kept as the fallback. Verified with the same Sunset-vs-Ocean technique used for the bars: Sunset (default) renders `rgb(167, 139, 250)` — byte-identical to before, since Sunset's own `--pomo-g2` literally is `var(--purple)`; picking Ocean renders `rgb(59, 130, 246)`, its own real blue. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
