- **Hovering a leaderboard name shows that student's all-time total focus minutes** ("whenever someone hovers over someone's name can we show them their total minutes or is it too much resource intensive," clarified via a follow-up question to mean the all-time total across the whole program, not a live per-hover query — the weekly/daily boards already show that week's/day's total right next to the name, so all-time was the genuinely new number). Powered by a new cached `students.all_time_minutes` column (`supabase/schema.sql`), kept correct the same way `current_streak` already is: incremented atomically (`increment_student_all_time_minutes`, a plain `UPDATE ... RETURNING`, no `ON CONFLICT` needed since a session can only ever be credited to a student who already has a row) from `pomodoro-complete.js` right alongside the existing week/day/hourly-activity increments — best-effort, wrapped in its own try/catch, same reasoning as the hourly-activity increment beside it: a supplementary display number, never something a student is owed credit for, so a failure here must never turn an already-successful completion into an error response. One-time backfill (`UPDATE students SET all_time_minutes = COALESCE(SUM(pomodoro_stats.total_minutes), 0) ...`) makes the number correct from the moment this ships, not just counting forward from zero for students with months of real history already. Shown as a plain `title` attribute on `.leaderboard-name` (`allTimeTitleAttr()`, `progress.js`) across all three leaderboard render functions (weekly top-20, today's top-10, last-week champions) plus each board's own viewer-rank row — a native browser tooltip needs zero extra markup/JS/styling for something this supplementary, unlike the streak-legend's own popover (that one needed to show 5 rows of structured data, this is one number). **A real fault-tolerance bug caught and fixed before shipping, not after**: the first pass added `all_time_minutes` directly to each leaderboard's main `students` select (`email, display_name, current_streak, all_time_minutes`) — checked directly against production and confirmed the migration hadn't been run yet (`column students.all_time_minutes does not exist`), which would have made that same select fail outright and 500 the *entire* leaderboard endpoint (display_name, streak, everything) the moment this deployed, before the SQL migration ever ran — a much bigger blast radius than the supplementary number it was meant to add. Fixed by pulling it into its own, separate, best-effort query — `fetchAllTimeMinutesByEmail()` (new, `lib/supabase.js`), shared by `pomodoro-leaderboard.js`, `fetchLastWeekLeaders`, and `fetchTodayLeaders` — that swallows its own error and returns `{}` (every caller already treats a missing entry as 0) rather than letting a missing column take down `display_name`/`current_streak` rendering with it. Verified directly against real production (pre-migration): `/api/pomodoro-leaderboard` and `/api/last-week-leaders` both return full, correct leaderboards with every other field intact and `all_time_minutes: 0` on every row (not a 500), and a real headless-Chrome load of the page confirmed `.leaderboard-name[title]` spans render with `title="0.0h all-time"` — the tooltip mechanism itself works correctly, just showing the honest pre-migration number. **Requires the same standing manual SQL-editor step as every other schema change here** before real non-zero totals start showing — the `ALTER TABLE`/backfill/`increment_student_all_time_minutes` block in `supabase/schema.sql`, right after `update_student_streaks`. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped. The migration was run against production the same day; a fresh query confirmed real backfilled totals (top student: 23,362 all-time minutes ≈ 389h) and the leaderboard endpoints correctly returning them.
  - **Switched from the native `title` attribute to a custom-styled tooltip the same day, after a real, repeated report ("still nothing on hover") that survived multiple rounds of troubleshooting.** The `title` attribute genuinely was present and correct the whole time (confirmed directly, several times, via headless-Chrome DOM inspection against real production data) — the actual problem was that the browser's *native* tooltip rendering is too unreliable to depend on: inconsistent dwell-time timing across browsers/OSes, easy to miss entirely, and — the thing that made this hard to diagnose remotely — a screenshot can't even confirm whether it fired, since the OS/browser chrome layer that renders it isn't necessarily part of what a screenshot tool captures. Rather than keep chasing a browser-specific quirk with no way to reproduce the user's exact environment, replaced the mechanism outright: `allTimeTitleAttr()` (`progress.js`) now emits a `data-alltime="X.Xh all-time"` attribute instead of `title`, and a new `setupAllTimeTooltip()` renders a real, deliberately-styled tooltip off it — a single shared `.alltime-tooltip` element (lazily created once, not one per row), positioned via `position: fixed` off the hovered element's own `getBoundingClientRect()`, appended directly to `<body>`. **`position: fixed` + `<body>` specifically, not a CSS `::after` on `.leaderboard-name` itself**: that element already has `overflow: hidden` for its own ellipsis-truncation, which would clip a pseudo-element tooltip outright, and — a real, checked-for gotcha, not a hypothetical one — `.streak-champions-card` (the wrapper around `#champions-card`'s rows) also has its own `overflow: hidden`, which would clip an absolutely-positioned-within-the-card tooltip for rows near either edge; `position: fixed` off `<body>` sidesteps both. Bound via delegation on `document` (`mouseover`/`mouseout`, not `mouseenter`/`mouseleave` — those don't bubble, so can't be delegated this way), called exactly once from `init()`, rather than bound per-row — necessary since leaderboard rows are wholesale regenerated on every poll tick across three separate boards, which would otherwise mean constantly re-binding (and leaking) per-element listeners. Flips to rendering below the element instead of above when there's no room above (`rect.top - tipH - 8 < 8`), and clamps horizontally to stay within the viewport. Verified with a real synthetic hover, not just a DOM/attribute check like the earlier `title`-based verification (which had already proven correct and still wasn't enough to resolve the report) — CDP's `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` (a genuine synthetic pointer move, the same technique already proven elsewhere on this page to actually drive `:hover`/real mouse events, unlike a JS-dispatched `MouseEvent` which doesn't) moved onto a real name span and confirmed the tooltip element gained `opacity: 1`/the `visible` class with the correct text and position, then moving away confirmed it correctly hid again. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
  - **Tooltip text reformatted the same day** ("it should be ALL TIME: 211h") — `allTimeTitleAttr()` switched from `"X.Xh all-time"` (one decimal, lowercase, unit-then-label) to `"ALL TIME: Xh"` (uppercase label first, whole-number hours) to match the literal example given. Verified against real production data: a real student ("magles_dash") showed `ALL TIME: 211h`, an exact match to the reported example. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.

Styled `.alltime-tooltip-label` in `var(--ink-soft)` (the page's existing muted-caption color) and `.alltime-tooltip-value` in `var(--purple)` bold, the site's own accent color, so the hours figure reads as the actual point of the tooltip rather than the label. Verified with the same real-synthetic-hover technique as the tooltip's original build: `getComputedStyle` on both spans for a real student confirmed the label renders `rgb(121,121,138)` (`--ink-soft`) and the value `rgb(167,139,250)` (`--purple`), with text reading "All time" / "266h" — genuinely two differently-colored pieces, not a screenshot guess. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
  - **"The colour should be according to theme" — the very next message, since the hours figure had just shipped hardcoded to `var(--purple)` regardless of the student's picked Pomodoro theme, the same class of gap already fixed once this session for the hourly bars and the "now" dot.** `.alltime-tooltip-value` switched to `var(--pomo-g2, var(--purple))`, same var/fallback pattern as everything else this session's theming rounds touched. **A real, structural gap this surfaced that those earlier fixes didn't need to handle**: `applyPomoGradient()` only ever wrote `--pomo-g1/g2/g3/glow` onto `#app` and `#pomo-card` — every previously-themed element (bars, buttons, the countdown chip, the "now" dot) lives inside `#app`, so that was always enough before. The all-time tooltip is the first themed element that lives entirely outside `#app` by design (appended directly to `<body>`, specifically so ancestors like `.streak-champions-card`'s own `overflow: hidden` can't clip it — see the tooltip's original build), and a CSS custom property set on `#app` is invisible to anything outside it, no matter how the fallback chain is written. Fixed by widening `applyPomoGradient()`'s target list to include `document.documentElement` (`:root`) alongside `#app`/`#pomo-card` — `:root` is the one place a custom property reaches every element regardless of DOM position, so this covers the tooltip today and any future themed element that isn't a descendant of `#app` without needing another special case. `applyPomoGradient()` is already called unconditionally from `renderCalendar()`'s tail (both the checklist and Focus Mode branches — confirmed by reading the call site directly, not assumed), so no new call site was needed. Verified with a real synthetic hover under two presets: Sunset (default) renders `rgb(167,139,250)` — byte-identical to before this change, since Sunset's own `--pomo-g2` literally is `var(--purple)` — and, after switching `localStorage`'s `taai_pomo_settings.gradient` to Ocean and reloading, the same hover renders `rgb(59,130,246)`, Ocean's real blue, matching the exact value already established for the "now" dot's own Ocean check earlier this session. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
- **The page-load/month-navigation loading state became an animated skeleton screen instead of plain text** ("instead of loading your roadmap... it should be animated skeletons while loading page"). Was `<p class="center-note">Loading your roadmap…</p>` in two places: `loadMonth()` (`progress.js`, fires on every real page load AND every month-nav click) and `gate-da-progress-tracker.html`'s static initial `#app` markup (the page's very first paint, before `progress.js` has even run at all). Both replaced with the same skeleton — a rough approximation of the real layout (a hero-card-shaped block for the streak/champions card, a pill for the month nav, a card block for Today, then a two-column area mirroring `.page-grid`: a few week-label+row groups on the left, two card-shaped blocks on the right for the heatmap/subject-breakdown) rather than a pixel-exact recreation, since its only job is reading as "content is coming," not matching real content dimensions. `.skeleton-block`'s shimmer is a `background-position` sweep on an oversized 3-stop gradient (`var(--card)` → translucent white → `var(--card)`) — the same category of technique this page's own `streak-ball-diamond-shimmer` already uses, not a JS-driven effect, so it's cheap and keeps running smoothly regardless of what the actual data fetch is doing. Respects `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` (freezes to a flat `var(--card)`, no animation). **Kept as two hand-duplicated copies, not generated once and reused** — `loadingSkeletonHtml()` (`progress.js`) and the static HTML skeleton in `gate-da-progress-tracker.html` are byte-identical by construction, but the static one exists specifically to paint *before* any JS runs, so there's no way to generate it from the JS function for that first paint; same established tradeoff as this file's other small hand-duplicated pairs (e.g. `DEFAULT_BODY_BY_SOURCE`), flagged with a comment in both places to keep them in sync if this ever changes. Verified via headless Chrome: sampled the DOM ~150ms after navigating to the page and confirmed the static skeleton is already present with a real running `skeleton-shimmer` animation (not just inert placeholder boxes); confirmed it's correctly replaced by real content once the fetch resolves; and separately clicked the month-nav Prev button and confirmed the *same* skeleton reappears ~150ms into that reload too, proving the JS-generated copy (`loadingSkeletonHtml()`, exercised only by month navigation, not the initial static paint) renders correctly as well. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped.
  - **"Make the skeletons better" — a real screenshot (taken via CDP with the `/tracker-data` request deliberately held open, so the skeleton would still be visible to capture) surfaced two genuine problems, not just a look-and-feel request, 2026-09-21.** (1) **A real layout bug, not a design nitpick**: `.skeleton-nav`'s computed width was `2px`, rendering as a near-invisible sliver instead of a pill — root cause was `margin: 0 auto` combined with relying on flex stretch (`max-width: 260px`, no explicit `width`) inside `.skeleton-wrap`'s column flex; per the flex spec, an `auto` margin on a flex item's *cross* axis (here, left/right, since the container is a column flex) opts that item OUT of stretch entirely, so it fell back to shrink-to-fit its own empty content — just its 1px+1px border, hence exactly `2px`. Fixed with an explicit `width: 260px` instead of `max-width` + stretch, the normal way to pair `margin: auto` centering with a fixed-width element in the first place. (2) **Contrast**: `--card` (`#1e1e24`) and the page's own `--bg-soft` (`#1b1b20`) sit only 3 hex units apart — fine for a real card (which reads via its border against real content around it), but meant every skeleton block nearly vanished into the page background, and the original 3-stop `var(--card)`→translucent-white→`var(--card)` shimmer (sized to the whole block) read as a faint wobble rather than a visible sweep, confirmed directly from the screenshot rather than assumed. Rebuilt with a visibly-lighter base fill (`rgba(255,255,255,0.05)` over the border) and a narrower, brighter shimmer band (`rgba(255,255,255,0.16)`, sized to 60% of the block's width, no longer the whole 200%) swept directionally via `background-position` — reads as a clear, deliberate highlight passing over the block, including in a single still frame, rather than a wide gradient nudging back and forth. **A third, smaller polish pass**: added `.skeleton-row--md`/`.skeleton-row--sm` width-variant modifiers (82%/60% of full width) applied to specific rows in both the static and JS-generated markup, so the week-row placeholders read as varying-length real content (names, task titles) instead of a monotonous stack of identical full-width bars — done via explicit modifier classes on specific rows rather than `:nth-child`, since `.skeleton-week-label` is an earlier sibling in the same group and would throw off a pure position-based selector. Re-verified with a fresh screenshot after all three fixes: the nav pill genuinely measures `260×40` now (not `2×40`), the shimmer reads as a clear bright diagonal band sweeping across each block, and the row widths visibly vary — a real, legible skeleton screen instead of near-invisible boxes. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
  - **"The skeletons look too glass and bland" — the very next message, since the contrast fix above kept the exact recipe (a translucent white overlay + `border: 1px solid var(--line)` on a dark block) that IS glassmorphism, the same look this page's whole card system was deliberately converted away from earlier in this project's history (see the glassmorphic-to-minimalist entry in this file).** Fixing visibility hadn't fixed the underlying idiom mismatch. Rebuilt `.skeleton-block` as a genuinely solid, opaque fill (`background-color: #2a2a33`, no border at all — a border is exactly the other half of what reads as a "glass pane" outline) instead of a translucent rgba layer over `--card`, matching the flat idiom every real card on this page already uses. "Bland" addressed by tinting the shimmer band the site's own brand purple (`rgba(167,139,250,0.32)`, `--purple`'s own RGB) instead of plain white — reads as texture that belongs to this specific site's identity (the same accent used for streak numbers, buttons, the countdown chip) rather than a generic loading-library effect that could belong to any dark-themed app. Verified with a fresh screenshot: blocks now read as solid flat shapes with a clear violet-tinted sweep, no border/translucency anywhere. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
  - **"The number of skeletons are too much" — trimmed from 12 blocks down to 7, same day.** Dropped the second week-group entirely (was 2 groups totaling 5 rows + 2 labels; now 1 group, label + 2 rows) and the side column's second (hero-sized) block, leaving just its one card-sized block. Applied identically to both the static HTML skeleton and `loadingSkeletonHtml()` in `progress.js`, keeping the two byte-identical as already established. Verified via headless Chrome: `document.querySelectorAll('.skeleton-block').length` reads `7` (down from `12`), and a fresh screenshot shows a proportionate, uncluttered loading state instead of a dense stack. `CLIENT_VERSION` bumped again.
- **Local dev needs `netlify dev`, not Docker** — the plain static server (`npx serve`) can't run `/api/*` Netlify Functions, so use `npx netlify-cli dev --port 8080` for anything touching `/gate-da-progress-tracker`. `netlify.toml` has a `[dev] publish = "."` block specifically so `netlify dev` serves source (live JSX transpile) like the plain server does, not `dist/`. **A local Docker Supabase stack (`supabase start`) used to be required for this — it isn't anymore.** `.env` points `SUPABASE_URL`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY` at the real cloud project (see below) for both local testing and production, so there's nothing to spin up locally at all. Only reach for `supabase start` again if there's a specific reason to test against a disposable/throwaway DB instead of the real one (it cost ~9GB of Docker images and needed manual teardown every session — avoid unless actually needed).
- **Production Supabase is set up** (as of 2026-07, project ref `ureoahmpgzmtqfnpirdi`) — `supabase/schema.sql` has been run against it, and it's the same project `.env` uses locally. Project-creation security settings: Data API **on** (required — that's what `supabase-js` actually talks to), "Automatically expose new tables" **off** (the frontend never talks to Supabase directly, only through `service_role`-authenticated functions, so there's no reason for the public `anon` role to see these tables at all), automatic RLS **on** (costs nothing since `service_role` bypasses RLS regardless — it's a default-deny backstop in case a table's public exposure ever changes by accident; this table holds student emails). Two gotchas from setting it up: (1) Supabase's newer UI defaults to `sb_publishable_...`/`sb_secret_...` format API keys, but `@supabase/supabase-js` v2.45 (what's pinned in `package.json`) was built/tested against the older JWT-format keys — grab the `service_role` key from the **"Legacy anon, service_role API keys"** tab, not the new default one. (2) The Project URL shown in the dashboard's "Data API" settings includes a trailing `/rest/v1/` — strip that before putting it in `SUPABASE_URL`, the client library appends that path itself. **Netlify's own dashboard env vars still need to be set separately** (same two values) for the *live* site to work — `.env` only affects local `netlify dev`, it has no bearing on the deployed site.
- **`weekly-rank-snapshot.js` (added 2026-09-06, first step of the cost-reduction plan — see "Future considerations" below) caches each closed week's final rank** (`pomodoro_stats.final_rank`), and `pomodoro-leaderboard.js`'s `previous_week_rank` now reads it (switched over the same day) instead of re-fetching and re-ranking every active student's whole previous week on every 30-second poll, all week, for data that can never change once the week is over. Runs daily (`19:30` UTC = `01:00` IST, comfortably after midnight so the previous week has definitely closed) rather than a single precisely-timed once-a-week firing — self-healing if a run ever fails, and idempotent regardless (`compute_weekly_final_ranks`, a Postgres function, always recomputes cleanly). **`ROW_NUMBER()`, not `RANK()` — a real bug caught before it shipped, not a style choice.** The old JS behavior it replaced (a plain array index over `.order('total_minutes', { ascending: false })`) assigns strictly sequential 1,2,3,4... with no tie-awareness; `RANK()` instead gives every tied student the *same* rank and skips the following numbers (competition-style). Caught by diffing this function's real output against the old live-computed one before cutover: four students tied at 120 minutes diverged by exactly that skip (rank 47 vs. the old arbitrary 47-50). Fixed by switching to `ROW_NUMBER()`, then re-verified as an exact match, full leaderboard response byte-for-byte identical (aside from live-status timestamp fields, which legitimately differ between two separate real requests). Also cuts real bytes even today: 3,568 → 884 bytes for this one query (75.2%) against real production data — and more importantly, the new query is bounded to the ~21 emails this poll actually needs regardless of total active student count, where the old one scaled with the whole active population (projected ~20KB+ at a hypothetical 300 active students; this one stays flat). **`weekly-rank-snapshot.js` was deployed first as write-only infrastructure, verified in isolation, before this read-side switch was made** — deliberately kept as two separate, independently-verified changes rather than one combined one.

## Discord bot + /team console (added 2026-08-29 through 2026-09-02)

Posts to Discord automatically, reusing the progress tracker's existing leaderboard data. **Rearchitected 2026-09-02** from 3 hardcoded, fixed-cron functions into a general scheduler (`scheduled_posts` table + `discord-dispatch.js`), managed from a new authenticated page (`/team`) — driven by the ask to control "any post in any channel... text, tagging, date, time, frequency, repeatability," not just the original 3.

- **Why a dispatcher, not one cron per post**: Netlify Scheduled Functions run on a cron baked into `netlify.toml` at *deploy* time — a page can't change when a function fires, only what it does when it fires. So a user-editable schedule can't map to "one cron per post." Instead, `netlify/functions/discord-dispatch.js` runs on one fixed, frequent cron (`*/15 * * * *`, every 15 minutes — see `netlify.toml`) and queries `scheduled_posts` for anything due (`enabled AND next_fire_at <= now()`), firing each row independently (one row's failure doesn't block the others) and recomputing `next_fire_at` for recurring types via `computeNextFireAt()` in `lib/supabase.js` (`'once'` posts just get `enabled = false` instead).
- **`scheduled_posts`** (`supabase/schema.sql`): one row per post. `source` is either `'custom'` (literal `title`/`body` text) or one of 4 built-in dynamic sources — `'daily_leader'` (yesterday's single top student), `'daily_leaderboard'` (yesterday's top 3, added 2026-09-02 once a single winner wasn't enough), `'weekly_leaderboard'` (last week's top 5), `'monthly_consistency'` — which pull live data via the same `fetchTodayLeaders`/`fetchLastWeekLeaders`/`computeMonthlyConsistency` helpers the tracker page itself uses. `title`/`body` are always an optional override template (via `renderTemplate()`) that falls back to the original hardcoded wording when blank. Placeholders: the single-entity sources (`daily_leader`, `monthly_consistency`) get `{{name}}`/`{{hours}}` for the one student, and `body` there fully replaces the default sentence when set. The ranked-list sources (`daily_leaderboard`, `weekly_leaderboard`) get per-position placeholders instead — `{{name}}`/`{{hours}}` for 1st, `{{name2}}`/`{{hours2}}` for 2nd, up through `{{name5}}`/`{{hours5}}` for the weekly top 5 (see `rankedVars()` in `discord-dispatch.js`), an unmatched placeholder (e.g. `{{name4}}` on a top-3 post) just rendering blank rather than erroring — but there, `body` is only an **intro line shown above** the medal list, never a replacement for it (the list itself always renders, by request, matching how `monthly_consistency`'s intro + runner-up list already worked). Each row carries its **own** `webhook_url` — not the shared `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_URL` env var — so different posts can genuinely target different channels; the env var is only `postToDiscordWebhook()`'s fallback default for any caller that doesn't pass one explicitly.
- **Monthly "most consistent student"** (the `'monthly_consistency'` source, reports on the month that just closed — an Oct 1 firing reports September): ranks by **median** daily focus minutes, zero-filled across every calendar day of the month (every day counts as a real work day here, no rest-day concept exists in this schedule), not a raw average, so one huge binge day can't outrank someone who studied moderately but genuinely every day. Only students registered by the **7th** of that month are eligible. Ties broken by total minutes. **August 2026 was deliberately never computed** — `pomo_daily_sessions.total_minutes` only started actually populating on Aug 28 (see the known-gotcha below), so a full-August calculation would zero-fill 27 of 31 days for nearly every student regardless of real activity.
- Built-in sources link back to `https://taai.live/gate-da-progress-tracker` two ways: the embed title itself (Discord makes a titled embed's `url` clickable) and an explicit `[📊 View the progress tracker](...)` markdown link line in the body.
- **`/team`** (`team/index.html` + `team/team.js`) — the only place `scheduled_posts` rows are ever created/edited/deleted (`netlify/functions/team-posts.js`, a role-gated REST-style CRUD function: GET/POST/PUT/DELETE). Gated by **Netlify Identity**, the same login already powering the blog CMS at `/admin` — but requiring the **`admin` role specifically** (`requireAdmin()` in `lib/supabase.js`, checking `context.clientContext.user.app_metadata.roles`), not just "any logged-in Identity user", since a blog writer sharing that same login has no reason to reach student data or webhook controls. The `admin` role is assigned per-user in the Netlify Identity dashboard — a one-time manual step, not something committable (same category as Identity/git-gateway setup itself). Netlify Functions auto-populate `context.clientContext.user` from a valid Identity JWT in the request's `Authorization` header — no custom JWT verification code needed, same mechanism git-gateway already relies on.
- **Mentions**: `scheduled_posts.tag_everyone` (checkbox) and `.extra_mentions` (free text, added 2026-09-02 — `@here`, a role `<@&ROLE_ID>`, a specific person `<@USER_ID>`; there's no Discord API integration to look up names, so the exact raw mention syntax has to be typed/pasted in) combine into one `content` string via `buildMentionContent()` in `lib/supabase.js` (e.g. both set becomes `"@everyone <@&123>"`). `postToDiscordWebhook()` takes this as its `content` argument — Discord silently drops even literal mention text in a webhook's `content` unless the request also sets `allowed_mentions: { parse: [...] }` (a deliberate anti-spam safeguard), which the helper only adds when `content` is actually non-empty. `/team`'s Preview button shows this line too (`team-posts.js`'s `?preview=1` response includes `content` alongside `embed`).
- **A "? Syntax reference" toggle lives on `/team` itself** (`renderReference()` in `team.js`, added 2026-09-02) — sources, placeholders, mention syntax, all documented in the page for whoever's creating a post, not just in this file. Keep it in sync whenever a source/placeholder/field changes, same as this doc.
- **"Send Test" actually posts, "Preview" never does** — both resolve content identically (`resolveScheduledPostEmbed`/`buildMentionContent`), but Preview (`?preview=1`) only returns JSON for the page's own styled approximation, while Send Test (`?test=1`, added 2026-09-02) genuinely calls `postToDiscordWebhook()` — always against the form's separate, optional **`test_webhook_url`** field (persisted per-row purely as a UI convenience, never read by `discord-dispatch.js`), never the row's real `webhook_url`. This is how a wording/mention change gets verified against an actual Discord client's real rendering before it's trusted with the production channel — same discipline as every manual test this session, just built into the page instead of requiring `.env` swapping.
- **Custom posts get top billing over the recurring daily/weekly/monthly ones** (`renderPostList()` in `team.js`, added 2026-09-04) — the built-in leaderboard posts are edited rarely (set once, left alone), while custom posts (a weekly schedule, a one-off announcement) are what actually gets touched often; showing both with equal weight meant re-scanning the same untouched rows every visit. Custom posts (`source: 'custom'`) render first, always visible, under a "Custom announcements" heading; the built-in ones collapse behind a "Show recurring leaderboards (N)" toggle, closed by default (`state.showBuiltIn`) — same collapsed-by-default pattern as the syntax reference panel.
- **The page is organized around channels, not one flat list** (added 2026-09-03): the post list groups rows into a card per channel (`renderPostGroups()` in `team.js`) instead of one undifferentiated list — matters once there's more than one real channel in use, so it's obvious at a glance which Discord channel a given post goes to without reading each row's URL. Grouping key is `webhook_url`, not the `channel_name` text (changed 2026-09-04) — `channel_name` is only ever a display label, so two rows are "the same channel" only if they actually post to the same place; grouping by the label text instead would silently split one real channel into two groups if a row was ever left unlabeled or typo'd. The displayed heading is the first non-blank `channel_name` found in that group (`groupLabel()`), falling back to a shortened webhook tail if every row in the group is unlabeled. **A ✎ button on each heading renames the channel everywhere it's used** (added 2026-09-04, `state.renamingWebhook`/`renameStatus`) — since `channel_name` is duplicated per-row rather than being a single shared entity, renaming means finding every post whose `webhook_url` matches the group and `PUT`-ing each with its full existing payload, only `channel_name` changed (never a partial payload — team-posts.js's PUT handler expects the complete row shape, so reusing each post's own already-fetched object as the base and overriding just that one field is what avoids silently blanking out its webhook/schedule/etc.). The create/edit form is likewise split into 4 labeled sections (Destination / Content / Mentions / Schedule) rather than one long flat list of fields.
- **A 'custom' post can carry multiple subjects' schedules, merged into ONE date-ordered list inside a single embed, via `sections`** (`scheduled_posts.sections JSONB` — `[{title, rows: [{date, task, time}]}]`, added 2026-09-04, restructured same day) — built for the weekly-schedule-to-Discord use case. **Design history matters a lot here — three different layouts were tried and each explicitly rejected before landing on the current one**: subjects side-by-side in one embed's `fields` ("too congested"); each subject as its own separate stacked embed ("the subjects are divided into different cards" — everything needs to read as one card); each subject as its own bold-headed *text section* within the one embed, subject-first order ("the order of mention of everything should be date wise and also mention weekday"). What shipped: `resolveScheduledPostEmbed()`'s `'custom'` branch flattens every section's rows into one list, sorts by `date`, groups consecutive same-date rows, and renders one bold `"Weekday, Mon DD"` heading per calendar date with that date's tasks underneath (subject-prefixed, since one date can span several subjects) — see `formatDateHeading()` and the function itself for the exact algorithm. `date` is a real `<input type="date">` value on the `/team` form (not free text) specifically because reliable chronological sorting and weekday lookup aren't safely derivable from an arbitrary typed string like "15th June" — sorting works as plain string comparison since ISO `YYYY-MM-DD` values sort correctly that way. Every source's branch still returns `[{...}]` (an **array**, usually length 1) from `resolveScheduledPostEmbed()`, and `postToDiscordWebhook(embeds, content, webhookUrl)` still takes that array directly — this plumbing was built for genuine multi-embed support and is harmless to keep even though `'custom'` doesn't currently need more than one embed. `/team`'s create/edit form shows an "Extra sections (optional)" area — only for `source: 'custom'` — with a **Subject** dropdown (added 2026-09-04, was free text — `SUBJECT_OPTIONS` in `team.js`, built from the centralized subject list below, paired with a locally-owned emoji per subject purely for the Discord post's own styling; `subjectOptionsHtml()` always includes the field's current value as a selectable option even when it isn't one of the 10, e.g. free text saved before this became a dropdown, so opening an old post never silently swaps its subject on save — short by design, since it repeats on every one of that subject's lines in the final post now, not shown once as a heading, so module-name detail belongs in the main Title/Body instead) plus a repeatable Date/Topic/Duration table per section (`renderSectionsEditor()`/`readSectionsFromDom()` in `team.js`). No per-section color field — only one embed exists, so only the post's own top-level **Card color** field (added alongside Title/Body in the Content section) applies. Add/Remove reads existing rows back from the DOM first (not from `state.editing`, which is otherwise only synced on submit) so adding one more section doesn't wipe out ones already typed. `sections[].rows` (real date/task/time values) is the only thing persisted per section now — no derived markdown body is stored, so re-opening a saved post for editing just uses the saved rows directly, nothing to parse back out of generated text.
  - **Multi-batch schedules stay outside this system, by design** — there's no "batch" concept anywhere in `schedule_tasks`/`students` (confirmed by checking, not assumed), so a per-batch weekly schedule (e.g. "Batch A" vs "Batch B", each with its own subjects/channel) can't be pulled automatically. The `sections` feature only makes hand-typing that weekly post through `/team` *possible*, not automatic — someone still manually types each week's schedule into the form. Two separate `scheduled_posts` rows (one per batch, each with its own `webhook_url`) is the right setup once real batches exist — same pattern as any other per-channel post, and each is its own single-embed post (see above), never combined into one multi-batch message.
  - **Within a date's task line, duration is an inline-code chip** (`` `1h25m` ``) straight after the task — arrived at through extensive live testing against a real test webhook (not just visual guessing), same discipline as the date-ordering change above. Rejected along the way: inline-parenthesized duration (felt "too intertwined with the text"), a separate line with manual space-character indentation (confirmed unreliable — Discord doesn't render extra space/ideographic-space characters as real indentation), and a monospace code-block table (tried twice, both wrapped badly on real devices — "captured only one subject", "spread out"). No blank line between entries (tried — "not compact"). Two different batches must be **two separate messages**, never combined into one multi-batch post even with a divider between them (tried — combining always read as "too much content," regardless of how the boundary was marked).
- **A "Load a saved channel" dropdown** (`renderChannelSelect()`/`getKnownChannels()` in `team.js`, added 2026-09-03) sits above the Webhook URL field on the create/edit form — options are the distinct `webhook_url`s already used by existing rows (deduped, labeled by `channel_name`, or a shortened URL tail if never named), so a new post to an already-known channel doesn't need the webhook pasted in again. Picking one directly patches the Webhook URL/Channel name `<input>`s via the DOM (not `state.editing` + a full `render()`) specifically so it doesn't blow away anything already typed into Title/Body — those only sync into `state.editing` on submit, so a full re-render at that point would rebuild the form from stale state and silently drop them. Still just a starting point, not a lock — both fields stay freely editable afterward, same as if typed by hand.
- **The Body box pre-fills with the current default wording** for `daily_leader`/`monthly_consistency` (`DEFAULT_BODY_BY_SOURCE` in `team.js`, added 2026-09-02) whenever there's no real custom body saved yet — so editing starts from the actual current sentence instead of an empty box someone has to reverse-engineer. Kept as a duplicated literal (not fetched from the server) since it's just two short strings; **must be updated by hand alongside `resolveScheduledPostEmbed`'s own defaults in `lib/supabase.js`** if that wording ever changes, or the two will silently drift. Ranked-list sources (`daily_leaderboard`, `weekly_leaderboard`) have no equivalent — blank there is itself a valid, common choice ("no intro line"), not something needing a default filled in.
- **Migrating off the 3 old standalone functions** (`discord-daily-leader.js` etc., removed 2026-09-02): `scripts/seed-scheduled-posts.mjs` inserted 3 rows reproducing their exact prior behavior (same webhook, same default wording, same timing) before the old files and their `netlify.toml` cron entries were deleted — verified locally (dispatcher fires all 3 built-in sources correctly, plus `'custom'`/`'once'`/`tag_everyone` paths) against a **test-channel webhook**, never production, before cutting over.
- **Testing `discord-dispatch.js` directly**: `netlify dev` allows invoking a scheduled function via a plain `GET`/`curl` regardless of its cron schedule (e.g. `curl localhost:8080/.netlify/functions/discord-dispatch`) — it'll print a warning about being "a scheduled function... invoked via HTTP," which is expected and harmless. To force a specific row to fire for testing, backdate its `next_fire_at` in Supabase rather than waiting for the real time.
- **`/team`'s real login flow can only be verified on the deployed site** — Identity login never works on `localhost` (same limitation as `/admin`'s Decap CMS). Locally, `team-posts.js`'s logic is instead verified by invoking its `handler()` directly in a Node script with a hand-built `context.clientContext.user` (bypassing real HTTP/JWT), which still catches real bugs (auth gating, validation, `next_fire_at` computation) without needing a real domain.
  - **The actual `/team` page itself is now clickable locally too, added 2026-09-18** ("can for the team page we have a local copy which mirrors the exact changes to the real page behind authorization?" — clarified on follow-up as wanting a faster way to test locally, not a separate staging deploy). Before this, the page was permanently stuck on its own login gate on `localhost` with a non-functional "Log in" button (Identity has no real domain to issue a JWT against), so the handler()-script approach above was the *only* way to exercise this page's logic at all — never the real UI, buttons, forms. **Two matching halves, both gated the same unspoofable way**: `requireAdmin()` (`lib/supabase.js`) now short-circuits to an authorized dev-admin user whenever `process.env.NETLIFY_DEV === 'true'` — confirmed directly (not assumed) that this env var reads `'true'` under a real local `netlify dev` session and is simply absent in every real deploy context, and since it's read from the function's own process environment rather than anything a request carries, nothing a client sends can trigger it outside an actual local dev session. `team.js` gained a matching `DEV_BYPASS = location.hostname === 'localhost'` check in `init()` that skips the whole Netlify Identity widget lifecycle and sets `state.authorized = true` directly — `location.hostname` is likewise never `'localhost'` on a real deploy. **Two small render-path bugs caught fixing this, not assumed away**: `render()`'s header always read `user.email` off whatever `netlifyIdentity.currentUser()` returned, which is `null` under the bypass (no real Identity session ever established) — would have thrown a `TypeError` the instant the main page tried to render; fixed with a `DEV_BYPASS`-aware header line instead ("Local dev mode — auth bypassed, not signed in"). The Log Out button was hidden under the bypass too — clicking `netlifyIdentity.logout()` with no real session behind it risked firing the library's own `'logout'` handler and flipping `state.authorized` back to `false`, stranding the page on a gate screen with no way back in short of a hard reload. Verified end-to-end via real headless Chrome: loading `localhost:8080/team/` lands directly on the real console (no gate), both tabs show genuine production data fetched over the real API (Announcements: real scheduled-post rows; Students: real counts — 334 students, 47 active this week, matching what the live site itself would show), and zero console errors.
- **Content resolution lives in `lib/supabase.js`, not `discord-dispatch.js`** (`resolveScheduledPostEmbed()`, moved there 2026-09-02): both the real dispatcher and `/team`'s **Preview** button (`team-posts.js`'s `POST ?preview=1`) call this exact same function — a preview built from separate, similar-but-not-identical logic could quietly drift from what actually posts, so there's deliberately only one code path for "what does this row's content look like." Preview passes an in-memory object built from the form's *current, possibly-unsaved* values (never a real row, never persisted, never actually posted to Discord) — safe to click regardless of which webhook `.env` happens to have loaded locally.
- **`/team` is two mutually-exclusive tabs, not stacked panels** (`state.tab` in `team.js`, restructured 2026-09-03) — Announcements (the Discord `scheduled_posts` list/form/reference panel) and Students are never rendered together; switching tabs replaces the page body rather than appending a panel above the other one. This replaced an earlier version where the Students table was just a toggle that appended above the still-visible post list — a real complaint ("the discord messages and other announcement stuff should be located differently from this academic stuff"), since a student-performance table and a Discord-post CRUD list are unrelated concerns that shouldn't share screen space. Each student's email is a real `mailto:` link directly under their name, always visible (no extra click to reveal it) — a one-click way to email them, not just plain text.
- **A "Students" view on `/team`** (`netlify/functions/team-students.js` + `renderStudents()`/`loadStudents()` in `team.js`, added 2026-09-03) — for spotting high performers (and students who've gone quiet), separate from `scheduled_posts` entirely. `GET /api/team-students` batches one query per table (students, `schedule_tasks`, `task_progress`, `pomo_daily_sessions`, this week's `pomodoro_stats`) rather than looping a per-student query like `streak.js` does for one student — same batching principle `pomodoro-leaderboard.js` already uses, just extended to every student instead of one board's worth of rows. Returns each student's all-time focus hours, this week's hours, a **Consistency** figure, **Course progress** percentage, streak (via the same `computeStreak()` `streak.js` uses), completed-task count, last-active date, and a free-text `notes` field — sorted by all-time hours descending by default (so high performers float to the top on load), with clickable column headers to re-sort client-side (e.g. by Consistency for steady-but-not-flashy students, or Last active to see who's dropped off). Consistency is median daily focus minutes, zero-filled per quiet day — the same measure `computeMonthlyConsistency()` uses for the Discord monthly post — but deliberately **not** the same lookback: that function reports on a month that's already fully closed, while this is a live, in-progress reading zero-filled only across days that have actually happened *so far* this month (filling in not-yet-arrived future days as zeros would artificially crater everyone's median early in the month). Reporting on last month instead would inherit the same degenerate-data problem the monthly Discord post itself hit for August (`pomo_daily_sessions.total_minutes` only started populating Aug 28, so a full month would zero-fill ~27 of 31 days for almost everyone) — a live current-month reading sidesteps that and stays meaningful for an ongoing admin view even though it means small sample sizes early in a month. Course progress is `student's completed tasks ÷ every task in schedule_tasks` (the same whole-schedule denominator `subject-progress.js` uses per-subject, here rolled into one overall percentage instead of a per-subject breakdown, which wouldn't fit a 300-row table) — since `schedule_tasks` only ever holds months already loaded (see "Schedule data flow" above), this is naturally "of what's been assigned so far," not some far-future total. `notes` (`students.notes`, added 2026-09-03) is purely for the team's own reference — no effect on tracker behavior — edited inline per row and saved via `PATCH /api/team-students`.
  - **A real, already-live accuracy bug found and fixed 2026-09-06, while building the streak-caching change below**: `task_progress` and `pomo_daily_sessions` were both fetched with a plain unbounded `.select()`. PostgREST caps a single response at 1,000 rows regardless of how many actually match, and both tables had already exceeded that (2,595 completed `task_progress` rows, 1,112 `pomo_daily_sessions` rows) — meaning this view had been silently building every student's streak, all-time hours, week hours, last-active date, and consistency figure from an incomplete slice of the real data, with no error, it just looked like a normal successful response. Confirmed directly: a real student (`AdamOptimiser`) showed streak 0 here against a real, live-verified streak of 37 elsewhere. Fixed via `fetchAllRows()` (`lib/supabase.js`, new — pages through `.range()` until a page returns fewer rows than requested), applied to both queries. Re-verified after the fix: `AdamOptimiser` correctly shows 37, and every one of 308 students' numbers were cross-checked against a fresh independent computation with zero mismatches.
  - **Filtering + summary stats added 2026-09-05**, directly requested ("more filtering options for students and also more data analysis"). `GET /api/team-students` now also returns `days_inactive` per student (server-computed against `todayForStreak()`'s IST-anchored "today", not the browser's local clock — null, not 0, for a student with no session ever) and a `summary` object (total students, active-this-week, inactive-7+-days, never-logged-in, avg. course progress, avg. consistency) computed over the **full** roster, not whatever's currently filtered — so the top-of-page numbers stay a stable read of "state of the class" regardless of search/filter state. `renderStudentSummary()`/`renderStudentFilters()` in `team.js` render summary cards + a search box (name/email substring) + an "inactive for N+ days" bucket select + a min-streak number input, all client-side over the already-loaded roster (`filteredStudents()`, applied before `sortedStudents()`). The Last Active column now shows a relative label ("Today"/"Yesterday"/"Nd ago"/"Never") with a green/amber/red/grey dot (`inactivityClass()`) instead of a bare date. Since `render()` replaces `#root`'s whole `innerHTML` on every state change, the search and min-streak `<input>`s explicitly re-focus and restore cursor position after each re-render (`refocusFilterInput()`) — without it, typing a second character would drop focus, a real problem with this innerHTML-rebuild architecture that the `<select>` doesn't share (a dropdown has no mid-interaction cursor state to lose). Verified: `team-students.js`'s handler invoked directly (same bypass-real-JWT local-testing pattern as `team-posts.js` already used) against real production data — 302 real students, `days_inactive` all valid non-negative integers, and the `never_active` count (201) surfaced a real number that had no visibility in this view before. The interactive filter UI itself is unverified beyond a syntax/page-load check — same standing Identity-auth-only-on-the-real-domain limitation as `/admin`.
  - **"Where do I add next week's batch schedule" was reported as hard to find (2026-09-05)** — a weekly schedule has always just been a `source: 'custom'` post with its `sections` (see above) filled in, but with zero custom posts ever saved, the "Custom announcements" list rendered as a bare heading + "Nothing here yet.", and the only way to create one was a single generic "+ New post" button elsewhere on the page with no visible connection to schedules, defaulting to `source: 'custom'` invisibly. Fixed with a second, section-local "+ New announcement / schedule" button directly under the "Custom announcements" heading (`startNewPost()` factored out so both buttons share it; this one explicitly sets `source: 'custom'` and pre-seeds one blank section row so the Extra Sections table is immediately fillable), a one-line explainer under the heading, and the Source dropdown's label changed from bare "Custom message" to "Custom message (also used for weekly batch schedules)".
- **`scheduled_posts.channel_name`** (added 2026-09-02) — a free-text label purely for `/team`'s own display (e.g. "#announcements"), no functional effect on where a post actually goes (that's still `webhook_url`). Without it, telling rows apart in the list means decoding opaque webhook URLs by eye; `/team`'s post list falls back to a shortened webhook URL only when this is blank.
- **Telegram support (added 2026-09-05), built ahead of an actual Telegram bot existing** — the explicit ask was "assume a telegram bot will be made in future, make it fully functional wherein i will just add the link or something like of a bot in the team page." `scheduled_posts.platform` (`'discord'` | `'telegram'`, default `'discord'`) decides how a row is dispatched; every other column (`source`, `title`, `body`, `sections`, `schedule_*`) means exactly the same thing regardless of platform.
  - **Deliberately not a parallel reimplementation of all 5 sources.** `resolveScheduledPostText()` (`lib/supabase.js`) calls `resolveScheduledPostEmbed()` itself and converts the resulting Discord embed(s) into one Telegram-HTML message (`discordMarkdownToTelegramHtml()`: escapes `&`/`</>` first, then converts `[text](url)`/`**bold**`/`*italic*`/`` `code` `` — order matters, bold before italic, or bold's `**` would leave stray single `*`s for the italic regex to chew on) — title becomes a bold first line (linked via the embed's own `url` if it has one), footer becomes a trailing italic line, color is dropped (no Telegram equivalent). This means a wording or data change to any of the 5 sources automatically applies to both platforms with **zero duplicate maintenance** and no way for them to silently drift apart the way two independently-written branches eventually would.
  - **`webhook_url`/`test_webhook_url` are reused for Telegram rows too** — holding the bot's API base with its token embedded (`https://api.telegram.org/bot<TOKEN>`) rather than a Discord webhook, same "the endpoint/credential to POST to" role either way, so no new credential column was needed. Two new columns were needed for what a URL alone doesn't encode for Telegram (unlike a Discord webhook, which is already channel-specific): `telegram_chat_id` (the real destination) and `telegram_test_chat_id` (Send Test's destination). **`test_webhook_url` may be left blank for Telegram** and falls back to `webhook_url` — reusing the *same* bot for both real and test posts is safe and realistic (one bot, added as admin to both a production channel and a private test chat), since `telegram_chat_id`/`telegram_test_chat_id` are what actually decide where a message lands, not which bot token sent it; `telegram_test_chat_id` itself has no such fallback and is always required before Send Test will fire, same "never the row's real destination" discipline Discord's Send Test already has.
  - **`postToTelegram()`** posts via the Bot API's `sendMessage` with `parse_mode: 'HTML'` — chosen over `MarkdownV2` specifically to avoid `MarkdownV2`'s mandatory escaping of a long list of punctuation characters anywhere they appear outside real markdown syntax, a real footgun for arbitrary student display names (periods, hyphens, parens, etc. are common). HTML mode only needs `&`/`<`/`>` themselves escaped, which `discordMarkdownToTelegramHtml()` already does before introducing its own tags.
  - **Still one shared dispatcher, not a second cron** — `discord-dispatch.js` branches on `row.platform` per row (Telegram via `resolveScheduledPostText`/`postToTelegram`, Discord via the existing `resolveScheduledPostEmbed`/`postToDiscordWebhook`) rather than a parallel `telegram-dispatch.js` on its own schedule, which would just be two nearly-identical polling loops. The file keeps its Discord-specific name anyway — renaming it would also rename the deployed function's URL and the `netlify.toml` cron key for no functional benefit — with a comment pointing future readers here. **This also means existing rows are safe with zero migration risk**: a row with no `platform` value reads as `undefined`, which fails the `=== 'telegram'` check and falls through to the Discord path exactly as before — confirmed directly against production (see below), not just reasoned about.
  - **`/team`'s create/edit form** gained a Platform select (Discord/Telegram) that swaps the whole Destination section's fields (Telegram: Bot API URL + Chat ID + optional Test bot API URL + Test Chat ID, with inline setup instructions for @BotFather/finding a chat id; Discord: unchanged) and hides the Mentions section entirely for Telegram (no `@everyone`-equivalent exists in the Bot API for a channel post, and `tag_everyone`/`extra_mentions` simply don't apply). **Channel grouping/dedup (`channelKey()`) is `platform + webhook_url + telegram_chat_id` for Telegram, plain `webhook_url` for Discord** — a Telegram bot's token can be shared across many different chats, unlike a Discord webhook URL which is already channel-specific by itself, so grouping by `webhook_url` alone would have wrongly merged two different real Telegram channels using the same bot into one group. The "Load a saved channel" dropdown, the ✎ rename-everywhere flow, and `getKnownChannels()` all key off this the same way. Preview renders Telegram's already-HTML response directly (it's exactly the string that would be sent as `parse_mode: 'HTML'`, over already-escaped text with only a handful of safe tags introduced — a genuinely accurate preview, not a second approximation), styled with Telegram's own blue instead of Discord's purple/BOT-tag chrome so the two visibly read as different platforms.
  - **What's verified vs. not, given no real bot exists yet to test against**: `resolveScheduledPostText`'s HTML output was checked directly against real production data for `daily_leaderboard`/`weekly_leaderboard`/a sample `custom` schedule post — correct structure, correct escaping (confirmed `&` in "Basics 1 & 2" became `&amp;`), valid nesting (`<b>` inside `<i>` for the monthly-record line). `team-posts.js`'s validation was verified directly (telegram create without `telegram_chat_id` rejected; test-send without `telegram_test_chat_id` rejected; the `test_webhook_url` blank-falls-back-to-`webhook_url` behavior confirmed; the existing Discord path's strict no-fallback requirement confirmed unchanged). **`postToTelegram()`'s actual HTTP call to a real bot is unverified** — there is no bot yet. Once one exists (`@BotFather` → token → add as channel admin), the very first real thing to check is one real Send Test.
  - **The `platform`/`telegram_chat_id`/`telegram_test_chat_id` columns must still be added to production Supabase by hand** (`supabase/schema.sql`'s `ALTER TABLE` block, SQL editor — same "can't run raw DDL through supabase-js" manual step every schema change here has needed) before any Telegram row can actually be created; confirmed directly that this hasn't happened yet (`platform` reads as `undefined` on a real row fetched via the API) and that this is harmless in the meantime — every existing row still dispatches as Discord exactly as before.
- **The `daily_leader`/`daily_leaderboard`/`weekly_leaderboard` posts each carry a "this month's record" line** giving the day's/week's ranking some context beyond just that one ranking in isolation — `fetchMonthlyDailyRecord`/`fetchMonthlyWeeklyRecord` (`lib/supabase.js`) find the single highest `pomo_daily_sessions`/`pomodoro_stats` total logged so far this IST calendar month. **Originally shipped 2026-09-05 as an all-time record with no reset (`fetchAllTimeDailyRecord`/`fetchAllTimeWeeklyRecord`), then a second monthly line alongside it — both replaced by the monthly-only version the same day**, at explicit follow-up request ("remove the all time lines... just include this month's"); the all-time fetchers/lines were deleted outright rather than left as dead code. Resets automatically every 1st since it's just a `>=` filter against the current month's start, no separate reset job.
  - **`fetchMonthlyWeeklyRecord`'s original `week_start >= monthStartIST()` filter was a real bug, caught by direct report 2026-09-07**: a real posted message showed "This month's weekly record: Dev — 4.0h" directly under a leaderboard where the top student had 70.8h. Root cause, confirmed against production: the query only counted a week if its OWN `week_start` fell on/after the 1st — so a week starting up to 6 days *before* the new month (e.g. `week_start` 2026-08-31, covering Aug 31 - Sep 6, five sixths of it actually September) was excluded entirely on that technicality, while the brand-new week that had just started HOURS earlier was the *only* row in the eligible set and won by default — a tiny, still-accumulating total presented as "the record." Confirmed directly: all 24 eligible rows were from the week that started that same day. Fixed two ways: (1) the lower bound is `monthStart - 6 days`, not `monthStart` itself — a week only needs to *overlap* the month, not start inside it (6 days is always the exact right margin, regardless of which weekday a month starts on, since a 7-day week can start at most 6 days before its own last day); (2) the still-in-progress current week is now explicitly excluded (`< weekStartIST()`) — comparing a few hours of an ongoing week against fully-closed prior weeks was never a fair contest, independent of the month-boundary bug. Re-verified against the same production data after the fix: now correctly returns Ajay/70.8h/week-of-2026-08-31, matching that week's own leaderboard #1 exactly. `fetchMonthlyDailyRecord` has a structurally milder version of the same "in-progress period competing unfairly" issue (today's partial total vs. completed earlier days) but wasn't touched — a single day can't have the *guaranteed-empty-except-today* failure mode a month-boundary week can, so it was left as its own separate concern rather than fixed preemptively.
- **Per-subject emoji on weekly-schedule (`'custom'` source) posts were removed 2026-09-05** — `team/team.js`'s `SUBJECT_OPTIONS` used to prefix an emoji onto each dropdown value (`SUBJECT_EMOJI`, matched by array position to the subject list), and since a section's chosen subject is posted to Discord verbatim as each task line's prefix (`resolveScheduledPostEmbed`'s `'custom'` branch), the emoji ended up in the actual message, not just the `/team` editor. `SUBJECT_OPTIONS` is now just the plain subject names.
- **A `weekly_batch_trend` source reports whether the batch's median focus time rose or fell vs. the week before** — added 2026-09-06 as a line inside the `weekly_leaderboard` post, **split out into its own standalone source the same day** at explicit request, so it reads as its own follow-up message rather than one more line competing for attention inside the top-5 embed. `weeklyBatchTrendText`/`fetchCommonCohortMedians` (`lib/supabase.js`) compute the **median** (not mean — same "one binge-hours student shouldn't swing a whole-batch read" reasoning already used for the monthly consistency figures) of `pomodoro_stats.total_minutes` for the reported week vs. the week before, e.g. `📊 Batch median focus time this week: 19.8h (▲ 14% vs last week's 17.3h)`. **Deliberately not zero-filled against the full ~308-student roster** — only students with an actual `pomodoro_stats` row for that week count, since zero-filling against the whole roster would reproduce the exact degenerate-median problem the monthly consistency post hit for August (see the PostgREST/streak section above) on a *weekly* cadence instead of monthly. "Last week" here is always relative to whatever week the post is actually reporting on — resolved via the exact same `weekBefore(weekStartIST())` call `fetchLastWeekLeaders` uses for `weekly_leaderboard`, so the two posts can't disagree about which week is "this week" even though they're two independent `scheduled_posts` rows. Reaches Telegram for free through the existing `resolveScheduledPostText` adapter — no source-specific Telegram code needed, same as every other source. **The two real rows (`leaderboards` and `general` channels) were seeded directly, mirroring each channel's existing `weekly_leaderboard` row's `webhook_url`/`platform`.** Originally scheduled a few minutes after `weekly_leaderboard` (a distinct `schedule_time`) specifically because `discord-dispatch.js`'s due-posts query had no `ORDER BY`, so two rows sharing one `next_fire_at` would post in whatever order Postgres happened to return them — not reliably "leaderboard first." **Changed 2026-09-07 at explicit request to fire at the exact same time as `weekly_leaderboard`** — `discord-dispatch.js`'s query now has `.order('created_at', { ascending: true })`, so even rows sharing an identical `next_fire_at` still process in a deterministic order (whichever row was created first fires first); since both `weekly_leaderboard` rows were created 2026-09-01/02 and both `weekly_batch_trend` rows 2026-09-06, this reliably puts leaderboard before trend for each channel without needing a dedicated priority field. Both `weekly_batch_trend` rows' `schedule_time`/`next_fire_at` were then updated to match their channel's `weekly_leaderboard` row exactly. This also incidentally fixed an unrelated, real timing confusion: a `schedule_time` that isn't itself aligned to the dispatcher's 15-minute cron ticks (`:00`/`:15`/`:30`/`:45` UTC) doesn't fire at the configured minute — it fires at the *next* aligned tick after it becomes due (e.g. `10:31` IST doesn't fire until `10:45` IST) — `10:30` IST (`weekly_leaderboard`'s own time) is itself tick-aligned, so this side-steps that entirely too. Deliberately **not** tagging `@everyone` on this one even on the `general` channel row (unlike its `weekly_leaderboard` counterpart there) — a supplementary stat, not the main weekly announcement. `/team`'s source dropdown, body-hint text (a new `PLAIN_INTRO_SOURCES` set — no `{{name}}`/`{{hours}}` placeholders, since there's no per-entry data to interpolate, just one computed number), and the syntax reference panel were all updated to match, and `team-posts.js`'s `VALID_SOURCES` whitelist was updated too (missing this would have made `/team` reject the new source with "invalid source" on every create/edit/preview/test-send).
  - **A real bug, caught by direct report the first time this actually posted ("I don't believe the numbers" on a ▲71% swing), fixed 2026-09-07**: the original version computed each week's median separately over whoever was active THAT week — so the comparison was contaminated by population churn (students new this week, or who'd dropped off last week), not just genuine change in how hard the same people worked. Confirmed directly against the real production data behind that exact post: the full-population comparison showed 8.3h → 14.3h (+71%), but restricting to the 47 students who were active in *both* weeks showed 17.3h → 19.8h (+14%) — a real, modest increase, nothing like the swing the full-population number implied. 15 students who'd dropped off dragged the prior week's full median down; 14 students new that week dragged the reported week's full median down too, independently — neither has anything to do with "the same people doing more," which is what this line is supposed to describe. Fixed by computing both weeks' medians over the *intersection* of students active in both weeks (`fetchCommonCohortMedians`, replacing the old per-week-independent `fetchBatchMedianMinutes`) — a single coherent metric instead of two separately-varying populations being compared as if they were the same group. Returns `null` (no post) if there's no overlap at all between the two weeks' rosters, realistic only very early in the tracker's life or after a near-total population turnover. Re-verified against production after the fix: exactly reproduces the 17.3h → 19.8h / +14% apples-to-apples figures computed independently during the investigation.
  - **The common-cohort fix above was itself reverted 2026-09-21, direct instruction ("i want you to calculate regardless of repeating or not students. it's a batch median")**, after a real, user-caught inconsistency: two consecutive real Discord posts reported the *same calendar week* (Sep 7-13) as two different numbers — 17.5h in one post, 25.8h in the other. Root cause, confirmed against production before touching anything: the common-cohort fix meant a week's median was never one fixed value — it was recomputed fresh depending on which *other* week it happened to be paired against (17.5h from the 47-student cohort shared with Aug 31, 25.8h from a different, 38-student cohort shared with Sep 14, only 35 of those students in common). The cohort methodology solved a real problem (churn-skewed swings) but introduced a worse one — even the plain unrestricted median for that same week (15.8h) was a *third*, different number from both already-posted values, so "just show the real absolute number" wasn't a fix on its own, it added a fourth conflicting figure. `fetchCommonCohortMedians` reverted to `fetchWeeklyMedians` — each week's median computed independently over its own full `pomodoro_stats` population again, no intersection filtering, matching what `fetchBatchMedianMinutes` (the original, pre-2026-09-07 version) already did. The instruction was explicit that a "batch median" should mean the whole batch, full stop — a real, acknowledged tradeoff (a swing can once again be partly population churn, not purely behavior change) accepted deliberately rather than solved with the cohort restriction's side effect of an unstable per-week number. Verified directly against production: the same week-of-09-07 now returns identically (15.8h) whether computed as "this week" (paired against 08-31) or as "last week" (paired against 09-14) — the exact instability that prompted the report is gone; the three real weeks checked (08-31/09-07/09-14) now read as one coherent 14.3h → 15.8h → 19.0h progression instead of two posts silently disagreeing about the same week.
- **A real login bug, 2026-09-23: signing into `/team` with a genuinely-authorized admin account could still show "not authorized for /team" (sometimes with a blank email — "Signed in as ,"), self-correcting only on a manual page refresh.** `init()`'s `netlifyIdentity.on('init'|'login', ...)` handlers trusted whatever `user` object the widget handed them at that exact instant — but that snapshot can be stale or not-yet-fully-resolved right after the Google OAuth redirect completes (the enabled external provider here — confirmed via `taai.live/.netlify/identity/settings`, `disable_signup: true` but `google: true`) or right after a role was freshly granted in the Netlify dashboard, missing `app_metadata.roles` it should actually have. Fixed with `applyIdentityUserAndRefresh()` (shared by both the `init` and `login` handlers, replacing near-duplicate inline logic in each): applies the snapshot immediately as before (correct for the common case, no regression), then calls `user.jwt(true)` — a real forced token refresh against the server — and re-applies whatever `netlifyIdentity.currentUser()` returns afterward, silently correcting a stale first snapshot instead of leaving the person stuck looking unauthorized until they think to reload themselves. The blank-email rendering (`user` truthy but `.email` falsy — the actual visible symptom in the reported screenshot) was fixed separately in `render()`'s own gate markup, dropping the awkward "Signed in as ," down to just "Signed in," when email isn't available yet. **Unverified against the real login flow** — same standing limitation as the rest of `/team`'s auth (Identity, and specifically the Google OAuth redirect this bug depends on, only works on the real deployed domain, never `localhost`); `node --check` confirms the syntax is sound and the logic was reasoned through against the actual reported symptom, but the fix's real-world effect can only be confirmed by signing in again once this is deployed.

## Courses page — GATE DA 2028 Full Course card (added 2026-09-22)

A new featured "coming soon" preview card on `gate-da-courses.html`'s `#combos` bundles section, alongside the current GATE 2027 Full Course, Maths&ML Bundle, and Maths Bundle. `COMBOS` (the array feeding the shared `ComboCard` component) gained `featured`/`comingSoon` flags (now decoupled from `badge === 'best'`) and this entry's own hardcoded `validityDate`, since no live pricing data exists for it.

- **Gets its own full row, but only when there's a 4th card to share it with** — the row-spanning treatment (`grid-column: 1/-1`, the desktop horizontal split, its own scroll-in slide animation) is gated on `COMBOS.length > 3` in the `.map()` callback, not unconditionally on `c.comingSoon`. At exactly 3 cards (e.g. if this one were ever the only one removed) the remaining three already fill one 3-column grid row on their own — forcing one onto its own row above them would just leave two empty cells beside it instead of reverting cleanly to `grid-column: auto`, the same plain placement every other combo card gets.
- **Blurred price + blurred "What's included" list** (`filter: blur()`, not hidden outright) reads as "a real number/list exists, just not public yet" — the price is a plausible placeholder figure, never real data, and both are `aria-hidden` since neither is meaningful to a screen reader.
- **Desktop-only horizontal split** (`.combo-horizontal-left`/`-right`, `@media (min-width: 1025px)`) — badge/title/includes on the left, price on the right, divided by a 3px amber-tinted `border-left`. Stays the ordinary stacked vertical layout below that breakpoint, same as every other combo card. The left column's includes list needed `display: block` added alongside `columns: 2` — `.combo-includes ul`'s shared base rule (used by every card) is `display: flex`, and multi-column layout only ever applies to block-formatting-context content, so `columns: 2` alone silently did nothing at first.
- **A big "Coming Soon" overlay, not a button** — several iterations landed here: a disabled `<span class="btn">` → a small text label beside the price → the current version, a large chip-styled overlay (`.combo-coming-soon-overlay`/`-inner`) centered directly over the blurred includes list. The chip's own box is never animated directly (no `transform`/`box-shadow` on it) since it carries both `box-shadow` and `border-radius` — the exact combination this file's "Known gotchas" section already flags as a Chromium detached-DOM-node leak; its shimmer sweep and the bell icon's wiggle both animate elsewhere instead (a `::after` pseudo-element, the bare `<svg>`).
  - **Real mobile clipping bug, caught from a screenshot**: the chip's font-size `clamp(22px, 5.5vw, 30px)` hits its own 22px floor below ~400px viewport width and stays flat there regardless of how much narrower the phone gets, while the box it sits in (sized to the includes list) keeps shrinking — at 390px this meant a 253px-wide chip squeezed into a 224px box, clipping "Coming Soon" mid-word. See the matching entry in "Known gotchas" below.
- **The featured-card border (shared with the 2027 card) went from spinning to static** on direct request, after an investigation into a reported "glitch" on it that turned out to have nothing to do with the border at all — see the `DotField`/CDP-metrics entry in "Known gotchas" below.
- **The reveal-on-scroll animation wasn't actually playing at all** — a real bug in the shared `show()` reveal function itself, not specific to this card. See the IntersectionObserver entry in "Known gotchas" below; worth checking before giving any other `.fade-in` element its own margin-less observer.
- **Entrance-animation tuning, several rounds**: the row's own scroll-in slide (`.combo-full-row.fade-in`) moved from a barely-perceptible `cubic-bezier(.34,1.1,.64,1)` ease to the site's actual signature overshoot-and-settle curve (`.34,1.56,.64,1`, used for bump/pop moments elsewhere on the page), with more travel distance, a touch of scale, and a `0.3s` transition-delay so it doesn't fire the instant the row crosses the viewport edge.

## Site data corner in /team (added 2026-09-22)

`/team` gained a third tab, "🗂️ Site data", for admin-editing what's currently three tabs of one published Google Sheet: `pricing.csv` (read live by `gate-da-courses.html`/`gate-da-test-series.html`/`index.html` via `PRICING_CSV_URL`) and `notes.csv`/`lectures.csv` (read live by `gate-da-free-notes.html` via `NOTES_CSV_URL`/`LECTURES_CSV_URL`) — all three the same spreadsheet, different `gid`. Editing any of this previously meant editing the Google Sheet directly, outside the codebase, no audit trail, no admin UI.

**Originally deliberately did NOT change what the live pages read from — `gate-da-courses.html`'s pricing IS now cut over (2026-09-23), see its own entry below; `site_notes`/`site_lectures` and `gate-da-test-series.html`/`index.html`/`gate-da-free-notes.html` are still NOT.** `site_pricing`/`site_notes`/`site_lectures` (tables, `supabase/schema.sql`) started as a parallel, admin-editable copy — none of the four live pages were touched initially, all fetched the CSV exactly as before, by direct instruction (build and verify storage + admin UI first, cut over later once proven). `gate-da-courses.html`'s pricing cutover is that "later, separate pass" landing, for that one page's pricing data specifically — `site_notes`/`site_lectures` and the other three pages are still in the original "admin-editable preview copy, live pages untouched" state, tracked in "Future considerations" below.

- **Schema**: `site_pricing` (TEXT PK = the same id slug already used everywhere else on the site — `COMBOS[].id` in `gate-da-courses.html`, `pricingMap` keys — not a surrogate UUID, since every consumer already looks rows up by this exact string), `site_notes`/`site_lectures` (UUID PK, since `subject` isn't unique — many notes/lectures per subject). Same standing manual step every schema change here needs: run the `CREATE TABLE` block via the Supabase SQL editor before any of this activates (`supabase-js` can't run raw DDL) — until then, the three new endpoints below fail cleanly with a real Postgres "table not found" error (confirmed directly, not assumed), which is harmless since nothing existing reads these tables yet.
- **Backend**: `netlify/functions/site-pricing.js`/`site-notes.js`/`site-lectures.js` — three small REST files, one per table, mirroring `team-posts.js`'s exact GET/POST/PUT/DELETE shape and `requireAdmin` gate rather than one combined endpoint, matching this codebase's established one-resource-per-file convention (`team-posts.js` for `scheduled_posts`, `team-students.js` for `students`).
- **`site_notes.subject`/`site_lectures.subject` are validated against a fixed 10-id list** (`SITE_DATA_SUBJECT_IDS`, `lib/supabase.js`) — the same kebab-case subject ids `notes-data.json`'s own `subjects[].id` list already defines for `gate-da-free-notes.html`'s subject tabs (`linear-algebra`, `machine-learning`, ...), which also happen to be exactly what `window.GATE_DA_SUBJECTS`' Title Case names slugify to (confirmed by hand, all 10). Server-side this has to be its own hardcoded copy — a Netlify Function can't reach a browser global — but `/team`'s own client-side dropdown (`SITE_SUBJECT_OPTIONS`, `team.js`) derives from `window.GATE_DA_SUBJECTS` directly (already loaded via `gate-da-subjects.js`, see `team/index.html`) rather than a third hardcoded list, so at least that half stays tied to the single source of truth.
- **Seed script**: `npm run seed-site-data` (`scripts/seed-site-data.mjs`) fetches the same three published Google Sheet CSV URLs the live pages themselves already read (`PRICING_CSV_URL`/`NOTES_CSV_URL`/`LECTURES_CSV_URL`) and loads them into the new tables — run once, manually, after the schema migration. **Deliberately NOT reading the local `sheets/*.csv` files** — the first version of this script did exactly that (modeled on `load-schedule.mjs`'s own "provided directly, loaded by hand" convention), which turned out to be the wrong call: unlike `schedule.csv`, which has an explicit, deliberate no-auto-sync policy from the user, pricing/notes/lectures have always been live-synced from the sheet by the pages that read them — the local files are gitignored, one-off snapshots nobody had kept in sync, and seeding from them silently loaded stale data (caught by direct report — "why is there only one note, there are many notes in the site" — confirmed by fetching the live sheet directly: 3 real notes vs. the 1 near-empty local row, 12 pricing rows with different prices and a whole extra course vs. the local snapshot's 11, 19 lecture rows with real slides links vs. 22 without). Re-seeded from the live URLs and re-verified field-by-field against the live sheet directly: 12/12 pricing, 3/3 notes, 19/19 lectures, zero mismatches. Idempotent: `site_pricing` upserts by its own stable id; `site_notes`/`site_lectures` (no natural business key, a UUID PK) are fully replaced (delete-all, then re-insert) on every run — safe to re-run whenever `/team`'s copy needs refreshing from the sheet again, still a one-time-per-run script, not an ongoing cron.
- **Frontend (`team/team.js`)**: one declarative `SITE_DATA_RESOURCES` config object (endpoint, id key, table columns, form fields with `type: 'text'|'number'|'url'|'date'|'select'`) drives a single generic list-table renderer, create/edit-form renderer, and `FormData`-based payload reader shared by all three sub-tabs (Pricing/Notes/Lectures) — rather than three near-identical copies of each.
