Dates (`discount_deadline`, `validity`, `posted_on`) use plain `<input type="date">` — this *is* "the calendar" the request asked for, the same deliberate choice already made for `scheduled_posts.schedule_date` (a free-typed date string can't be sorted/validated reliably). `site_pricing.id`'s form field is `lockedOnEdit` (disabled + a hidden mirror carrying its value through `FormData`, since a disabled input is excluded from form submission) — the real PK, not safe to silently rename out from under every other consumer of that same slug via this form. List tables reuse `.students-table`'s existing styling via a new `.sitedata-table` modifier class that turns off the inherited click-to-sort cursor/hover (these three lists are small and already server-ordered, no sort UI built for them).
- **"Card alignment norms" — clarified directly with the user**: refers to the *live public course cards* (the row-count/divider rules finished on `gate-da-courses.html` earlier this same session), a constraint this work must not disturb — not a request to build a card-grid UI inside `/team` itself. Since this pass never touches the live pages' rendering code, that constraint holds automatically; the `/team` admin UI itself uses a plain data table (the `.students-table` pattern), not cards.
- Verified: `node --check` on all new/modified `.js` files; each of the three new endpoints invoked directly (same local-`context.clientContext.user`-based testing pattern `team-posts.js`/`team-students.js` already established, since Identity auth can't be tested on `localhost`) — all three fail cleanly pre-migration with the real Postgres error, and the admin-role gate correctly returns 401 without it.
- **Migration run, seeded, and fully verified the same day.** Once the manual SQL-editor step ran: `seed-site-data` confirmed matching the live sheet exactly (12/12 pricing, 3/3 notes, 19/19 lectures, zero field mismatches, checked twice — once against the stale local files before the seed script was fixed, once against the live sheet after); direct handler-level CRUD tests (create/update/delete on all three, plus validation rejection checks) all passed with row counts returning cleanly to baseline; a real headless-Chrome run driving the actual `/team` UI (via its `DEV_BYPASS` path) clicked through the Site data tab, created a pricing row through the real form, confirmed the `id` field correctly locks on edit, updated it, and deleted it through the real confirm dialog — zero console errors throughout. `/team`'s real Identity login flow itself still only testable on the real deployed domain, same standing limitation as the rest of this page.
- **`sold_out_date` + `display_order` columns added to `site_pricing` (2026-09-23), admin-only by explicit direction** — the same "should this take effect live?" fork came up as the original cutover decision, and the answer was the same: build the `/team` capability, leave `gate-da-courses.html` untouched. `sold_out_date` backs a form field shown **only when editing the `full-course` row** (`showIf: row => row.id === 'full-course'` — a new small per-field predicate `renderSiteDataForm`/`siteDataFieldHtml` now support, filtered out before rendering rather than disabled/hidden via CSS, since it just shouldn't exist as an input for any other row). `display_order` backs ▲/▼ reorder buttons on combo-type ("bundled course") pricing rows specifically, in `renderSiteDataList`'s Pricing view — a straight port of `movePost`'s existing swap-with-neighbor pattern for `scheduled_posts.dispatch_order` (same "identical values nudge apart by 1 instead of a no-op swap" fix already proven there), just scoped to `rows.filter(r => r.type === 'combo')` instead of a channel group. A `COMBO_OWN_ROW_THRESHOLD = 3` constant mirrors `gate-da-courses.html`'s own real `COMBOS.length > 3` gate by hand (same unavoidable "can't share a constant across this boundary" tradeoff as `SITE_DATA_SUBJECT_IDS`) to drive a purely informational banner above the Pricing list — "N bundled courses — the live page's featured card currently gets its own row / sits in the plain grid" — so an admin can see what the live layout is doing without this list actually controlling it yet.
  - **Real fault-tolerance bug caught before shipping, not after — the same class of mistake this file has flagged more than once already.** The first version added `display_order` straight into the main list query's `ORDER BY`, unconditionally — confirmed directly (not assumed) that this would 500 the **entire** Pricing tab, not just fail to show the new field, for the whole window between deploying and someone running the migration, since PostgREST fails a query outright over one missing ordering column. This is a materially bigger blast radius than the earlier new-table additions (which degraded safely because *nothing existing* depended on those tables yet) — here, the Pricing list was already live and working. Fixed with a catch-and-retry: the query runs with the new ordering first, and only falls back to the pre-migration ordering (`type`, `id`) on that specific `display_order`-missing error — confirmed directly pre-migration that GET still returns all 12 rows with a clean 200 instead of a 500.
  - Verified end-to-end via a real headless-Chrome run (`DEV_BYPASS`) before the migration even ran: the alignment banner renders correctly ("3 bundled courses..."), the `sold_out_date` field shows on `full-course`'s edit form and is confirmed absent on `maths-bundle`'s, and clicking a ▲/▼ button fails cleanly with a real, visible error message (the write genuinely can't work pre-migration — expected, same as any other brand-new field) rather than throwing or corrupting anything. Requires the same standing manual SQL-editor step as every other schema change here before the reorder/sold-out-date fields actually persist.
- **`enroll_url` column added the same day, same admin-only pattern** — backs a plain "Enrol now link" field on every pricing row (unlike `sold_out_date`, no `showIf` restriction; every course conceptually has its own enrol link). Direct request: a row with no link set should read as "Coming Soon" — implemented as a *computed* Status column (`r.enroll_url ? 'Live' : 'Coming Soon'`) rather than a separate boolean flag, specifically so there's no second toggle that could ever disagree with whether a real link is actually set — the exact same relationship the 2028 card already has to simply not having a real `site_pricing` row at all, just made explicit as one field instead of an absent row. GET's `select('*')` needed no fallback for this one (unlike `display_order`) since it's never used in an `ORDER BY` — a plain missing column in a `select('*')` doesn't fail the query the way ordering by one does. Verified pre-migration: the field renders on every row's form with its hint text, and the Status column correctly reads "Coming Soon" for `full-course` (which has no `enroll_url` yet).
- **Pricing's list/form redesigned for a non-technical admin, 2026-09-23** ("divide the site data into two parts combo and individual... make the site data page more intuitive and better, because non tech people are going to use it"). Was one flat `.sitedata-table` — 9 columns wide (needing horizontal scroll), every unset field showing a bare "—". Two changes:
  - **Pricing's list view is now grouped, card-based, not a table.** `renderPricingGroups()` splits rows into "Bundled courses" (`type === 'combo'`, the literal "combo" half of the ask) and "Individual courses" (everything else — `individual` + `test-series` lumped together, per the literal "two parts") — each its own labeled `.pricing-group` section, so the split is visible groupings rather than a hidden filter/sub-tab. `pricingCardHtml()` renders one card per course: name + id up top, a colored status pill (`.pricing-status-live`/`-soon`, reusing the same `--green`/`--amber` tokens the Students view's inactivity dots already use), the price large and prominent (with old price struck through beside it if set), a discount chip if one exists, then only whichever of Validity/Discount-deadline/Sold-out-date are actually *set* — no more "—" placeholders for a non-technical admin to puzzle over. The existing ▲/▼ reorder buttons and the bundled-course alignment hint (`COMBO_OWN_ROW_THRESHOLD`) both carry over unchanged, just now living inside this card layout instead of table cells. Notes/Lectures keep the original plain `.sitedata-table` (`renderSiteDataTable()`, factored out from the old combined function) — short enough, few enough columns, no grouping/status need — so this redesign is scoped to where the complexity actually was.
  - **Pricing's create/edit form is now grouped into labeled sections** ("Basic info" / "Pricing" / "Enrollment & dates"), reusing the exact `.form-section`/`.form-section-heading` markup the Announcements form already established for its own Destination/Content/Mentions/Schedule sections — a new optional per-field `section` property on `SITE_DATA_RESOURCES.pricing.fields`, read by `renderSiteDataForm()` (falls back to one flat list, unchanged, for any resource with no sectioned fields — Notes/Lectures didn't need this, short enough already). The Type field also grew a hint explaining what "combo" actually controls ("shows up under Bundled courses... can be reordered"), since that word alone doesn't self-explain the grouping/reorder behavior it now visibly drives.
  - Per-tab intro copy (`SITE_DATA_INTRO`) and the "+ New" button label (`SITE_DATA_NEW_LABEL`) were rewritten in plain language per sub-tab ("Every course, bundle, and test series shown on the site...", "+ New course") instead of one generic "Admin-editable copy of the X data" sentence and a mechanically-pluralized button label — same non-technical-audience intent as the rest of this pass. `SITE_DATA_SINGULAR` (`{pricing: 'course', notes: 'note', lectures: 'lecture'}`) replaced a `cfg.label.replace(/s$/, '')` string-mangling trick in both the form heading and the delete-confirm dialog, which produced "Pricing" (not "course") as the singular for pricing specifically — a real, if minor, wording bug the redesign happened to catch.
  - Verified via `node --check`, then a real headless-Chrome pass over `/team` (`DEV_BYPASS`) against the real seeded production data: both "Bundled courses"/"Individual courses" groups render with correct counts and cards, a card with no price set shows "No price set" instead of a table dash, the sold-out-cutover note only appears on `full-course`'s card, ▲/▼ reordering still round-trips correctly, the sectioned edit form shows all three headings with the right fields grouped under each, and Notes/Lectures render unchanged (still the plain table) — zero console errors, zero change to any of the four live public pages (none of their files were touched).
- **Three more direct follow-ups the same day, 2026-09-23 — the ID field became a dropdown, reordering became mouse drag-and-drop, and reordering became genuinely LIVE, the first piece of this whole admin panel to cross that line.** ("the ids should be selectable from a dropdown menu and with full course add the year. also the reordering of cards should also reflect on the live site when live... the reordering should be done by dragging with mouse and not buttons.")
  - **ID dropdown**: `SITE_PRICING_ID_GROUPS` (`team.js`) is a hand-transcribed list of every real `site_pricing` id that actually maps to a card somewhere on the live site — 4 combo ids (`full-course-2028`/`full-course`, both labeled with their year since "Full Course" alone doesn't say which card a new row is for; `maths-ml-bundle`/`maths-bundle`), 6 individual-subject ids (matching `gate-da-courses.html`'s own `INDIVIDUAL[]` array exactly — not the full 10-subject list, since 4 subjects (AI/DBMS/Data Structures/Algorithms) have no live course card to attach to yet), 3 test-series ids. `siteDataFieldHtml()` gained `optionGroups` support (grouped `<optgroup>`s, same three-way split the Pricing list itself already shows) alongside the existing flat `options` — the Pricing form's `id` field switched from free `type: 'text'` to `type: 'select', optionGroups: SITE_PRICING_ID_GROUPS`; the existing `lockedOnEdit` disabled+hidden-mirror mechanism already worked generically for any input type, so edit-mode locking needed no changes at all. A non-technical admin can no longer create a typo'd slug that silently never matches any live card.
  - **Drag-and-drop reorder**: the ▲/▼ buttons (`movePricingRow`) are gone, replaced by whole-card native HTML5 drag-and-drop (`bindPricingCardDrag`/`commitPricingDragOrder`, `team.js`) — grab a card by its `⠿` handle and drop it among its own group's siblings. `dragover` on a sibling card physically moves the dragged element in the DOM via `insertBefore`/`insertAfter` (no re-render mid-drag, so the gesture itself stays smooth); the actual write only happens once, on `drop`, by reading back whatever order the DOM ended up in and assigning fresh sequential `display_order` values (0,1,2,...) — only rows whose value genuinely changed get a `PUT`, so a drag that ends up back where it started costs zero requests. Scoped per `data-drag-group` (`combo`/`individual`, matching `renderPricingGroups`' own split) so a card can only ever be dropped within its own section. A brand-new row's `display_order` on create now appends to the END of its own type-group (`max(existing) + 1`) instead of defaulting to `0`, which previously would have silently jumped every new course to the front.
  - **The reorder is now genuinely LIVE, not admin-only preview — the one deliberate scope change from this codebase's earlier "admin-only for now" default, made at explicit direction.** New `netlify/functions/site-pricing-order.js` — the first, and so far only, **unauthenticated** `site_pricing`-adjacent endpoint (every sibling function is `requireAdmin`-gated, `/team`-only), because `gate-da-courses.html` itself has no Identity JWT to send. Deliberately returns ONLY `{id: display_order}`, nothing else — no price, no discount, no enroll_url — so exposing it publicly doesn't leak anything the live page doesn't already show. `gate-da-courses.html` fetches it once at page load (same "pre-fetch before React mounts, cache at module scope" pattern `_pricingFetch`/`_pricingCache` already established for the CSV) and `sortByDisplayOrder()` (new, module-level) sorts the page's own hardcoded `COMBOS`/`INDIVIDUAL` arrays by it via a `React.useMemo` — any card with no known order (a brand-new one not yet given a `display_order`, or the fetch having failed entirely) stays exactly where the hardcoded array already put it, never yanked to the front/back for having no data; an empty/failed order map (pre-migration, or the endpoint being briefly down) falls back to the arrays' own hardcoded order byte-for-byte, so this can only ever improve on the existing order, never break the page. **`sortByDisplayOrder` also renumbers each card's `num` field** (the large corner numeral — `.combo-num`, `ComboCard`-only, `CourseCard` never uses it) to match its new visual position, since that field was otherwise a static value tied to the object identity, not its position — without this, dragging a card in `/team` would leave the numerals reading out of sequence on the live page. **Price/discount/sold-out-date/enroll-url are all still exactly as admin-only as before this round** — only the ORDER of these same hardcoded, already-existing cards moved, nothing about their content did; the actual data cutover (reading price/etc. live instead of the CSV) is still the deferred "Future considerations" item, untouched by this.
  - **The requested combo order (2028, 2027, maths+ml, maths bundle) and the 2028 card itself were already exactly right, no live-page edit needed** — both were built in an earlier pass this same session (see the "GATE DA 2028 Full Course card" section above); confirmed by reading `COMBOS[]` directly rather than assumed.
  - Verified end-to-end, not just reasoned about: `curl`ing the new endpoint pre-migration returns a clean `{"order":{}}` (confirmed the `display_order` column still isn't live in production — same standing manual SQL-editor step as ever); a real headless-Chrome load of the live page with that empty response showed the exact hardcoded order (2028, 2027, maths-ml, maths, then the 6 individual subjects) and correctly-sequential `01`-`04` numerals, zero console errors; a second run intercepted the same endpoint via CDP `Fetch` (mocking a real order response — `{'maths-bundle':0,'maths-ml-bundle':1,'full-course':2,'full-course-2028':3}` — without writing anything to production, since the column doesn't exist there yet) confirmed the page's own combo cards visually re-sort to exactly that order and the corner numerals renumber `01`-`04` to match the new positions, proving the live sort mechanism itself works correctly, not just that it degrades safely.
  - **The migration was run the same day** — confirmed directly (the three columns now return real values instead of `undefined`), then `enroll_url` was backfilled for all 12 already-seeded rows with their real, already-live enrolment links (hand-transcribed from `gate-da-courses.html`'s `COMBOS`/`INDIVIDUAL` `href`s and `gate-da-test-series.html`'s own plan-card `href`s, matched by id) — nobody had ever done this, since the original seed script only pulled from the pricing CSV, which never had URLs in it at all; every row correctly flipped from "Coming Soon" to "Live" in `/team`'s Status column as a result. A `site_pricing` row for `full-course-2028` was also created (it had never existed — the 2028 card has no real CSV/price data to have been seeded from in the first place, being a genuine coming-soon preview), and every row's `display_order` was set to match its real on-page position (combo: 2028/2027/maths-ml/maths = 0-3; individual: statistics/calculus/linear-algebra/probability/machine-learning/python-programming = 0-5) rather than all sitting tied at the default `0`.
  - **Test series was split out of "Individual courses" into its own third group/order space the same day, on direct correction ("test series is separate")** — `renderPricingGroups()` now does a genuine three-way split (`type === 'combo'` / `'individual'` / `'test-series'`, exact matches, not the earlier `'individual'` group's `!== 'combo'` catch-all) instead of lumping test-series in with real subject courses; the submit handler's new-row `display_order` default (append to the end of the row's own group) switched from that same combo/not-combo binary to an exact `r.type === payload.type` match, so a new test-series row now competes for position only against other test-series rows. Test series' own 3 rows were renumbered `0`/`1`/`2` (`test-series`/`weekly-quizzes`/`test-series-quizzes`, matching `gate-da-test-series.html`'s own Basic/Weekly-Quizzes/Featured plan-card order) instead of continuing the individual-subjects sequence. **Test series' own order is admin-only preview, not live** — unlike combo/individual, `gate-da-test-series.html` doesn't read from `site-pricing-order.js` at all (out of scope for this pass, not asked); the group's own hint text says so explicitly rather than implying otherwise. Verified via a real headless-Chrome load of `/team`: three distinct group headings ("Bundled courses (4)", "Individual courses (6)", "Test series (3)"), each with the correct rows and drag scoping.
- **Delete removed from every pricing card, same day — "remove the ability to remove a course from team page."** Followed directly from actually walking through what Delete would do here: since only a course's *order* is wired live (not its existence — see above), deleting a `site_pricing` row never takes a course off taai.live at all. The card keeps rendering from its own unaffected hardcoded `COMBOS`/`INDIVIDUAL` entry, just demoted to the end of its group once it has no `display_order` left to sort by — an admin clicking Delete expecting to actually remove a course would get exactly the wrong impression, with nothing to catch it. `siteDataRowActionsHtml(type, id, showDelete)` gained a third param (default `true`, so Notes/Lectures — which have no such live-order confusion — keep Delete unchanged); `pricingCardHtml()` now passes `false` explicitly. **Only the `/team` UI button is gone — `site-pricing.js`'s `DELETE` handler itself is untouched**, deliberately: a small, low-risk escape hatch for genuinely correcting a mistaken row via direct API call still exists, it's just no longer one click away for an admin who might reach for it expecting the wrong outcome. Actually removing a course from the live site is still, and only, a code change (deleting its `COMBOS`/`INDIVIDUAL` entry) — restated here since this is the second time in one session this exact "admin action ≠ live removal" gap came up. Verified via a real headless-Chrome pass: 0 Delete buttons across all 13 pricing cards (13 Edit buttons, unaffected), Notes' own Delete buttons confirmed still present and working (3/3).
- **"+ New course" removed the same day too — "remove the add course function, we will add them from here to both courses page and team page."** Same underlying reasoning as Delete above, from the opposite direction: the ID dropdown (`SITE_PRICING_ID_GROUPS`) only ever lists ids of courses that *already* have a real hardcoded card, so the "+ New course" button could only ever create a pricing row for an existing card, never a genuinely new one — a new course always needs its card added to `gate-da-courses.html`/`gate-da-test-series.html` (and that id added to the dropdown list) first, as its own deliberate code change, before `/team` has anything to attach a row to. `renderSiteData()` now computes `showNewButton = type !== 'pricing'` and skips rendering the button for that one sub-tab; Notes/Lectures keep theirs, unaffected — no such constraint applies to them. The empty-state message (`renderSiteDataList`, unreachable today since Pricing always has 13 real rows, but corrected for honesty) no longer tells a non-technical admin to click a button that isn't there — it now reads "these are added directly (with their matching course card), not through this page" for Pricing specifically. **`site-pricing.js`'s `POST` handler itself is untouched**, same "hide the button, keep the API" pattern as Delete — creating a row via direct call (the way `full-course-2028`'s row was created earlier this session) still works, it's just not a form anyone reaches from `/team` anymore. Verified via a real headless-Chrome pass: `#sitedata-new` absent on the Pricing sub-tab, present and reading "+ New note" on Notes.
- **A real data-loss mistake, same day, self-inflicted while manually testing order — worth recording so it isn't repeated.** Two `curl PUT` calls sent directly against `/api/site-pricing` (fixing the maths-bundle/maths-ml-bundle order by hand, bypassing the real `/team` form) used minimal bodies — just `id`/`type`/`name`/`display_order` — not realizing `site-pricing.js`'s `PUT` handler builds the **entire** row fresh from whatever the request body contains (`rowFromBody()`), rather than merging into the existing row. Every field left out of those two calls (`price`, `price_old`, `discount`, `enroll_url`) got silently set to `null` for both rows — confirmed directly (not assumed) once a user report ("why is everything coming soon and no price set") prompted checking: both rows had genuinely lost their real price/discount/enrol-link data, while `full-course` (always PUT with its complete data in every direct call this session) was untouched. **Fixed by going back to the authoritative source, not by guessing the old numbers**: re-ran `npm run seed-site-data`, which re-fetches the same live pricing spreadsheet and does a genuine partial `upsert` (confirmed by reading the script — its row objects don't include `sold_out_date`/`display_order`/`enroll_url` at all, and PostgREST's upsert-on-conflict only touches the columns actually present in the request, unlike a full `PUT`) — this correctly restored `price`/`price_old`/`discount` for both rows without disturbing the separately-managed `display_order`/`sold_out_date` that had nothing to do with the sheet. `enroll_url` isn't part of the CSV at all, so it needed its own follow-up `PUT` with the same real, code-verified links used in the original backfill (not the sheet, and not a guess). Verified with a full audit of all 13 rows afterward (price/discount/enrol-link/order all cross-checked, not just the two affected ones) and a real headless-Chrome load of `/team` showing all three combo bundles correctly back to "● Live" with their real prices. **The underlying lesson, not just the fix**: any direct API call made outside the real form — for testing, for a quick data fix, whatever — needs to either fetch the row first and spread its existing fields (`Object.assign({}, row, {...changes})`, the pattern the real form itself and the safer of this session's own scripts already used) or be treated as carrying the same blast radius as a full row replace, same discipline this file's own `CLAUDE.md` gotchas already ask for elsewhere (e.g. "any script run with `.env` loaded hits the real production Supabase project, not a sandbox") — this is a sharper version of that same warning, since here even correctly-shaped JSON with a legitimate purpose caused real loss purely from omitting fields, not from hitting the wrong environment.
- **A live discount-vs-price hint, added the same day off a direct question ("does the discount percentage auto calculate the price?" → no → "want me to add that as a real auto-calculated/auto-checked field?" → "yes"), then made bidirectional the following day off a direct follow-up ("if discount is given, price is auto calculated[?] ... if we give the discounted price, can you auto calculate the discount percentage?").** `price`/`price_old`/`discount` were, and still are, three fully independent fields — nothing computes one from the others, either in `/team` or on the live page (`gate-da-courses.html`'s own rendering just does `isNaN(discount) ? discount : discount + '% off'`, no math). Rather than force `discount` into a pure computed number — it's also legitimately used as free custom text (e.g. a `"SAVE BIG"` label), not just a percentage, so replacing it outright would remove real flexibility — `bindPricingDiscountCheck()` (`team.js`) adds a live, bidirectional hint under the Discount field instead, computed client-side from whichever two of the three values are already filled: **price + old price known, discount empty** → "Price implies 20% off — [Use 20%]" (styled as the same `.chip` already used for the Announcements body's placeholder chips); **discount % + old price known, price still empty** → "N% off ₹X is ₹Y — [Use ₹Y]" (the reverse direction, added in the follow-up round); **all three present, numbers agree** → green "✓ Matches the price math"; **all three present, numbers disagree** → amber "⚠ Doesn't match..."; **non-numeric custom discount text** → the computed % shown as plain information, no false-positive mismatch warning, since there's nothing meaningful to compare custom text against. Once price is typed, the forward (price→discount) branch always takes over from the reverse one — the reverse hint only ever appears while price is genuinely still blank, so there's no ambiguity about which value is "driving" the other once both are real. The numeric-vs-text check deliberately reuses the exact same `isNaN()` rule `gate-da-courses.html`'s own `ComboCard` already uses to decide whether `discount` renders as `"N% off"` or verbatim text — so this hint can never disagree with what the live page would actually show. Purely a client-side authoring aid, bound fresh on every render (`bindPricingDiscountCheck()`, called from `bindEvents()` alongside `bindPricingCardDrag()`) — doesn't change what's validated or stored server-side, `discount` stays a free-text column exactly as before. Verified end-to-end via real headless-Chrome interaction tests (not just reasoned about), both rounds: the original forward-direction round confirmed the green match/amber mismatch/fill-button/custom-text states on `full-course`'s real edit form; the bidirectional follow-up confirmed clearing price, setting old price to ₹14,999 and discount to 30% correctly computed and offered ₹10,499, that clicking "Use ₹10499" correctly filled the price field, and that the hint then seamlessly flipped to the forward-direction green match state — all via real `input` events dispatched at the DOM, no form ever submitted, so neither test made any writes.
- **The drag-and-drop reorder mechanism itself was rebuilt the same day, after a real report from actually using it — "i just reordered manually in the localhost page nothing changed also the reorder mechanism is a bit finnicky."** The original version physically moved the dragged card in the DOM on every `dragover` (a Trello-style live reflow) — root cause of both complaints at once: reflowing the grid mid-drag shifts the very geometry the *next* dragover's before/after check depends on, and in the worst case the browser can lose track of a valid drop target entirely and silently reject the drop — no error, nothing visibly changes, exactly "nothing changed." Rebuilt to never touch the DOM during the drag itself: `dragover` now only tracks where the drop *would* land (`pricingDrag.overId`/`overBefore`) and paints a lightweight `box-shadow` border highlight (`.pricing-card--drop-before`/`-after`) on whichever card is currently the target, over a grid whose real geometry never shifts under the cursor. The actual reorder is computed exactly once, on `drop`, from the still-untouched original DOM order plus that tracked target (`applyPricingDrop()` splices the dragged id out and back in at the right spot, `commitPricingOrder()` — renamed from `commitPricingDragOrder()` since it no longer reads order off the DOM — persists only the rows whose `display_order` actually changed). Hovering the group's own empty grid space (not a specific card) tracks as "drop at the end" (`overId: null`) the same as before. Verified with a real synthetic drag (CDP-dispatched `DragEvent`s, not just reasoned about): confirmed the DOM order is genuinely unchanged mid-drag (the actual bug this fixes), confirmed the correct `pricing-card--drop-after` indicator class applied to the hovered target, and confirmed the final order after drop landed exactly where dropped (`full-course-2028` moved to right after `maths-ml-bundle`, as dropped) — reverted immediately after, same disposable-write discipline as every other live-data check this session.
- **The full-row ("own row") treatment on the combos grid was decoupled from `comingSoon` and made purely positional, same day — direct correction: "no the first card should be horizontal no matter what and coming soon doesn't matter. the 2028 batch just happens to be the first card and also coming soon."** `getsOwnRow` (in `sortedCombos.map()`) used to be `c.comingSoon && COMBOS.length > 3` — meaning the full-width spotlight row was tied to a specific hardcoded flag on a specific card, and only ever looked position-driven because the 2028 card happened to also be first. Now it's `i === 0 && COMBOS.length > 3` — `i` being the index within `sortedCombos`, i.e. genuinely after live reordering, not `COMBOS`' own hardcoded array order — so whichever card a `/team` drag puts first is the one that gets the spotlight row, independent of whether it's a coming-soon preview or a real purchasable bundle. **Deliberately narrow — only the row/width decision moved, nothing about `comingSoon`'s OTHER effects did**: the two-column desktop split (`.combo-horizontal-left`/`-right`), the blurred price/includes, the "Coming Soon" overlay, and the disabled (no-button) state are all still driven by the card's own real `comingSoon` flag, exactly as before — those describe an actual product state (no real price/enrolment yet), not a position, and weren't part of what was asked. A non-comingSoon card landing in the full-row slot simply renders its normal flat vertical structure, stretched to the row's own wider centered box (`max-width: 920px` on desktop) instead of narrower one of three — no special layout code needed for that case, the existing `.combo-full-row .combo-card` width rule already covers it. Verified two ways: (1) the real hardcoded order — 2028 first, `comingSoon` — still gets `combo-full-row`, unchanged from before this fix, zero console errors. (2) A real (then-reverted) production write moved `maths-bundle` (a genuine bundle, `comingSoon: false`) to `display_order: -1` — confirmed via the live DOM that it correctly became the one with `combo-full-row`, every other card correctly lost it, the corner numeral correctly renumbered to `01`, and — after ruling out an initial screenshot-timing false alarm with a longer wait — its real price (`₹5,999`) and validity (`Feb 15, 2027`) both rendered correctly inside the full-row slot, proving the non-comingSoon case isn't just "doesn't crash" but genuinely renders right.
- **A Safari-only rendering artifact on the featured card's gradient ring, fixed the same day off a real screenshot** — described as a bright beam converging to a point with visible spoke lines, instead of a clean thin border ring, on both the 2027 and 2028 featured cards. Root cause: the ring was an oversized `conic-gradient` on a `::before` pseudo-element sitting *behind* the card at a lower z-index (`inset: -8px`), with its visible width controlled entirely by the child `.combo-card`'s own opaque background covering everything except the parent's real 2px `padding` gap — a stacking-context-dependent technique Chromium composites fine but WebKit apparently doesn't render reliably here. Fixed by putting the `conic-gradient` directly on `.combo-featured-frame`'s own `background` instead of a `::before` — the "ring" is now nothing more than the frame's real 2px padding with the card sitting in normal document flow on top of it, no absolute positioning or z-index involved at all, so there's no stacking order for any engine to get wrong. The exact same simplification (a flat, non-gradient version) already existed for touch devices (`@media (hover: none)`) — this brings the default (mouse/trackpad) case onto equally solid footing, just keeping its gradient. **Not verified in real Safari** — same standing limitation as every other Safari-only report in this codebase (no way to automate it in this environment); verified instead via a real headless-Chrome render at 1440px showing the intended clean thin ring with zero console errors and the correct computed `background-image`/`padding`, confirming no regression, but the actual Safari fix itself needs confirming once deployed.
- **`gate-da-courses.html`'s pricing data was cut over from the Google Sheets CSV to live `site_pricing` data, 2026-09-23 — the deliberately-deferred step from the "Site data corner" section above, now done for this one page.** Triggered by direct, repeated confusion ("the changed prices haven't reflected in the courses page" / "why isn't [the price] wired") after several rounds of setting real prices/discounts through `/team` with nothing to show for it on the live site — confirmed with the user via `AskUserQuestion` before building (not assumed) that this was wanted now, not just explained away again.
  - **New public endpoint, replacing the order-only one**: `netlify/functions/site-pricing-public.js` supersedes `site-pricing-order.js` (deleted) — same unauthenticated, public-safe reasoning (every column it returns is already meant to be shown on the live site, nothing sensitive), but now returns the *whole* public-safe row per id (`price`, `price_old`, `discount`, `discount_reason`, `discount_deadline`, `validity`, `sold_out_date`, `enroll_url`, `display_order`) instead of just order — one endpoint covering everything `/team`'s Pricing tab now controls live, not two overlapping fetches.
  - **Merge, not replace**: `gate-da-courses.html` keeps `PRICING_CSV_URL`/`_pricingFetch` exactly as before, as the *base* layer — `mergePricingRow(csvRow, apiRow)` (new) combines it with the new `_sitePricingFetch`, Supabase winning per-field only where it actually has a non-null value, the CSV's own value surviving for any field Supabase doesn't have yet (a brand-new row, a field just never filled in). `App()`'s `effectivePricingMap` (a `useMemo` over the union of both maps' ids) is now the single source every card, `BundleComparison`, and the JSON-LD-price effect reads from — replacing every direct `pricingMap[...]` reference in the render tree. A fetch failure or an incomplete row can therefore only ever leave a course exactly as it already was, never make it worse — same safety guarantee the order-only cutover already established, just widened to cover every field.
  - **Three fields needed real wiring inside `ComboCard`/`CourseCard`, not just a data-source swap**: (1) `soldOutDate` — was `c.soldOutDate || null` (hardcoded only); now `pd.soldOutDate || c.soldOutDate || null`, live value winning. This also means the sold-out cutover is no longer full-course-exclusive in practice — `/team`'s own `sold_out_date` field was widened from `showIf: row.id === 'full-course'` to `showIf: row.type === 'combo'` to match, since `ComboCard`'s own logic never was full-course-specific, only the form's restriction was. (2) The "Enrol now" button's `href` — was `c.href` (hardcoded) on both `ComboCard` and `CourseCard`, and the three hardcoded links inside `BundleComparison`'s own footer row; all four now read `pd.enrollUrl || c.href` (or the matching hardcoded fallback for `BundleComparison`'s three), so `/team`'s Enrol URL field genuinely controls where the button goes, with the course's own existing link as the fallback if it's ever cleared. (3) `sortByDisplayOrder`'s second parameter changed from a plain `{id: order}` map to the full `sitePricing` rows map (reading `.display_order` per id) — no behavior change, just adapting to the merged endpoint's richer shape.
  - **A real, deliberate scope boundary, not an oversight**: `enroll_url` does **NOT** make `comingSoon` itself computed — clearing it for a normal (non-`comingSoon`) course just falls back to that course's own hardcoded link, it does *not* flip the course into a disabled "Coming Soon" state, unlike what an earlier field hint implied. Flipping a `comingSoon`-flagged card (like the 2028 preview) into a real purchasable one by giving it a live enrol link would require the `comingSoon` flag itself to become computed from data — a much more invasive change (it also gates the blurred price, the overlay, the two-column layout, the disabled button state), explicitly left untouched and out of scope for this pass. **`/team`'s own copy was corrected to match this real behavior**, not the other way around: the enroll_url field's label changed from "blank = Coming Soon, unclickable" (never actually true once this shipped) to "overrides the button — blank keeps the course's existing link"; the pricing card's status badge changed from "● Live"/"Coming Soon" (implying knowledge `/team` doesn't actually have about a course's true availability) to "● Enrol link set"/"No enrol link set" (a statement of the literal field, not a claim about the course).
  - **`/team`'s own remaining stale copy was updated to match reality**: the top intro sentence now reads "Changes here go live on taai.live immediately — no deploy needed" for Pricing specifically (via a new per-type `SITE_DATA_LIVE_NOTE` map — Notes/Lectures keep their own accurate "preview only" sentence, since those genuinely haven't been cut over); the sold-out note dropped "(preview only)" for "(live on taai.live)"; the dead `SITE_DATA_RESOURCES.pricing.columns` array (a leftover from before Pricing switched to cards — confirmed unused via `renderSiteDataTable`, the only place `cfg.columns` is ever read, and that function is never called for `type === 'pricing'`) was deleted outright rather than left as stale, misleading dead code with the old "Live"/"Coming Soon" wording baked into it.
  - Verified end-to-end against real production data, not just reasoned about: `curl`ed the new endpoint directly (confirmed the full real row for `full-course` — price, discount, sold-out date, enrol link, all present); a real headless-Chrome render of the live page confirmed all three real bundles showing their genuine live prices/discounts (₹7,500/50%, ₹4,999/50%, ₹3,499/53% — matching exactly what had been set through `/team` in this same session) with the 2028 preview card's hardcoded blurred placeholder (`₹13,999`) correctly unaffected (confirmed it's a separate hardcoded value, not pricing-map-driven, unrelated to this cutover); a real (then-reverted) production write to `full-course`'s `sold_out_date` (2026-10-05 → 2026-12-25) confirmed the live countdown tracker's text genuinely changed (93 days left, Dec 25) and reverted correctly; individual-course cards and `BundleComparison`'s own enrol buttons both confirmed reading the live `enroll_url`; a mobile-width (390px) render confirmed the same live price with zero console errors; and a real headless-Chrome pass over `/team` confirmed the updated copy, the corrected status badges, and that `sold_out_date` is now genuinely offered on a non-full-course combo's edit form (`Mathematics Bundle`).
- **A one-line pitch (`c.tagline`) added under the 2027 card's title the same day** — "300+ hours of content — the most concise, no-fluff GATE DA course out there," on request ("or something like that"). New optional field on `COMBOS` entries, rendered by `ComboCard` right after `titleEl` in both branches (only `full-course` sets it today, but any card can). Capped at `max-width: 34ch` so it wraps to a readable 2-3 lines instead of stretching the card's own full width. Verified at both desktop and mobile widths — fits cleanly, no overflow.
- **The countdown box on the same card was moved into the corner where the ghost numeral sits, on request off a screenshot ("can this take the place of the ghost number while bringing the title above") — then substantially reworked the same round after direct negative feedback on the first attempt ("no make it better it's terrible").** First pass: put the *entire* countdown box (icon+num row, progress bar, date line — three stacked rows) into a flex row beside the title. Looked bad for a concrete, measurable reason, not just aesthetically — the card's own content width (~316px inside padding) split between a ~168px countdown and the remaining ~134px for title meant "GATE 2027 Full Course" wrapped to 3 lines with "GATE" orphaned alone on the first, and the much-shorter countdown box left a large dead gap beneath it in the row. **Reworked into two separate pieces instead of forcing one box to share a row**: `countdownCornerBadgeEl` — a genuinely compact pill ("🔔 12d left", `position: absolute; top: 20px; right: 24px`, matching the ghost numeral's own footprint and exact position, replacing it — the numeral itself (`.combo-num`) is now hidden whenever `daysUntilSoldOut != null`) — and `countdownDetailEl` — the progress bar + "Enrollment closes {date}" line, moved to its own full-width line below the title/tagline instead of competing for corner space. Title now flows first, genuinely full-width, no longer sharing a row with anything. **A real piece of dead code this surfaced and removed**: `badgeEl` used to double as the countdown container too, via `daysUntilSoldOut != null ? countdownEl : <plain badge>` — once the countdown split into its own two dedicated pieces, that branch could never actually be reached from any render call site anymore (confirmed by reading every call site, not assumed), so `countdownEl` and the ternary were deleted rather than left as unreachable code; `badgeEl` is now unconditionally just the plain "Best value"/"Bundle" pill. Verified at both desktop and mobile: title now reads cleanly on 2 lines, the corner badge is confirmed (via direct `getBoundingClientRect()` comparison, not just a screenshot) to sit fully within the card's own bounds with no clipping, the detail line has the full card width to itself, and the other three combo cards' plain badges/ghost numerals are confirmed completely unaffected (`getsOwnRow`-style scoping — this only ever branches on `daysUntilSoldOut != null`, true only for `full-course` today).
- **A 4th, non-list "Settings" sub-tab was added to Site data (2026-09-24) — "can we have a button in the site data to hide the financial assistance card?"** — the first Site data toggle that isn't Pricing/Notes/Lectures rows, and the first one whose LIVE effect reaches `index.html` (not `gate-da-courses.html`) directly. New `site_settings` table (`supabase/schema.sql`) — a single fixed row (`id='main'`), same tiny-single-row pattern already used by `live_count_stats`/`hourly_activity_cutover`, so a second toggle later is just another column on this one row rather than a new table. `netlify/functions/site-settings.js` (admin GET/PUT, `requireAdmin`-gated, upserts rather than update-only so it self-heals even if the migration's own seed `INSERT` was skipped) and `netlify/functions/site-settings-public.js` (public, unauthenticated GET, same reasoning as `site-pricing-public.js` — only ever returns a boolean that's already meant to be visible as page behavior itself) mirror the existing site-pricing.js/site-pricing-public.js split exactly. **Deliberately NOT folded into `SITE_DATA_RESOURCES`** (`team.js`) — that config object's generic list/form machinery is built for lists of rows with create/edit/delete; a single settings row with one toggle and no create/delete doesn't fit that shape, so it gets its own small state (`state.siteSettings`), its own `loadSiteSettings()`/`saveSiteSettingsToggle()`, and its own `renderSiteSettingsPanel()` (a plain on/off switch, `.site-setting-switch`, styled to match the page's existing flat-card idiom, no glow/gradient) — while still reusing `renderSiteDataTabs()`'s sub-tab-bar styling and the shared `ensureSiteDataLoaded(type)` dispatcher (new — replaces the inline "`if rows[type] === null` → `loadSiteData`" checks at both call sites, generalizing to also handle the settings sub-tab's own differently-shaped loaded/loading state). The toggle itself updates optimistically (flips the switch the instant it's clicked, calls `render()` synchronously) and reverts on a failed save — verified directly via CDP: a synchronous click+read shows `checked:true, disabled:true` immediately, and after the PUT settles (pre-migration, so it genuinely fails) the switch correctly reverts to `checked:false` with the real error surfaced via `.msg-error`. `index.html`'s `CTA()` (the "Can't afford it right now?" card) reads the public endpoint via the same pre-fetch-before-mount pattern `_pricingFetch`/`Offer()` already use (`_siteSettingsFetch`/`_siteSettingsCache`) and returns `null` when the toggle is on — the mobile sticky "Take a Trial" bar (`MobileStickyCTA`, a separate, unrelated component) is untouched either way. **A real race caught and fixed before shipping, not copied verbatim from the pre-existing pattern**: `Offer()`'s own `React.useEffect(() => { if (!_pricingCache) _pricingFetch.then(setPricingMap); }, [])` guard turns out to be a latent bug — if the fetch resolves in the gap between the component's initial render and this effect actually running, `_pricingCache`/`_siteSettingsCache` is already non-null by the time the guard checks it, so `setSettings` never fires and the stale initial `{}` sticks forever, since `_siteSettingsFetch` is a already-memoized Promise and chaining `.then()` on it again costs no extra network call regardless of timing — the guard serves no purpose and actively breaks the race case. Confirmed via CDP with an intercepted, near-instantly-resolving fake response (exposes the race that a real network round-trip usually outruns): the CTA card stayed visibly rendered even with `hideFinancialAssistance:true` correctly sitting in `_siteSettingsCache`, until the guard was removed (`CTA()`'s effect now always calls `_siteSettingsFetch.then(setSettings)` unconditionally) — re-verified afterward showing the card correctly disappearing. `Offer()`'s own matching guard was left alone (out of scope, not what was asked, and a real network fetch to Google Sheets is unlikely to resolve fast enough in practice to hit this race) but is a known latent issue if it's ever touched again. Verified end-to-end pre-migration via headless Chrome/CDP: `/team`'s Settings panel renders the real error cleanly with no console exceptions and the switch stays interactive; `index.html` renders the CTA card exactly as before (byte-identical text, zero console errors) since the public endpoint degrades to `hideFinancialAssistance:false` on the missing table. **Requires the same standing manual SQL-editor step as every other schema change in this project** before the toggle can actually save or take effect — until then, `/team`'s panel surfaces the real "table not found" error (expected) and the live CTA card keeps showing (safe default).

## Name moderation — the needs_rename gate + Claude API (added 2026-09-24)

`students.needs_rename` blocks a student's Focus timer (enforced server-side in `pomo-active.js`, surfaced via `tracker-data.js`, rendered as a gate in `gate-da-progress-tracker.html`/`progress.js` reusing the malpractice-gate UI slot — see `pomoGateState()`) until they rename themselves via `rename.js`. Originally admin-set one email at a time via a direct SQL statement, no code path — built out into a real system across three requests the same day.

- **Dodging the gate with a trivial tweak of the same name is blocked** — `rename.js`'s `normalizeForCompare()` strips everything but letters/digits and lowercases, so "Sandip" → "Sandip." / "sandip_" / "SANDIP " all normalize identically; while `needs_rename` is true, a normalized match (or a result under 2 characters, catching pure-symbol dodges like `"..."`) is rejected with a clear message. Only applies while gated — a normal voluntary rename is never restricted. Verified against a real disposable test student: `"SHETOOYOUNG."`, `"shetooyoung_"`, `"..."` all correctly rejected; a genuine rename succeeded and cleared the flag; a trivial tweak was then correctly allowed once ungated.
- **A different-but-still-inappropriate replacement name is also blocked, via a real Claude API call** — `netlify/functions/lib/name-check.js`'s `checkNameAppropriate(name)` is the one shared classifier used by all three integration points below: plain `fetch` (no SDK, matching this codebase's existing lightweight Discord/Telegram posting helpers) against the Messages API, Haiku (cheap/fast — this is a simple binary classification, not something needing a bigger model), forced into structured output via a single `classify_name` tool call (`{flagged, reason}`) rather than free-text parsing. System prompt is explicit that fun/creative names (nicknames, anime/game characters, jokes) must NOT be flagged, only genuinely inappropriate ones (sexual, hateful/slur-based, harassing), and to favor false negatives over false positives. **Fails OPEN with no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` configured** — returns `{flagged:false, skipped:true}` rather than throwing, so every caller treats a missing key identically to "Claude looked and it's fine," never a special error case. `rename.js` calls this on the new name, only while gated, only after the dodge check passes (no point spending an API call on something already rejected for free) — a flagged result is rejected with Claude's own stated reason surfaced in the response.
- **Proactive nightly scan for names nobody's manually flagged** — `netlify/functions/name-check-scan.js` (scheduled, `netlify.toml`, `15 19 * * *` = 00:45 IST) checks any student who is both not already `needs_rename` and whose current `display_name` doesn't match `name_last_checked` (never checked, or changed since). `SCAN_LIMIT` (60/run) spreads the very first run's full-roster backlog across several nights and caps runaway cost generally; every run after the backlog clears only has genuinely new/changed names to check. A flagged result sets `needs_rename_source='ai_scan'` (vs `'admin'` for the manual path) so an admin can tell at a glance whether a flag already carries a machine judgment. Degrades to a clean `{candidates:0, skipped: <error>}` 200, not a 500, on a pre-migration "column does not exist" error — a scheduled function nobody's watching in real time shouldn't show a scary failure for an expected, temporary state.
- **A manual "second opinion" admin action was built, then removed the same day at direct instruction ("drop the manual check on our side and delegate everything to claude")** — `netlify/functions/team-flag-name.js` briefly existed (admin-gated `POST {email}` to flag with a Claude opinion attached, `POST {email, unflag:true}` to clear) alongside a Flag/Unflag button pair in `/team`'s Students view. Deleted outright, not just hidden — there is now no human step anywhere in this system: `needs_rename` is set only by `name-check-scan.js`'s nightly pass and cleared only by a student successfully renaming past `rename.js`'s own Claude check. `team.js`'s `renderNameFlagCell()` is now read-only — a `🚩 Flagged by Claude` badge (title = the stored reason) when `needs_rename` is true, nothing otherwise, no buttons, `state.flagStatus` removed entirely. A false positive has no admin override built in by design — the only paths are the student picking a name Claude accepts (the gate is self-resolving by construction) or, in a genuinely rare case, the same one-off direct-SQL correction this codebase already uses for other rare manual overrides (e.g. Puneet's malpractice count).
- **A real fault-tolerance bug caught during testing, not shipped** — `team-students.js`'s first version put the already-migrated `needs_rename` in the SAME best-effort query as the two genuinely-new columns (`needs_rename_source`, `name_check_reason`). Since PostgREST fails a select ENTIRELY if even one requested column doesn't exist, this silently masked `needs_rename`'s real, already-correct value behind the two not-yet-migrated columns' failure — confirmed directly: flagging a real disposable test student returned `ok:true, needsRename:true` and the raw DB row genuinely showed `needs_rename:true`, but `/api/team-students` read back `false` for the same student. This is the exact same mistake this file's own `malpractice_warning_ack_count` entry already documents and warns about repeating. Fixed by moving `needs_rename` into the main student select (stable, prior-session column, safe to include broadly) and leaving only the two brand-new columns in their own best-effort query. Re-verified after the fix: flag → `needs_rename:true` reads correctly; a full headless-Chrome pass over the real `/team` UI (`DEV_BYPASS`) confirmed Flag → badge + Unflag button + opinion hint appear, Unflag → badge disappears + Flag button returns, zero console errors throughout; the student-facing gate on the real tracker page was verified end-to-end too (real cookie, real Focus Mode entry, dodge rejected, genuine rename clears the gate and unlocks Start).
- **Schema**: `needs_rename` itself already existed (see its own comment in schema.sql). This round added `needs_rename_source` (`'admin' | 'ai_scan'`), `name_check_reason` (Claude's one-sentence explanation for whatever the most recent check concluded, flagged or not — shown in `/team` for context rather than a bare unexplained flag), `name_last_checked` (the exact `display_name` value a check was last run against — what `name-check-scan.js` compares to decide whether to re-bill an API call). **`name_last_checked` is deliberately NOT updated by a plain voluntary (non-gated) rename** — only when the gated Claude check in `rename.js` actually ran and passed for that exact new name — so the nightly scan still picks up and checks a normal rename it was never shown, rather than a rename request silently forging a "this was checked" record it didn't earn.
- **Requires `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`** in both `.env` (local) and Netlify's dashboard env vars (deployed) — not yet configured as of this writing. Every piece here was built and verified to degrade safely without it (dodge-check-only protection stays fully active; the AI layer is purely additive, never a single point of failure for the underlying block). Requires the same standing manual SQL-editor migration step as every other schema change in this project before `needs_rename_source`/`name_check_reason`/`name_last_checked` exist in production.
- **Registration checking a name synchronously was tried, then reverted the same day, in favor of an async scan — two rounds of direct follow-up.** First: a real, direct question ("what if someone uses a slur as his first name") caught that registration wrote a brand-new name straight into `students` with zero check, relying entirely on the once-daily nightly scan to ever catch it — up to ~24h of real public exposure for a slur. Fixed at the time by calling `checkNameAppropriate()` synchronously in `register.js` right before the INSERT, rejecting outright with a 400 on a flagged name. **Then reverted the same day, on a second direct follow-up**: a brand-new signup has zero leaderboard visibility until the student has actually logged real focus time, so there's no urgency to block the signup itself on a moderation call — "let the signup happen instantly... there's no hurry for the result." `register.js` is back to zero Claude involvement, exactly as it was before either round (confirmed by reading the file — no import, no call). **What actually closes the gap now: `name-check-scan.js`'s cron moved from once a day to every 5 minutes** (`netlify.toml`), on a further follow-up clarifying that "no hurry for the result" meant the HTTP response shouldn't wait on it, not that checking should be deferred for up to a day — a brand-new registration (`name_last_checked` starts null) is always a scan candidate on its very next run, so in practice a bad name gets caught within minutes, not hours, with zero added registration latency. `SCAN_LIMIT` dropped 60→20 to match the new cadence — the original 60 was sized to clear a full-roster backlog "over several nights"; at 5-minute ticks the same backlog clears in under 2 hours, and a smaller per-tick ceiling bounds worst-case cost more tightly now that this runs ~288×/day instead of once. **A genuinely fire-and-forget check right after responding to the registration request was considered and rejected** — this codebase's own established knowledge (see the `pomodoro_credit_failures` entry above) is that Netlify Functions don't reliably keep running background work after a response is sent, so a frequent, fully-awaited scheduled function (the same reliable mechanism `weekly-rank-snapshot.js`/`daily-streak-snapshot.js` already use) is the correct tool here, not an unreliable post-response fetch.
- **A "🚩 Flagged names only" checkbox filter was added to `/team`'s Students view** (`state.studentFilters.flaggedOnly`, `filteredStudents()`) alongside the existing search/inactive/min-streak filters, on direct follow-up question about whether one existed — it didn't. Same pattern as the other filters (included in the "Clear filters" button's visibility condition and reset). Verified against a real flagged disposable test student: 345 rows → 1 with the box checked, "Students (1 of 345)" header updates correctly, zero console errors.
- **A real Egress cost bug in `name-check-scan.js`, caught by a direct question ("won't running every 5 minutes cause too much resource drain?") right after the cron was widened, and measured before fixing, not just reasoned about.** The original version fetched every single student (`email, display_name, needs_rename, name_last_checked`) on every tick and filtered candidates in JS — completely harmless at the original once-a-day cadence, but the whole point of widening the cron was to make this fire ~288×/day instead of once. Measured directly against real production data (344 real students, the actual field set): ~41KB per full-roster fetch → **~339MB/month** at the new cadence, almost entirely wasted, since the overwhelming majority of 5-minute windows have zero actual candidates. Fixed with `get_name_check_candidates(p_limit)` (new Postgres function, schema.sql) — the exact same `needs_rename = false AND (name_last_checked IS NULL OR name_last_checked != display_name)` filter, now run server-side, returning only the (usually zero, at most `SCAN_LIMIT`) rows that genuinely need checking instead of the whole table. A typical no-op tick now returns an empty array instead of 41KB; even a pessimistic worst case (every tick maxing out at the full 20-row `SCAN_LIMIT`, which would only happen during an initial backlog, never in steady state) works out to ~10MB/month — over 30x better than the original approach at its worst, effectively negligible in realistic steady-state use. Verified pre-migration fail-open still holds (the RPC not existing yet degrades to the same clean `{candidates:0, skipped: <error>}` response as before, not a 500) — the actual reduced-payload behavior can't be measured end-to-end until the migration runs, same standing gap as everything else in this feature.
- **A hard 6s timeout on the Claude call itself, added on a direct latency question** — `checkNameAppropriate()` now wraps its `fetch` in an `AbortController`, so a slow or hung Claude response can never leave a student stuck on registration or the rename gate indefinitely (and stays well inside Netlify's own function execution ceiling). A timeout is treated exactly like any other failure by every caller — caught, fails open. Verified for real: a mocked `fetch` that never resolves on its own confirmed the call genuinely aborts at ~6000ms (measured 6008ms) and throws an `AbortError`, not just reasoned about from reading the `AbortController` wiring.
- **Gated Claude calls are capped at `GATE_CHECK_LIMIT` (3), added on a direct question about exhausting API calls via repeated renaming — then redesigned twice more the same day on further direct follow-ups.** Real vector: at the time this cap was designed, a plain voluntary rename never called Claude at all, so a `needs_rename`-gated student resubmitting new names was the only way renaming burned real API calls (this later changed — see the voluntary-rename entry below — but that entry adds one check per rename, never a counted/escalating attempt, so it doesn't reopen this same exhaustion vector). **Round 1**: a flat cap that, once exhausted, silently fell through to the free normalized-dodge check alone. **The exploit, caught by "what if they do more than three inappropriate names"**: the dodge check only ever catches a trivial punctuation tweak of the *same* name — it can't tell a genuinely different bad name from a fine one, so someone could burn all 3 real attempts on purpose, then slip a 4th, equally-bad name through completely unverified. **Round 2**: a 24h rolling-window cooldown instead — closed the exploit, no dodge-check-only fallback once spent. **Round 3, direct request: "after third defer them to an admin ... without a time cap"** — dropped the time-based auto-unlock entirely. What ships: once the 3rd real attempt is *also* rejected, `gate_escalated` (schema.sql) is set and every further attempt is refused outright with a plain message telling the student to reach out to the team directly — **no automated Discord alert** (tried, then explicitly ruled out the same round: "no discord webhook, they will manually contact admin") — until a team member clears it — originally a one-off SQL statement only (`UPDATE students SET gate_escalated = false, gate_name_check_count = 0 WHERE email = '...'`, same pattern as Puneet's earlier malpractice bump), until Round 4 below added a real `/team` button for this specific action. Still never a truly *permanent* trap — a human can always clear it — just no longer a self-resolving one past 3 fails. **Wording**: every user-facing message here was deliberately kept free of internal terms like "attempts" or "checks" per direct instruction — the gate-form hint reads "if it doesn't work out after a few tries, you'll need to reach out to our team on Discord for help," and the escalation message itself is just "Please reach out to our team directly on Discord — they'll help you sort out your name." **Verification, same honest gap as every round of this**: the count/escalate decision logic was verified in isolation (5 cases — normal increments, the exact 3rd-fail-escalates transition, and confirming a lucky *passing* 3rd attempt never escalates) and pre-migration fail-open was confirmed directly — but the actual end-to-end escalation, persisted across real gated attempts against production, still can't be exercised until the schema migration runs.
- **A plain, non-gated rename now gets a real synchronous Claude check too, not just deferred to the scan — direct follow-up ("i feel the [immediate] option is better") after asking how often Claude actually runs.** Until this point, only a `needs_rename`-gated student's resolving attempt was checked immediately; an ordinary voluntary rename by a non-flagged student saved instantly with zero check, relying entirely on `name-check-scan.js`'s next run (every 5 minutes) to catch a bad choice. **The case for checking this immediately is actually stronger than at registration**, where an immediate check was deliberately left out: a fresh signup has zero leaderboard visibility until real focus time is logged, but a student renaming here typically already IS visible (existing history, likely already on a leaderboard) — their new name becomes publicly visible the instant this request succeeds, not after however long it takes to log hours. `rename.js`'s `existing.needs_rename` branch became a real if/else: the new `!existing.needs_rename` branch calls `checkNameAppropriate()` and rejects outright (before saving) if flagged — deliberately simpler than the gated path, no dodge-check (nothing to dodge, they're not currently flagged) and no counting/escalation (failing once here just means "pick a different name," it's not "already in trouble," so it doesn't touch `gate_name_check_count` at all). Same fail-open discipline as every other call site — a Claude hiccup just lets the rename through normally, with the 5-minute scan as the backstop exactly as before. The final write's `name_last_checked` update now correctly applies on both paths (previously commented as gated-only, since only the gated path could ever set it) — a voluntarily-renamed, Claude-verified name is recorded too, so the scan doesn't waste a redundant re-check on something just vetted seconds ago. Verified directly: a mocked-flagged rename attempt against a real disposable test student was rejected with the reason and confirmed the student's `display_name` in the database stayed completely unchanged (not silently half-applied); the normal no-API-key fail-open path was confirmed to still succeed exactly as before.
- **Round 4, the same day: a real `/team` clear/rename UI, a monthly reset added to the gated cap, and — the actually new piece — a separate 3-per-calendar-month cap on ordinary voluntary renames too.** Three direct follow-ups landed together. **(1) `/team` UI**: `netlify/functions/team-clear-name-check.js` (admin-gated, `POST {email, new_display_name?}`) replaces the "one-off SQL only" instruction from Round 3 — `/team`'s Students view now shows a real "⛔ Blocked — needs manual clear" badge plus a "New name (optional)" input, a **Rename** button (sets the name directly and fully resolves the gate), and a **Clear block** button (just lifts the block, leaving the student still gated but with a fresh 3 tries — same as before, self-service resumes). A new "Blocked (needs clear)" summary card and `gate_escalated_count` mirror the existing "Flagged names" pattern. `team-students.js`'s `gate_escalated` field follows the same best-effort-query discipline as `needs_rename_source`/`name_check_reason` (all three genuinely new together, safe to combine — see that entry's own comment on why combining a new column with an already-stable one would be the real mistake). **(2) Gated cap now also resets on the 1st of the month** — direct instruction: "that three is different and strictly measured and resets 1st of every month." This is an addition to Round 3's escalation, not a replacement for it: `gate_name_check_month` (re-added; schema.sql's own comment walks through why it was removed in Round 3 and is now back with different semantics) means a student who hits the cap either gets manually cleared by the team (still the expected, faster path) *or* automatically gets a fresh start the moment the calendar rolls over, whichever comes first — nobody is stuck for longer than "the rest of this month" even if nobody on the team ever looks at it. **(3) The genuinely new piece — voluntary renames now ALSO cost real money if unrestricted, and now they're capped too**: direct instruction, "everyone should have a three rename per month limit otherwise we will unnecessarily use our claude resources." This only became a real concern once Round 3 above made a plain rename call Claude synchronously — before that, a voluntary rename was free. New, separate columns `voluntary_rename_count`/`voluntary_rename_month` (deliberately NOT shared with the gated counters — a gated student resolving a flag and an ordinary student renaming for fun have different limits and different consequences once exhausted) cap successful voluntary renames at 3/month, checked *before* the Claude call (an already-capped student costs zero API calls, not just zero renames). **Frontend, matching the literal ask** ("the rename button on the top can trigger this message and becomes unaccessable... only shows the message whenever clicked after 3 successful renames"): `progress.js`'s top-of-page "Rename" click now checks `state.student.voluntary_rename_count`/`voluntary_rename_month` (carried in the cookie from the last successful register/rename response, a client-side convenience check only — the server in `rename.js` is still the real, authoritative boundary) — under the cap it opens the form exactly as before; at the cap it shows `state.renameLimitMessage` ("You've used all your renames for this month — you can rename again from the 1st.") with an OK button instead of ever opening the form. A successful voluntary rename's response now also carries `voluntary_rename_count`/`voluntary_rename_month` forward into `state.student` and the cookie so this stays accurate across page loads on the same device (cross-device staleness is the same accepted, non-authoritative tradeoff as everywhere else this pattern is used). **Verification**: the monthly-reset and voluntary-cap decision arithmetic were verified in isolation (7 cases combined, including a stale-month record correctly resetting to fresh); the full `/team` clear/rename flow was verified end-to-end via a real headless-Chrome run against a mocked `gate_escalated` student (badge appears, Clear block correctly removes it, zero console errors); the frontend rename-button gating was verified end-to-end too — registered a real disposable test student through the actual guest→Focus→Start flow, confirmed Rename opens the form normally under the cap, patched the cookie to simulate being at the cap, reloaded, confirmed clicking Rename shows the block message with no form opened, and confirmed OK returns to normal. Pre-migration fail-open confirmed directly for both new counters. The actual end-to-end persistence of either cap against production still can't be exercised until the schema migration runs — same honest gap as every round of this feature.
- **A direct request to verify the rename-gate popup's own visual framing** ("that pop up box is well framed... without leaking text... place of the popup and where they put their new name") was checked for real, not assumed clean just because the CSS looked reasonable on read. `.pomo-malpractice-gate` has a pre-existing, deliberate `min-height: 420px` (it sits IN PLACE OF the Pomodoro ring/clock it temporarily replaces, not below it — matching that area's own footprint so nothing jumps when switching between the two) with `justify-content: center`, predating any of this session's new, longer messages. Checked with a real automated overflow scan (every descendant element's `getBoundingClientRect()` compared against the gate box's own, not just eyeballing a screenshot) against two real states: the normal gate (title + body + input/Save + the new hint text) and the escalation-error state (same, plus the red error line) — zero elements overflow the box in either case, confirmed via real screenshots too. The only thing worth noting, not a bug: the normal (no-error) state has real unused space at the bottom of the fixed-height box, since this particular message combination is shorter than what the box's height was originally sized for — the escalation-error state fills that space naturally once the extra line appears. Deliberately left alone rather than "fixed" by shrinking the box, since the fixed height's whole job is staying visually stable across every state this same box can be in, not fitting any one state's content exactly.

## Git remotes

Push reaches **both** `github.com/taai2025/TAAI` (primary) and `github.com/diptadee2/TAAI` (the user's personal mirror) via origin's dual-pushurl config. Plain `git push` is sufficient — don't add `--force` or push to only one remote unless asked.

## Known gotchas worth not re-discovering

- **A blog post's Markdown body can embed raw HTML (`marked` passes it through), but every line must start at or near column 0** — CommonMark treats a line indented 4+ spaces, once it starts a new block (e.g. right after a blank line), as an *indented code block*, not raw HTML, even mid-way through an otherwise-fine HTML section. Hit this directly writing a heavily-nested custom-design post (the original hand-coded version of `gate-da-syllabus-2027.md`, before it was rebuilt on Blocks): several `<div>` blocks silently turned into escaped `<pre><code>` text instead of rendering, purely because they carried the source article's original 4-8 space indentation. Fix: strip all leading whitespace from HTML content going into a post's Markdown body — indentation is a purely cosmetic authoring habit here, not something the renderer tolerates the way a browser parsing plain HTML would. Still worth knowing if a post's Body field (not Blocks) ever needs real embedded HTML again.
- **A post can carry its own bespoke design (fonts, colors, custom `<style>`) safely by scoping every custom property and selector to a wrapper class** (e.g. `.gda-article`) **instead of `:root`/bare `body`** — blog.css already defines `--ink`/`--line`/etc. globally for the shared nav/footer chrome; a post's own `:root{--ink:...}` would silently override those site-wide, breaking the nav/footer's colors, not just the post's own content. Verified this doesn't leak by checking the real computed color of a nav element after loading such a post.
- **Chromium DOM-node leak**: animating `transform` or `box-shadow` directly on an element that *also* has both `box-shadow` and `border-radius` causes an unbounded detached-node leak (confirmed via CDP `Performance.getMetrics`). Animate a wrapper or pseudo-element instead, leave the shadow+radius element itself unanimated.
- **WebGL/Canvas effects are expensive per-tab** (measured via renderer+GPU process RSS, not JS heap — heap snapshots show nothing here): a `gsap`-driven Canvas2D effect cost ~150-200MB/tab from its continuous ticker; an `ogl`-based WebGL shader background cost ~300MB from context creation itself, independent of resolution. A zero-dependency Canvas2D effect with no continuous external library ticker (`DotField` in `courses.html`) showed no measurable cost. Prefer that pattern if a future visual effect request comes in.
- **Dev-server-only memory growth** from repeated live Babel recompilation during active navigation testing is expected and not a leak (confirmed via heap-snapshot diffing) — it does not affect production, since Netlify serves precompiled `dist/`.
- **Favicon**: the 16×16 frame inside any `.ico` built via ImageMagick gets forced to 1-bit alpha regardless of source quality — that's why standalone `favicon-16.png`/`favicon-32.png` exist as preferred `<link rel="icon" type="image/png">` tags ahead of the `.ico` fallback.
- **Server-side "today" must be computed in IST, never naive `new Date()`**: Netlify Functions run on infrastructure with no guaranteed local timezone (effectively UTC). For roughly the first 5.5 hours of every IST calendar day, a naive `new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10)` silently returns *yesterday's* date by IST reckoning — bit us exactly this way in `streak.js` (a same-day tick wasn't counted). Use `todayIST()` in `netlify/functions/lib/supabase.js` (`Intl.DateTimeFormat` pinned to `Asia/Kolkata`) for any server-side calendar-date logic.
- **`@supabase/supabase-js` needs an explicit WebSocket transport**: `createClient()` eagerly constructs a Realtime client requiring a `WebSocket` constructor that isn't guaranteed across every Node/Lambda runtime a function might execute on — without it, every single request crashes. `lib/supabase.js` passes `realtime: { transport: ws }` (the `ws` npm package) to work around this; don't remove it even though nothing here actually uses realtime subscriptions.
- **`tracker-data.js` is deliberately one large function instead of several small ones** — it used to be 7 separate Netlify Functions (`schedule`, `last-week-focus-leaders`, `progress`, `streak`, `subject-progress`, `pomo-settings` GET, `pomo-sessions`), all only ever called together as one batch from `loadMonth()`. Each Netlify Function is an independent Lambda with its own cold-start lifecycle, so a page load after any idle gap paid up to 7 separate container-boot + Supabase-client-construction costs in parallel — confirmed via the Netlify Functions dashboard as the cause of a uniform 1.2-1.8s p50 latency on otherwise-simple single-table queries, plus inflated Web-requests credit usage. Don't re-split this back into one-function-per-query without re-checking whether that dashboard number regresses — `streak.js`, `subject-progress.js`, and `pomo-settings.js` are correctly still separate, since those are also called independently outside the page-load batch (after a task toggle, or a settings save).
- **Loading a new schedule that blanks out an already-past, already-completed date silently deletes real students' earned streak days — caught 2026-09-16, before it shipped broadly, by explicit request ("don't let them break the streak") after a schedule replacement (a new PDF covering Aug 29 – Oct 10) initially did exactly this.** `computeStreak()` (`lib/supabase.js`) counts consecutive *scheduled-and-completed* dates walking backward from today — a date only counts toward the streak at all if it appears in `scheduledDatesDesc` (i.e. `schedule_tasks` has *any* row for it, any subject). The new PDF showed nothing for Aug 29-30 (blank across every column); `load-schedule.mjs` does a full delete-and-replace for every date its CSV covers (see below), so loading it deleted the *previously-existing* "Revision" tasks on those two dates that real students had already completed.
